[WEMP-M01-001 Section 1] 1. Objective

Module 01 establishes the complete identity and authentication foundation for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

The purpose of this module is to provide secure, consistent, and auditable identity handling across:

Customer Web Portal
Seller Web Portal
Admin Web Portal
Super Admin Web Portal
Customer Mobile Application
Seller Mobile Application
Backend APIs

Module 01 shall define how users:

Create accounts
Verify ownership of email addresses and mobile numbers
Authenticate
Maintain sessions
Recover account access
Revoke sessions
Protect privileged accounts
Access protected application areas

No marketplace business functionality shall be implemented in this module.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 2] 2. Scope
Included

Module 01 includes:

Identity model
Customer-initiated universal Identity registration
Seller-initiated universal Identity registration
Privileged Admin access Identity provisioning workflow
Super Admin access bootstrap workflow
Email and mobile verification
Password-based login
JWT access tokens
Rotating refresh tokens
Session management
Device session management
Logout and logout-all-devices
Forgot-password flow
Password reset
Account lockout
Authentication rate limiting
MFA foundation for privileged users
Authentication audit logs
Authentication guards
Protected-route foundation
Web authentication screens
Mobile authentication screens
Authentication database entities
Authentication APIs
Authentication tests
Excluded

Module 01 shall not include:

Seller business verification or approval
Seller company onboarding
Detailed role-permission management
Product catalog
Inventory
Cart
Wishlist
Orders
Payments
Shipping
Returns
Reviews
Coupons
Marketplace dashboards
Social login
Third-party identity providers
Customer loyalty or membership
Production SMS commercial configuration
Production email commercial configuration
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 3] 3. Identity Architecture

The identity architecture shall use one centralized authentication domain within the NestJS backend.

The authentication domain shall serve all approved clients:

Next.js Web Application
        │
Flutter Mobile Application
        │
        ▼
NestJS Authentication APIs
        │
        ├── PostgreSQL
        └── Redis
Responsibilities

PostgreSQL shall store durable identity records, including:

Users
Password credentials
Verification state
Sessions
Refresh-token families
Login history
MFA configuration
Authentication audit records

Redis shall support temporary or high-frequency security operations, including:

OTP state
Rate-limit counters
Temporary lockouts
Short-lived verification state
Revocation support where required

PostgreSQL remains the authoritative identity data store.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 4] 4. Authentication Principles

The authentication system shall follow these principles:

Secure by default

Every protected resource shall deny access unless authentication requirements are satisfied.

Least privilege

Authentication proves identity only. Authorization permissions shall be handled separately through approved role and permission rules.

Server-side enforcement

All authentication, verification, session, and account-state checks shall be enforced by the backend.

Client-side validation shall never be treated as a security control.

Minimal disclosure

Authentication APIs shall return only the information required by the requesting client.

Passwords, password hashes, OTP values, token hashes, secrets, and internal security metadata shall never be returned.

Explicit lifecycle

Every identity shall move through clearly defined states.

Auditable activity

Security-sensitive authentication actions shall create structured audit records.

Revocability

Sessions and refresh tokens must be revocable without deleting the user account.

Fail securely

Unexpected authentication failures shall deny access and return standardized safe error responses.

These principles align with current digital-identity lifecycle guidance and modern authentication security practice.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 5] 5. Identity, Profile, Security Classification and Role Ownership Model

Objective

This section separates identity ownership, business-profile ownership, authentication-security classification, authentication assurance, and authorization roles.

Universal Identity

Module 01 shall maintain one universal Identity record for each individual.

An Identity represents the individual for authentication purposes only.

The Module 01 Identity shall not contain:

Customer profile data
Seller profile data
Seller business status
Business onboarding state
Roles
Permissions
Role assignments
Authorization policies
Authorization decisions

A single Identity may be associated with multiple business profiles and multiple Module 02 roles without creating duplicate identities.

Approved Ownership Model

Concept	Authoritative Owner
Identity and authentication lifecycle	Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Customer profile and customer business data	Module 06 – Customer Management
Seller profile, seller organization and seller onboarding	Module 03 – Seller Management
Admin authentication-security classification	Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Super Admin authentication-security classification	Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Roles, permissions and authorization assignments	Module 02 – Role & Permission Management
Session authentication assurance	Module 01 – Identity & Authentication

Business profiles, authentication-security classifications, roles and authentication assurance shall remain independent concepts.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 6] 6. Customer Profile Boundary

A Customer profile represents marketplace customer information and customer business capabilities associated with an Identity.

Module 06 – Customer Management shall own the Customer profile.

Module 01 may create and authenticate the underlying Identity but shall not own customer-profile data or determine customer permissions.

Customer self-registration shall:

Create or locate the Module 01 Identity
Complete the required identity verification
Request creation or association of a Customer profile through an approved Module 06 contract
Request any required customer role assignment through an approved Module 02 contract

Creating a Customer profile or assigning a Customer role shall not create a second Identity.

Customer permissions shall be determined exclusively by Module 02.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 7] 7. Seller Profile Boundary

A Seller profile represents seller-specific business information associated with an Identity.

Module 03 – Seller Management shall own:

Seller profiles
Seller organizations
Seller business onboarding
Seller approval
GST verification
PAN verification
Bank verification
Warehouse setup
Commission agreements
Seller business status

Module 01 may create and authenticate the underlying Identity but shall not own seller-profile or seller-onboarding data.

Seller self-registration shall:

Create or locate the Module 01 Identity
Complete the required identity verification
Request creation or association of a Seller profile through an approved Module 03 contract
Request any required seller role assignment through an approved Module 02 contract

Creating a Seller profile or assigning a Seller role shall not create a second Identity.

Seller permissions shall be determined exclusively by Module 02.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 8] 8. Admin Authentication-Security Classification

Admin is a Module 02 authorization role and is not part of the Module 01 Identity model.

Module 02 shall own the Admin role and all permissions associated with that role.

Module 01 shall own an authentication-security classification that identifies an Identity as subject to privileged-administrator authentication controls.

Approved classification:

PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION

This classification shall control authentication security only, including:

Mandatory MFA
Approved authentication methods
Web-only authentication in Phase 1
Privileged-session timeout requirements
Enhanced authentication audit requirements
Privileged-account recovery requirements

The classification shall not grant Admin permissions and shall not constitute an Admin role assignment.

An Identity shall receive Admin access only when:

Module 01 has successfully applied the required privileged authentication-security classification;
Module 01 authentication requirements have been satisfied; and
Module 02 has assigned the Admin role through its approved authorization workflow.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 9] 9. Super Admin Authentication-Security Classification

Super Admin is a Module 02 authorization role and is not part of the Module 01 Identity model.

Module 02 shall own the Super Admin role and all permissions associated with that role.

Module 01 shall own an authentication-security classification that identifies an Identity as subject to the highest approved privileged-authentication controls.

Approved classification:

SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION

This classification shall control authentication security only, including:

Mandatory MFA
Approved high-assurance authentication methods
Web-only authentication in Phase 1
Restricted provisioning
Privileged-session timeout requirements
Enhanced authentication audit requirements
Privileged-account recovery requirements
Bootstrap authentication controls

The classification shall not grant Super Admin permissions and shall not constitute a Super Admin role assignment.

An Identity shall receive Super Admin access only when:

Module 01 has successfully applied the required Super Admin authentication-security classification;
Module 01 authentication requirements have been satisfied; and
Module 02 has assigned the Super Admin role through its approved authorization workflow.

The initial controlled bootstrap process shall provision Module 01 authentication state and Module 02 authorization state through their respective approved ownership boundaries.

Direct cross-module database modification is prohibited.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 10] 10. Identity Uniqueness

The platform shall enforce uniqueness for normalized identity identifiers.

Email

Email addresses shall be:

Trimmed
Normalized to lowercase
Validated
Uniquely indexed when present
Mobile number

Mobile numbers shall be:

Stored in normalized international format
Validated by country code and number structure
Uniquely indexed when present

An account shall not be created when the submitted verified identifier already belongs to another active account.

Account-linking and identity-merging are not included in Module 01 Version 1.0.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 11] 11. Identity States

Every user identity shall have one approved lifecycle state.

State	Meaning
PENDING_VERIFICATION	Account created but required verification is incomplete
ACTIVE	Identity verified and permitted to authenticate
LOCKED	Temporarily blocked because of security controls
SUSPENDED	Administratively restricted
DISABLED	Access intentionally disabled
DELETED	Account removed or anonymized according to approved policy

State transitions shall be validated by the backend.

Approved basic transitions
PENDING_VERIFICATION
        ↓
ACTIVE
        ↓
LOCKED
        ↓
ACTIVE

Administrative transitions may include:

ACTIVE → SUSPENDED
ACTIVE → DISABLED
SUSPENDED → ACTIVE
DISABLED → ACTIVE

Deletion behavior shall be finalized with privacy and retention requirements before production use.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 12] 12. Verification States

Email and mobile verification shall be tracked separately.

Example:

emailVerifiedAt
mobileVerifiedAt

Verification shall not be represented only by client-provided Boolean values.

The backend shall determine verification status using trusted server-side records.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 13] 13. Authentication Methods

Approved Version 1.0 methods:

Method	Status
Email + Password	Approved
Mobile + Password	Approved
Email OTP Verification	Approved
SMS OTP Verification	Approved
Admin MFA	Approved
Super Admin MFA	Approved
Social Login	Deferred
Passkeys	Deferred
Magic Links	Deferred
Passwordless Customer Login	Deferred
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 14] 14. Session Strategy

The platform shall support multiple independent sessions per user.

A user may log in from:

Multiple browsers
Multiple mobile devices
Web and mobile simultaneously

Each session shall have its own:

Session identifier
Refresh-token family
Device metadata
Creation timestamp
Last activity timestamp
Expiry timestamp
Revocation state

Users shall be able to:

Log out from the current session
View active sessions
Revoke a selected session
Log out from all devices
Authoritative Session Rule

Every protected request shall require both:

Successful JWT signature and claim validation; and
Successful validation of the referenced authoritative Module 01 session state.

A valid JWT shall not override an expired, revoked, missing or otherwise invalid session.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 15] 15. Token Strategy

The platform shall use:

Short-lived JWT Access Tokens
Authoritative server-managed sessions
Rotating opaque Refresh Tokens
Server-side protected Refresh Token state
Refresh Token Family revocation
Session-version validation
State-aware validation of every protected request

JWT signature validation shall remain stateless.

Authentication acceptance shall remain state-aware and shall require a currently valid Module 01 session.

Refresh Token rotation shall prevent continued use of a replaced token.

The detailed validation, rotation, reuse-detection and revocation semantics shall be defined in Part 3.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 16] 16. Identity and Cross-Module Lifecycle

Module 01 Identity Lifecycle

Every individual shall follow one Module 01 identity lifecycle:

Registration or Controlled Provisioning
        ↓
Required Contact Verification
        ↓
Identity Activation
        ↓
Authentication
        ↓
Session Creation
        ↓
Session Refresh
        ↓
Logout, Expiry or Revocation

Customer Profile Lifecycle

Customer profile creation and lifecycle shall be owned by Module 06.

Customer self-registration shall associate the universal Identity with a Module 06 Customer profile through an approved cross-module contract.

Seller Profile Lifecycle

Seller profile creation, business onboarding and seller approval shall be owned by Module 03.

Seller self-registration shall associate the universal Identity with a Module 03 Seller profile through an approved cross-module contract.

Seller business approval shall not modify the Module 01 Identity lifecycle unless an approved security event requires an authentication-state change.

Admin Access Lifecycle

Admin invitation or provisioning shall coordinate:

Module 01 Identity provisioning
Module 01 privileged authentication-security classification
Module 01 verification and MFA enrollment
Module 02 Admin role assignment

Super Admin Access Lifecycle

Super Admin bootstrap or provisioning shall coordinate:

Module 01 Identity provisioning
Module 01 Super Admin authentication-security classification
Module 01 verification and MFA enrollment
Module 02 Super Admin role assignment

Module 01 activation shall establish the ability to authenticate only.

Identity activation shall never grant customer, seller, Admin or Super Admin permissions.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 17] 17. Identity and Security Audit Governance

Objective

Module 01 shall create independently verifiable security audit evidence for authentication-sensitive and privileged operations without transferring authorization-audit ownership from Module 02.

Module 01 Audit Ownership

Module 01 owns audit evidence for Identity registration and authentication-state transitions; identifier verification; authentication success/failure; Credential operations; password reset and Recovery execution; MFA operations; Session and Refresh Token lifecycle; Trusted Device operations; authentication-security-classification changes; privileged provisioning/bootstrap authentication; Recovery policy/evidence/approval receipt/execution/invalidation; cryptographic-key lifecycle; Module 01 API security outcomes; C8 publication/quarantine/redrive/reconciliation; and audit access, verification, export, retention, legal hold and integrity operations.

Module 02 Audit Ownership

Module 02 exclusively owns role, permission, assignment, authorization-policy, authorization-evaluation, authorization-decision and delegated-authorization audit evidence. Module 01 may record only a protected Module 02 decision reference required to explain a Module 01 action. It shall not copy roles, permissions, policies or full authorization evidence.

Mandatory Audit Principle

Every security-sensitive Module 01 state change shall create its authoritative Audit Record in the same PostgreSQL transaction where state and evidence share a boundary. A mandatory security mutation shall fail closed when its Audit Record cannot commit. Failure of independent archival after PostgreSQL Audit Record and required Outbox intent commit shall not reverse committed state; it shall remain pending, observable and reconcilable.

Audit Evidence Requirements

Audit Records shall be append-only; database-enforced insert-only for application identities; versioned; timestamped; correlated; causally traceable; actor- and subject-attributed where known; outcome/reason coded; cryptographically tamper-evident; independently verifiable; protected from ordinary deletion; retention/legal-hold governed; and integrity-checked after restoration.

Prohibited Audit Content

Passwords/hashes, raw Access or Refresh Tokens, token digests, OTP values/evidence, MFA secrets, Recovery Codes/evidence secrets, cookies, private keys, data-encryption keys, full authorization policies and unnecessary personal/profile data are prohibited. Security-sensitive identifiers use approved protected references.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 18] 18. Privacy, Purpose Limitation and Data Minimization

Objective

Module 01 shall process only the minimum Identity and authentication data required for an approved purpose and approved legal-basis reference.

Applicable Privacy Regime

Applicable legal and regulatory privacy regimes remain APPLICABLE_PRIVACY_REGIME_TBD pending formal Legal approval. Implementation shall not infer statutory rights, retention periods, age thresholds, consent requirements, residency obligations or transfer mechanisms.

Processing-Purpose Catalogue

Canonical purpose classes: IDENTITY_REGISTRATION, IDENTIFIER_VERIFICATION, AUTHENTICATION, CREDENTIAL_SECURITY, SESSION_SECURITY, MFA_SECURITY, DEVICE_SECURITY, ACCOUNT_RECOVERY, FRAUD_AND_ABUSE_PREVENTION, SECURITY_MONITORING, PRIVILEGED_ACCOUNT_PROTECTION, LEGAL_OR_REGULATORY_RETENTION, SECURITY_AUDIT and PRIVACY_REQUEST_FULFILMENT.

Each purpose defines identifier, description, owner, data/Identity categories, operations, legal-basis reference, notice version, retention class, recipients, environments, residency/transfer constraints, classification, consent requirement, withdrawal applicability, approval/effective/retirement state. Business, Legal, Privacy and Security approval is mandatory.

Legal-Basis Reference

Canonical reference categories: CONTRACTUAL_NECESSITY_REFERENCE, LEGAL_OBLIGATION_REFERENCE, LEGITIMATE_OR_APPROVED_INTEREST_REFERENCE, SECURITY_NECESSITY_REFERENCE, CONSENT_REFERENCE and OTHER_LEGAL_BASIS_TBD. Legal owns the exact applicable authority.

Data Minimization

Module 01 shall not persist Customer/Seller profiles, roles, permissions, commercial, product, order, payment or shipping data. Public APIs never expose password hashes, MFA/OTP/Recovery secrets, token values/digests, lockout counters, audit-integrity metadata, private identifiers without authorization, internal reasoning, legal-hold/case details or Module 02 internals. Collection, use, disclosure and retention are purpose-limited.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 19] 19. Module Dependencies

Module 01 depends on Module 00 components:

NestJS foundation
PostgreSQL
Prisma
Redis
Logging
Error handling
Correlation IDs
Environment validation
Swagger
CI/CD
Testing framework
Monitoring
Security baseline

Module 01 shall not duplicate or replace Module 00 foundations.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 20] 20. Part 1 Acceptance Criteria

Part 1 is complete when:

Identity architecture is approved.
Universal Identity and cross-module ownership boundaries are approved.
Registration channels are approved.
Admin and Super Admin provisioning rules are approved.
Identity states are approved.
Verification model is approved.
Session strategy is approved.
Token strategy is approved in principle.
Identity lifecycle is documented.
Privacy and audit principles are documented.
Out-of-scope items are clearly identified.
No business module has been introduced.
Part 1 Decision Summary

Before proceeding to Part 2, the approved baseline is:

Decision	Approved Baseline
Customer registration	Email or mobile
Seller registration	Email or mobile, followed by future onboarding
Admin access provisioning	Coordinated Module 01 provisioning and Module 02 role assignment
Super Admin access provisioning	Controlled Module 01 bootstrap and Module 02 role assignment
Login	Email/mobile + password
Verification	Email OTP and SMS OTP
Universal Identity	One Module 01 Identity per individual
Multi-device sessions	Allowed
Admin mobile access	Not allowed
MFA	Required for PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION and SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION
Social login	Deferred





[WEMP-M01-001 Section 21] 21. Identity Provider
Objective

This section defines the approved identity providers for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

The platform shall maintain a centralized identity service to ensure consistent authentication, verification, session management, and audit logging across all applications.

Approved Identity Provider

The approved identity provider for Phase 1 is:

Internal Authentication Service

Implementation Platform:

NestJS Authentication Service

Responsibilities:

User registration
User authentication
Password management
Session management
JWT issuance
Refresh token management
OTP verification
MFA management
Authentication audit logging

The Internal Authentication Service shall act as the single source of truth for all platform identities.

External Identity Providers

External identity providers are not supported in Phase 1.

The following providers shall not be implemented in this module:

Google Identity
Apple Sign-In
Microsoft Entra ID
Facebook Login
GitHub Login
LinkedIn Login
Future Expansion

Future releases may introduce federation with external identity providers following architecture approval.

Any external identity provider shall integrate through the centralized authentication service rather than directly with business modules.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 22] 22. Identity, Verification and Business-Profile Status Separation
Objective

Identity state, communication-channel verification, authentication-security classification, authentication assurance, business-profile state and authorization assignment shall remain independent concepts.

Identity State

Module 01 owns Identity state.

Approved Identity states:

Identity State	Description
PENDING_VERIFICATION	Identity registration or provisioning is incomplete
ACTIVE	Identity may attempt authentication subject to applicable security policy
LOCKED	Identity is temporarily prevented from authenticating by a Module 01 security control
SUSPENDED	Identity authentication is administratively restricted
DISABLED	Authentication is disabled
DELETED	Identity is deleted or anonymized according to approved retention policy

Verification State

Module 01 separately owns email verification, mobile verification, MFA enrollment and other authentication-factor verification states.

Authentication-Security Classification

Module 01 owns authentication-security classifications used only to select authentication controls.

Approved classifications are:

STANDARD_AUTHENTICATION
PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION
SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION

These classifications shall not grant permissions.

Business-Profile State

Module 06 owns Customer profile state.

Module 03 owns Seller profile, onboarding and approval state, including seller business verification.

Examples of Module 03-owned state include:

Seller approval
GST verification
PAN verification
Bank verification
KYC or business verification

Authorization State

Module 02 owns roles, permissions and authorization assignments.

A change in business-profile state or authorization state shall not directly modify Module 01 data.

Any required authentication-state change shall occur only through an approved cross-module contract processed by Module 01.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 23] 23. Authentication Assurance Model
Objective

Authentication Assurance represents the strength of the authentication completed for the current session.

Authentication Assurance shall be owned and calculated by Module 01.

Authentication Assurance shall not represent:

Customer or Seller profile status
Admin or Super Admin role assignment
Business approval
Identity type
Authorization permission
MFA enrollment without successful MFA verification

Approved Assurance Levels

Assurance Level	Meaning
AAL0	No authenticated session exists
AAL1	The current session completed one approved primary authentication factor
AAL2	The current session completed an approved primary factor and an approved independent MFA factor

AAL0 shall not be issued as an authenticated session assurance value.

Email or mobile ownership verification shall remain verification state and shall not independently raise the assurance level of a session.

MFA enrollment alone shall not produce AAL2.

AAL2 shall be established only when an approved MFA factor is successfully verified for the current authentication event or approved step-up authentication event.

Admin and Super Admin roles shall not produce an assurance level.

Module 01 authentication-security classifications may require a minimum assurance level but shall not grant authorization.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 24] 24. Authentication Boundaries

Authentication and authorization shall remain independent architectural concerns.

Authentication

Authentication establishes:

Who the user is.

Authentication confirms identity using approved credentials.

Authentication does not determine system permissions.

Authorization

Authorization determines:

What an authenticated user is permitted to access.

Authorization shall be implemented through the approved Role-Based Access Control (RBAC) model defined in Module 02.

Authentication shall never directly grant application permissions.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 25] 25. Identity Ownership Rules

Every identity shall represent one individual.

The following rules are mandatory:

One identity belongs to one individual.
Account sharing is prohibited.
Identity transfer between individuals is prohibited.
Credential sharing is prohibited.
Identity recovery shall follow the approved recovery workflow.
Administrative identity modifications shall be fully audited.

Future business modules may define organization-level account delegation without changing identity ownership.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 26] 26. Reserved Identity Categories

The platform reserves the following identity categories for future use.

These identities shall not be implemented during Phase 1.

Reserved identity categories:

Guest
Service Account
API Client
System Account
Vendor Integration Account
Machine Identity

Future implementation requires architecture approval.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 27] 27. Identity Naming Convention

Every identity shall use globally unique identifiers.

Approved identifier:

UUID Version 7

Requirements:

Sequential database identifiers shall never be exposed publicly.
Internal database primary keys shall remain private.
Public APIs shall expose only approved public identifiers.
Identity identifiers shall remain immutable throughout the account lifecycle.

All future modules shall reference identities using the approved UUID format.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 28] 28. Authentication Objectives

The authentication system shall satisfy the following security objectives.

Confidentiality

Identity credentials shall remain confidential.

Integrity

Authentication data shall not be modified without authorization.

Availability

Authentication services shall remain available according to approved platform availability objectives.

Non-Repudiation

Authentication events shall generate audit records sufficient to support security investigations.

Auditability

Security-sensitive operations shall be fully traceable through approved audit logging.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 29] 29. Privacy and Data Protection

Identity information shall be processed according to approved privacy principles.

The authentication system shall enforce:

Data minimization
Purpose limitation
Storage limitation
Secure retention
Secure deletion where permitted
Least-privilege access
Encryption of sensitive authentication data
Protection of authentication secrets

Identity information shall only be processed for approved business purposes.

Deletion requests shall comply with applicable legal, contractual, financial, and regulatory retention requirements.

Authentication data shall never be exposed through public APIs.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 30] 30. Cross-Module Dependencies

Module 01 establishes the identity and authentication foundation for all future platform modules.

Module 01 Produces
User identities
Authentication sessions
JWT access tokens
Refresh token families
OTP verification
Authentication audit logs
Identity lifecycle management
Authentication middleware
Authentication guards
Authenticated identity context

Module 01 provides authentication outputs to Module 02 through approved contracts. Direct database coupling is prohibited.



[WEMP-M01-001 Section 31] 31. Authentication-to-Authorization and Profile Ownership Boundary
Objective

This section defines the ownership boundaries between Identity, business profiles, authentication-security classifications, Authentication Assurance and authorization assignments.

Identity Ownership

Module 01 owns the universal Identity and its authentication lifecycle.

Each individual shall have one immutable Identity Identifier.

The Identity aggregate may contain only Module 01-owned identity and authentication information.

Customer Profile Ownership

Module 06 owns the Customer profile and customer business data.

Seller Profile Ownership

Module 03 owns the Seller profile, seller organization, seller onboarding and seller business status.

Authentication-Security Classification Ownership

Module 01 owns authentication-security classifications used exclusively to select authentication controls.

The approved privileged classifications are:

PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION
SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION

Authentication-security classifications shall not contain permissions and shall not grant application access.

Authorization Ownership

Module 02 owns:

Roles
Permissions
Role assignments
Permission assignments
Role hierarchy
Authorization policies
Authorization evaluation
Authorization decisions

Module 01 shall not persist, manage, infer or evaluate Module 02 roles or permissions.

Authentication Assurance Ownership

Module 01 owns Authentication Assurance for each authenticated session.

Authentication Assurance shall describe the authentication factors completed for that session.

Authentication Assurance shall not represent a role, profile or business approval.

Integration Rules

All modules shall reference the universal Identity using the approved Identity Identifier.

Cross-module communication shall use approved versioned contracts.

Direct cross-module database access and cross-module foreign keys are prohibited.

The authenticated identity context shall contain authentication information only.

Module 02 shall obtain role assignments from its own authoritative data and shall not expect roles or permissions in a Module 01 JWT.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 32] 32. Identifier Tombstoning and Reuse Policy

Objective

Email and mobile identifiers shall not be reassigned merely because an Identity is deleted or anonymized.

Canonical Identifier States

ACTIVE
RETIRED
TOMBSTONED
REUSE_ELIGIBLE
REASSIGNED

ACTIVE belongs to one Identity. RETIRED cannot authenticate/register. TOMBSTONED preserves a protected non-reversible collision reference. REUSE_ELIGIBLE requires approved retention, fraud, Recovery, cross-module and security checks. REASSIGNED records approved transfer without merging history.

Before reuse Module 01 verifies no active Identity, pending registration/Recovery, active Session/Credential/verification dependency, hold or cross-module conflict; the anti-impersonation waiting period elapsed; and Security/fraud permits reuse. Historical Credentials, Sessions, Devices, Recovery and authentication evidence never transfer. Waiting/eligibility remain Business/Security/Legal-TBD. Until approved, reuse is prohibited.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 33] 33. Authentication Assurance Calculation
Objective

Module 01 shall calculate Authentication Assurance from authentication factors successfully completed for the current session.

Approved Calculation

Condition	Authentication Assurance
No authenticated session	AAL0
Approved primary authentication completed	AAL1
Approved primary authentication and approved MFA factor completed	AAL2

Calculation Rules

Authentication Assurance shall:

Be calculated by Module 01
Be bound to the current session and authentication event
Record the authentication methods used
Record the authentication timestamp
Be recalculated after approved step-up authentication
Expire according to the applicable authentication policy
Never be assigned by a client
Never be derived from a Module 02 role
Never be derived solely from MFA enrollment
Never be derived from Customer or Seller profile state

PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION shall require AAL2 before a privileged session is established.

SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION shall require AAL2 before a privileged session is established.

Authorization requirements based on assurance shall be evaluated by Module 02 using the approved authenticated identity context.
Recovery Assurance

Identity recovery is an alternative authentication-security workflow and shall not grant ordinary authenticated access by itself.

Module 01 shall own Recovery policy evaluation, Recovery state, evidence validation, approval-evidence validation, credential and factor changes, Session and token invalidation, Recovery Assurance and the final authentication outcome.

Module 02 shall only authorize which identities may act as Recovery approvers. Module 02 shall not execute Recovery, modify Module 01 authentication records, reset credentials, reset MFA or create an Authenticated Identity Context.

Recovery Assurance shall be evaluated independently from Authentication Assurance.

Successful Recovery shall not establish AAL1 or AAL2 automatically.

A new ordinary authenticated Session shall require fresh authentication after Recovery completion. AAL2 shall require a qualifying MFA authentication event using an approved retained or newly enrolled factor.

PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION and SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION Recovery shall follow the deterministic Recovery policy matrix in Part 5.5. When approved strong self-service evidence is unavailable, privileged Recovery shall require separation of duties and dual control by two distinct Module 02-authorized approvers. The requester shall never approve their own Recovery.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 34] 34. Identity and Security-State Relationship
Objective

The following concepts shall remain independently owned and independently validated:

Concept	Owner
Identity state	Module 01
Email and mobile verification state	Module 01
MFA enrollment state	Module 01
Authentication-security classification	Module 01
Session Authentication Assurance	Module 01
Customer profile state	Module 06
Seller profile and onboarding state	Module 03
Roles, permissions and authorization assignments	Module 02

Approved Relationship

Universal Identity
        ↓
Module 01 Authentication and Verification
        ↓
Session Authentication Assurance
        ↓
Authenticated Identity Context
        ↓
Module 02 Authorization Evaluation

Customer and Seller profiles shall reference the universal Identity through approved identifiers and contracts.

Module 02 role assignments shall reference the universal Identity through the approved Identity Identifier.

No business-profile state or role assignment shall be stored as part of the Module 01 Identity aggregate.

No Module 01 authentication state shall directly grant business access.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 35] 35. Privileged Administrative Provisioning Workflow
Objective

Privileged administrative access shall be established through coordinated Module 01 authentication provisioning and Module 02 role assignment.

Admin Workflow

Authorized Module 02 Admin Provisioning Request
        ↓
Module 01 Identity Creation or Resolution
        ↓
Module 01 PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION Classification Applied
        ↓
Invitation Validation
        ↓
Password Creation
        ↓
Email Verification
        ↓
MFA Enrollment and Verification
        ↓
Module 01 Identity Activation
        ↓
Module 02 Admin Role Assignment
        ↓
Privileged Access Eligible

Super Admin Workflow

Approved Controlled Bootstrap or Provisioning Request
        ↓
Module 01 Identity Creation or Resolution
        ↓
Module 01 SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION Classification Applied
        ↓
Required Verification
        ↓
MFA Enrollment and Verification
        ↓
Module 01 Identity Activation
        ↓
Module 02 Super Admin Role Assignment
        ↓
Privileged Access Eligible

Module 01 Identity activation shall not grant Admin or Super Admin access.

Module 02 role assignment shall not bypass Module 01 privileged-authentication requirements.

If either side of the coordinated workflow fails, privileged access shall remain unavailable.

Public registration of privileged administrative access is prohibited.

All transitions shall be auditable and idempotent.

Direct database coupling is prohibited.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 36] 36. Temporary Account Lock Policy
Objective

Temporary account lockouts shall protect against automated credential attacks while minimizing unnecessary disruption to legitimate users.

Temporary Lock

Temporary authentication lockouts shall be maintained within Redis.

Redis shall store:

Failed login counters
Lock duration
Temporary security metadata
Permanent State

Permanent account status shall remain stored within PostgreSQL.

Temporary Redis lockouts shall not permanently modify the underlying identity record.

Redis failures shall not result in unauthorized authentication.

[WEMP-M01-001 Section 37] 37. Super Admin Access Bootstrap Policy
Objective

The initial universal Identity associated with Super Admin access shall be provisioned through a controlled and auditable bootstrap process.

Approved Bootstrap Workflow

Secure Deployment
        ↓
Approved Bootstrap Command
        ↓
Module 01 Universal Identity Provisioning
        ↓
Module 01 SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION Classification
        ↓
Module 01 Authentication Audit Record
        ↓
Module 02 Super Admin Role Assignment

Module 01 bootstrap shall not grant Super Admin authorization.

Module 02 shall remain the authoritative owner of the Super Admin role.

Direct database manipulation shall not be used to create operational privileged access in either module.

Every bootstrap and role-coordination event shall generate an audit record.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 38] 38. Identity Deletion, Retention and Anonymization Architecture

Objective

Identity deletion shall use a deterministic, auditable and cross-module-safe lifecycle.

Canonical Deletion States

NONE
REQUESTED
VERIFICATION_REQUIRED
VALIDATED
DEPENDENCY_CHECK_PENDING
RETENTION_HOLD
SCHEDULED
EXECUTING
COMPLETED
CANCELLED
REJECTED

Transitions

NONE to REQUESTED creates an approved request. REQUESTED to VERIFICATION_REQUIRED requires additional validation. VERIFICATION_REQUIRED to VALIDATED requires approved AAL2 and request verification. VALIDATED to DEPENDENCY_CHECK_PENDING evaluates retention, legal holds and dependencies. DEPENDENCY_CHECK_PENDING moves to RETENTION_HOLD when blocked or SCHEDULED when clear. SCHEDULED to EXECUTING requires the execution point and concurrency protection. EXECUTING to COMPLETED requires durable Module 01 invalidation, destruction/anonymization and cross-module intents. Cancellation is allowed only from policy-approved non-terminal states. Invalid requests move to REJECTED. COMPLETED, CANCELLED and REJECTED are terminal; no unapproved state skipping.

Execution Effects

At the approved boundary Module 01 prevents new authentication; revokes Sessions and Refresh Token Families; increments Session Version/authentication epoch; revokes Trusted Devices, Credentials and MFA factors as required; invalidates Recovery Code Sets; cancels active Verification/Recovery; destroys authentication secrets; anonymizes/tombstones approved identifiers; preserves C9 Audit; emits required C8 intents; and records immutable transformation evidence. Deletion never grants access.

Cross-Module Boundary

Module 01 does not delete Module 02 authorization data, Module 03 Seller data, Module 06 Customer data or business/financial records. The approved privacy orchestrator coordinates owners through C8. Module 01 completion is not enterprise-wide completion.

Retention Hold and Anonymization

Active C9 legal/retention holds retain RETENTION_HOLD and cannot be bypassed. Authentication may be disabled while evidence remains when approved. Anonymization irreversibly transforms approved identifiers while preserving required integrity; pseudonymization/encryption alone is not called anonymization. C9 transformation/checkpoint rules apply. Immediate physical deletion requires explicit approved Legal/Privacy policy. Waiting, cancellation and retention durations remain Business/Legal/Privacy-TBD.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 39] 39. Authentication Availability and Service Objectives
Objective

The production Module 01 platform shall provide measurable availability without weakening authentication security.

Approved Minimum Availability

The existing minimum production availability target remains 99.9%.

The target applies to the production Module 01 service boundary defined by the C6 criticality and operation catalogues. It shall not be interpreted as permission to accept stale, unverifiable or unauthorized authentication state.

Availability SLI

Availability shall be calculated from eligible production requests grouped by stable C5 operation identifier.

Numerator: eligible requests that complete with an approved successful or valid client-outcome response within the approved latency objective.
Denominator: all eligible requests, including Module 01 and required-dependency server failures.

Malformed requests, invalid credentials, authorization denials, client-cancelled requests and approved rate-limit responses shall be classified consistently and shall not conceal service failures.

Measurement Governance

Measurement window: TBD – Business/Operations approval required.
Planned-maintenance treatment: TBD – Business/Operations approval required.
Latency objectives by service tier: TBD – Business/Product/Operations approval required.
Reporting cadence: TBD – Operations approval required.
Error-budget policy and owner: TBD – Business/Product/Operations approval required.

Production approval is blocked until every required TBD above is approved.

Security Rule

A request rejected because authoritative security state cannot be established is a secure outcome but remains an availability failure where the service was expected to be operational. Security failures shall never be reclassified as availability successes merely because the platform failed closed.

Observability

Availability, latency, saturation, dependency failure, degraded-mode use, failover and restoration objectives shall be monitored through the approved Module 00 observability foundation.
[WEMP-M01-001 Section 40] 40. Part 1 Final Approval
Module Completion Statement

Module 01 – Part 1 establishes the approved identity strategy for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

The following areas are approved:

Verification Model

Token Strategy (Conceptual)

Privacy Principles

Cross-Module Dependencies




WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform
Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 2/10 – Registration Strategy, Verification Entry, Validation Rules, Duplicate Handling & Account Activation

Document ID: WEMP-M01-002
Version: 1.0
Status: Draft
Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 1] 1. Objective
Purpose

This section defines the complete registration lifecycle for all identities within the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

It establishes how identities are created, validated, verified, activated, and protected before authentication is permitted.

The registration process shall ensure:

Identity uniqueness
Contact verification
Fraud prevention
Consistent onboarding
Secure activation
Complete auditability

Registration shall remain independent from business onboarding.

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 2] 2. Registration Principles

The registration system shall comply with the following principles.

Identity First

Registration creates an identity only.

Business onboarding shall occur in later modules.

Verification Before Activation

No identity shall become ACTIVE until all required verification steps have been completed successfully.

Backend Authority

Registration validation shall always occur on the backend.

Client-side validation exists only to improve user experience.

Auditability

Every registration event shall generate audit records.

Privacy

Collected identity information shall follow the approved privacy policies established in Part 1.

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 3] 3. Supported Identity Entry Workflows

Module 01 supports the following identity entry workflows.

Workflow	Purpose	Status
Customer-Initiated Identity Registration	Create or resolve an Identity before Module 06 Customer profile association	Approved
Seller-Initiated Identity Registration	Create or resolve an Identity before Module 03 Seller profile association	Approved
Privileged Admin Access Identity Provisioning	Provision an Identity subject to PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION	Approved
Super Admin Access Bootstrap	Provision an Identity subject to SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION	Approved
Public Privileged-Administrator Registration	Public creation of Admin or Super Admin access	Prohibited
Guest Identity Registration	Create a persistent Guest Identity	Deferred
Social Identity Registration	Register through an external identity provider	Deferred

These workflows shall create or resolve only the universal Module 01 Identity.

Customer profiles shall be created or associated by Module 06.

Seller profiles shall be created or associated by Module 03.

Admin and Super Admin roles shall be assigned only by Module 02.

Module 01 registration and activation shall never assign a business role or grant business permissions.

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 4] 4. Customer-Initiated Identity Registration

Customer-initiated registration shall create or resolve the universal Module 01 Identity only.

Approved identity registration channels are:

Email
Mobile Number

Minimum Module 01 registration information:

First Name
Last Name
Email or Mobile
Password
Country
Acceptance of Terms
Acceptance of Privacy Policy

After required identity verification, Module 01 shall request Customer profile creation or association through an approved Module 06 contract.

Any required Customer role assignment shall occur through an approved Module 02 contract.

Identity activation does not grant customer permissions.

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 5] 5. Seller-Initiated Identity Registration

Seller-initiated registration shall create or resolve the universal Module 01 Identity only.

After required identity verification, Module 01 shall request Seller profile creation or association through an approved Module 03 contract.

Module 03 owns Seller profile data, business onboarding and seller approval.

Any required Seller role assignment shall occur through an approved Module 02 contract.

Identity activation does not grant seller permissions or selling privileges.

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 6] 6. Privileged Admin Access Identity Provisioning

Public Admin registration is prohibited.

Module 01 owns Identity provisioning and PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION controls.

Module 02 owns the Admin role and related authorization.

Completion of Module 01 provisioning alone does not grant administrative access.

Invitation tokens shall be:

Single-use
Time-limited
Cryptographically secure

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 7] 7. Super Admin Access Bootstrap

Super Admin identities shall never be created through public registration APIs.

Approved Identity provisioning methods are:

Bootstrap Command
Controlled Deployment Process

Module 01 owns Identity provisioning and SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION controls.

Module 02 owns the Super Admin role and related authorization.

Completion of Module 01 provisioning alone does not grant Super Admin access.

Every creation and role-coordination event shall be audited.
[WEMP-M01-002 Section 8] 8. Registration Entry Points

Approved entry points:

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 11] 11. Email Validation Rules

Every email shall:

Be trimmed
Converted to lowercase
Pass RFC-compliant validation
Reject disposable domains (configurable)
Reject malformed addresses
Reject duplicate active accounts

Maximum length:

320 characters

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 12] 12. Mobile Validation Rules

Every mobile number shall:

Include country code
Follow E.164 format
Remove whitespace
Reject invalid prefixes
Reject duplicate active accounts

The backend shall normalize all stored numbers.

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 13] 13. Password Validation Rules

Password is mandatory.

Password validation shall follow the approved Password Policy defined in Module 01 – Part 3.


[WEMP-M01-002 Section 14] 14. Duplicate Identity Detection

Duplicate checks shall occur before account creation.

Checks include:

Email
Mobile Number
Identity Identifier

Registration shall fail if a conflicting active identity exists.

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 15] 15. Duplicate Handling Policy

The backend shall return standardized responses.

Duplicate email:

Email already registered.

Duplicate mobile:

Mobile number already registered.

Internal identity information shall never be disclosed.

Enumeration attacks shall be prevented.

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 16] 16. Pending Registration

Unverified registrations shall remain in:

PENDING_VERIFICATION

Pending identities:

Cannot authenticate
Cannot create sessions
Cannot access protected APIs

Audit record generated

be permitted.

Secure password hashing
OTP generation requirements shall be defined in Module 01 – Part 4.

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 21] 21. Terms Acceptance, Privacy Notice and Consent Architecture

Objective

Module 01 shall distinguish contractual Terms acceptance, Privacy Notice acknowledgement and consent. They shall not use one generic flag.

Terms Acceptance Receipt records document ID/version, timestamp, Identity/registration reference, locale, channel, protected source where permitted, evidence schema, correlation and creation time.

Privacy Notice Receipt records notice ID/version, presentation time, acknowledgement where required, Identity/registration reference, locale, channel, purpose/data-category/controller references, evidence schema and correlation. Acknowledgement is not consent.

Consent is collected only when an approved purpose uses consent. It is purpose-specific, informed, versioned, timestamped, withdrawable where applicable and independent of unrelated purposes. Consent Record fields: ID, Identity, purpose, notice ID/version, evidence reference, state, grant/withdraw timestamps, channel, schema and correlation.

Canonical Consent States

NOT_REQUIRED
PENDING
GRANTED
WITHDRAWN
EXPIRED
SUPERSEDED
INVALIDATED

Withdrawal stops future processing that depends exclusively on that consent after policy evaluation. It does not automatically delete Identity/evidence, unrelated processing, Modules 03/06 profiles or Module 02 roles. Registration proceeds only when required Terms, notice and genuine consent evidence is valid. Terms/notice shall not be labelled consent.
[WEMP-M01-002 Section 22] 22. Registration Rate Limiting Policy

Objective

Registration endpoints shall implement rate limiting to protect against automated abuse, denial-of-service attempts, and mass account creation.

Requirements

• The backend shall enforce configurable limits for:
  - Maximum registrations per IP Address
  - Maximum registrations per Email Address
  - Maximum registrations per Mobile Number

• A configurable cooldown period shall apply after the approved threshold is exceeded.

• Rate-limit thresholds shall remain configurable through environment settings.

Rate limiting shall be enforced by the backend and shall not rely on client-side controls.


[WEMP-M01-002 Section 23] 23. Country & Region Validation

Objective

Registration shall validate geographic information to ensure consistent identity records.

Requirements

• Supported countries shall be configurable.
• Country codes shall follow international standards.
• Mobile country codes shall be validated.
• Locale defaults shall be assigned automatically.
• Time zone information shall be stored where applicable.

Country validation rules shall be maintained centrally and applied consistently across all registration channels.


[WEMP-M01-002 Section 24] 24. Identity Normalization Rules

Objective

Identity information shall be normalized before duplicate detection and persistence.

Requirements

• Email addresses shall be converted to lowercase.
• Leading and trailing whitespace shall be removed.
• Unicode normalization shall be applied where required.
• Mobile numbers shall be normalized using E.164 format.

Normalization shall occur before validation and duplicate detection.

[WEMP-M01-002 Section 25] 25. Registration State Machine

Objective

Every registration shall follow a defined lifecycle before activation.

Approved Registration States

Draft
      ?
Pending Verification
      ?
Verified
      ?
Active

Alternative Transitions

Pending Verification
        ?
Expired

Pending Verification
        ?
Cancelled

State transitions shall be validated exclusively by the backend.


[WEMP-M01-002 Section 26] 26. Registration Expiration Policy

Objective

Pending registrations shall expire if verification is not completed within the approved timeframe.

Requirements

• Pending registrations shall expire after a configurable duration.
• Expired registrations shall not be activated.
• Expired OTPs shall never be accepted.
• Expired registration records shall follow the approved cleanup policy.

Expiration periods shall remain configurable.


[WEMP-M01-002 Section 27] 27. Registration Error Codes

Objective

Registration failures shall return standardized error codes.

Approved Error Codes

| Code | Meaning |
|------|---------|
| REG-001 | Duplicate Email |
| REG-002 | Duplicate Mobile |
| REG-003 | Invalid Password |
| REG-004 | Registration Expired |
| REG-005 | Validation Failed |

Error codes shall remain stable across API versions.


[WEMP-M01-002 Section 28] 28. Registration Audit Fields

Objective

Every registration event shall generate an audit record.

Audit Record Fields

• Timestamp
• Correlation ID
• IP Address
• User Agent
• Device ID (where available)
• Identity ID
• Event Type

Audit records shall support security investigations while protecting sensitive information.


[WEMP-M01-002 Section 29] 29. Cross-Module Outputs

Objective

Module 01 – Part 2 produces identity-registration artifacts consumed through approved cross-module contracts.

Produces

Pending Universal Identity

Seller Profile Association Request for Module 03 where applicable

Consumed By

Module 01 registration outputs shall not contain business-profile data, roles, permissions or authorization decisions.

Future modules shall consume approved registration outputs rather than implementing independent registration processes.
