WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform
Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 3.1A — Authentication Foundation & Credential Architecture

Document ID: WEMP-M01-003A

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-003A Section 1] 1. Objective
Purpose

This section establishes the authentication architecture and credential management principles for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

It defines how authenticated identities shall securely obtain, maintain, and revoke access across all approved applications.

Authentication shall provide:

Secure identity verification
Enterprise-grade credential protection
Multi-device support
Session lifecycle management
Auditability
Scalability
Future federation readiness

Authentication shall remain independent from authorization.

Authorization shall be defined separately in Module 02.

[WEMP-M01-003A Section 2] 2. Scope
Included

This part defines:

Authentication architecture
Credential architecture
Authentication principles
Hybrid authentication model
Identity credential lifecycle
Excluded

The following items belong to later parts of Module 01:

Password hashing implementation
JWT payload definition
Access token policy
Refresh token rotation
MFA implementation
Session storage
Route guards
Authentication middleware
Logout workflow
API contracts

No business functionality shall be implemented within this part.

[WEMP-M01-003A Section 3] 3. Authentication Principles

The authentication platform shall follow the principles below.

3.1 Identity Before Access

Authentication shall always establish identity before access to protected resources.

Authentication shall never directly grant permissions.

3.2 Separation of Responsibilities

Authentication determines:

Who the user is.

Authorization determines:

What the authenticated identity may access.

Permission evaluation shall occur after successful authentication.

3.3 Backend Authority

Authentication decisions shall always be made by the backend.

Client applications shall never determine authentication status.

3.4 Zero Trust

Every protected request shall require authentication validation.

Authenticated requests shall never be trusted solely because previous requests were authenticated.

3.5 Secure by Default

Authentication services shall deny access whenever validation cannot be completed successfully.

Unexpected failures shall result in access denial rather than access approval.

3.6 Auditability

Authentication events shall generate immutable audit records.

Authentication failures shall be auditable.

Authentication successes shall be auditable.

Security-sensitive operations shall be fully traceable.

3.7 Revocability

Authentication shall support immediate session revocation.

Credential revocation shall not require identity deletion.

3.8 Multi-Device Support

A single authenticated identity may maintain multiple concurrent authenticated sessions.

Each session shall remain independently managed.

C2 Session Validation Clarification

ADR-M01-001 shall distinguish stateless JWT cryptographic validation from state-aware protected-request authentication.

Every protected request shall validate authoritative Session state and Session Version. Access Token invalidation shall occur through Session state/version validation. PostgreSQL shall remain authoritative and Redis shall contain only a non-authoritative Session Validation Projection.
[WEMP-M01-003A Section 4] 4. Hybrid Authentication Architecture
Objective

The WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform shall adopt a Hybrid Enterprise Authentication Architecture.

The approved architecture combines:

JWT performance
Stateful session management
Refresh token rotation
Server-side session visibility
Device awareness

This architecture becomes the approved authentication foundation for all future modules.

Architecture Overview
                    Identity
                       │
                       ▼
              Authentication Service
                       │
        ┌──────────────┼──────────────┐
        ▼                              ▼
 Session Store                 JWT Access Token
(PostgreSQL + Redis)                  │
        │                             ▼
        │                     Protected APIs
        │
        ▼
Refresh Token Family
Responsibilities
Authentication Service

Responsible for:

Identity verification
Password validation
Session creation
Session revocation
Token issuance
Refresh token rotation
Audit logging
PostgreSQL

Stores:

Identity records
Session records
Refresh token families
Login history
Authentication audit records

PostgreSQL remains the authoritative authentication datastore.

Redis

Stores temporary authentication state including:

Active session cache
Temporary lockouts
Rate-limit counters
Short-lived security state

Redis improves performance but shall not become the source of truth.

JWT Access Tokens

JWT Access Tokens shall provide short-lived cryptographic proof of authentication.

JWT Access Tokens shall not grant authorization and shall not store business state.

JWT signature and claim validation shall be stateless.

Acceptance of a JWT for a protected request shall remain state-aware because the referenced Module 01 session must remain valid.

A cryptographically valid JWT shall not be sufficient when:

The referenced session does not exist;
The referenced session is not ACTIVE;
The referenced session has expired;
The referenced session has been revoked;
The Identity is not permitted to authenticate;
The token session version does not match the authoritative session version; or
Any mandatory authentication validation cannot be completed safely.

Refresh Tokens

Refresh Tokens shall remain opaque, server-controlled credentials.

Every Refresh Token shall belong to one Identity, one Session and one Refresh Token Family.

Only protected Refresh Token representations shall be persisted.

Refresh Tokens shall support atomic rotation, reuse detection and family revocation.
[WEMP-M01-003A Section 5] 5. Credential Architecture
Objective

Credentials represent the authentication factors used to verify an identity.

Credentials shall remain separate from identity records.

Approved Credential Types

Phase 1 supports:

Password Credential
Email Verification Credential
Mobile Verification Credential

Future credential types may include:

Passkeys
Hardware Keys
OAuth Providers
Enterprise SSO
Credential Ownership

Every credential shall belong to one identity only.

Credential sharing is prohibited.

Credential transfer is prohibited.

Credential Lifecycle

Every credential shall follow the lifecycle below.

Created
      ↓
Verified
      ↓
Active
      ↓
Updated
      ↓
Revoked

Credential deletion shall follow approved retention policies.

Credential Storage Principles

Credentials shall never be stored in plaintext.

Sensitive credential material shall never appear in:

API responses
Browser storage
Application logs
Audit records
Monitoring dashboards

Only approved credential representations may be persisted.

Credential Revocation

Credential revocation shall immediately invalidate future authentication attempts using the revoked credential.

Credential revocation shall not automatically delete the associated identity.

Credential Independence

An identity may possess multiple authentication credentials over its lifetime.

Credential updates shall not require creation of a new identity.

Identity continuity shall be preserved across credential changes.

Part 3.1A Acceptance Criteria

This section is complete when:

Authentication principles are approved.
Hybrid authentication architecture is approved.
Credential architecture is approved.
Credential ownership rules are approved.
Credential lifecycle is approved.
Storage principles are approved.
Revocation principles are approved.
No JWT implementation details have been introduced.
No password implementation details have been introduced.
No session implementation details have been introduced.






WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform
Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 3.1B – Password Security & Credential Validation

Document ID: WEMP-M01-003B

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-003B Section 6] 6. Password Architecture
Objective

This section defines the enterprise password architecture for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

Passwords represent one of the approved authentication credentials within the Identity & Authentication framework.

Password management shall prioritize:

Confidentiality
Integrity
Availability
Resistance to credential theft
Compliance with enterprise security standards

Passwords shall never be treated as business data.

Password Ownership

Each password shall belong to one identity only.

Password sharing is prohibited.

Password transfer is prohibited.

Administrative users shall never know another user's password.

Password Lifecycle

Every password shall follow the lifecycle below.

Created
      ↓
Validated
      ↓
Hashed
      ↓
Stored
      ↓
Authenticated
      ↓
Updated
      ↓
Retired

Passwords shall never revert to plaintext after hashing.

Password Storage Principles

Passwords shall:

Never be stored in plaintext
Never appear in logs
Never appear in audit records
Never appear in API responses
Never be cached
Never be exported

Only approved password hashes shall be stored.

[WEMP-M01-003B Section 7] 7. Password Policy
Objective

Passwords shall comply with the enterprise password policy before account creation or password updates.

Minimum Requirements

Passwords shall:

Be required for every identity
Meet the approved complexity policy
Meet the approved length policy
Pass strength validation
Pass prohibited-password validation

Detailed password complexity requirements shall remain centrally configurable.

Prohibited Passwords

Passwords shall not contain:

Email address
Mobile number
First name
Last name
Sequential keyboard patterns
Common dictionary passwords
Previously compromised passwords (where supported)
Password Strength

Passwords shall satisfy the approved minimum strength score before acceptance.

Strength evaluation shall occur before password hashing.

[WEMP-M01-003B Section 8] 8. Password Hashing Strategy
Objective

Passwords shall be protected using the approved one-way Argon2id password hashing algorithm.

Approved Algorithm

Argon2id is the only approved Phase 1 password-hashing algorithm. Alternatives require architecture and Security approval.

Versioned Parameter Profile

Every stored password hash shall identify a versioned Argon2id parameter profile containing:

Memory cost: SECURITY-TBD
Time cost: SECURITY-TBD
Parallelism: SECURITY-TBD
Output length: SECURITY-TBD
Salt length/entropy requirement: SECURITY-TBD
Profile version

No numerical parameter shall be invented by implementation. Credential implementation approval is blocked until Security approves the required numerical profile.

Benchmark Governance

Security and Engineering shall benchmark the profile on production-equivalent ECS Fargate compute classes under expected concurrency.
Benchmarking shall measure median and tail verification latency, memory pressure, CPU saturation, concurrency limits and denial-of-service resistance.
Maximum acceptable authentication latency and concurrency budgets remain Security/Performance-TBD.

Hashing Principles

Every password shall use a unique cryptographically secure salt.
Raw passwords shall never be persisted or logged.
Password hashes shall never be exposed through APIs, events, metrics or traces.
Comparison shall use the approved Argon2 verification function.

Progressive Rehash

On successful authentication, Module 01 shall compare the stored profile with the current approved profile.
An outdated profile shall trigger progressive rehash within a bounded, observable workflow.
Failed authentication shall not trigger rehash.
Rehash shall not change authentication outcome and shall use the C4 Credential transaction boundary.
Profile upgrades shall preserve Password History requirements.

Denial-of-Service Controls

Rate limits, bounded work queues, concurrency limits and capacity monitoring shall protect expensive password verification without weakening the approved profile.
[WEMP-M01-003B Section 9] 9. Password History Policy
Objective

Password reuse shall be controlled to improve account security.

Requirements

The platform shall maintain password history for each identity.

Previously used passwords shall not be immediately reusable.

History depth shall remain configurable.

Password history shall store only password hashes.

Password Reuse

The backend shall validate password history before accepting a new password.

Reuse of restricted historical passwords shall be rejected.

[WEMP-M01-003B Section 10] 10. Credential Validation Rules
Objective

Credential validation shall occur before authentication decisions.

Validation Principles

Credentials shall be validated for:

Format
Completeness
Identity ownership
Account status
Verification status
Security restrictions
Backend Validation

Credential validation shall always occur on the backend.

Client-side validation shall improve usability only.

Validation Order

The authentication service shall validate credentials using the following sequence.

Receive Credential
        ↓
Normalize Input
        ↓
Validate Format
        ↓
Locate Identity
        ↓
Validate Identity State
        ↓
Validate Credential
        ↓
Authentication Decision
Failed Validation

Failed credential validation shall:

Generate an audit event
Increment applicable security counters
Return standardized authentication errors
Avoid exposing internal identity information
Part 3.1B Acceptance Criteria

This section is complete when:

Password architecture is approved.
Password policy is approved.
Password hashing strategy is approved.
Password history policy is approved.
Credential validation workflow is approved.
Backend validation responsibilities are defined.
Credential lifecycle requirements are documented.
No JWT implementation details have been introduced.
No session implementation details have been introduced.






WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform
Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 3.2 — JWT Architecture, Access Tokens, Refresh Tokens & Session Architecture

Document ID: WEMP-M01-003C

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

Architecture Decision Reference

This document implements the approved authentication architecture defined in:

ADR-M01-001

Approved Authentication Architecture

Hybrid Enterprise Authentication

All authentication implementation shall comply with ADR-M01-001.
C1 Ownership Clarification

ADR-M01-001 shall treat all sessions as universal Identity sessions rather than role-specific sessions.

JWTs shall contain authentication claims only. The acr and amr claims shall represent session authentication assurance and methods. Module 02 shall resolve roles and perform authorization independently.

[WEMP-M01-003C Section 11] 11. JWT Architecture
Objective

This section defines the approved JWT architecture for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

JSON Web Tokens (JWT) shall serve as short-lived cryptographic authentication credentials associated with authenticated Module 01 sessions.

JWT signature and registered-claim validation shall be stateless.

Authentication of a protected request shall not be fully stateless.

Every protected request shall validate the referenced authoritative Module 01 session before the request is accepted.

JWTs shall not replace server-managed sessions.

A valid signature shall prove that Module 01 issued the token.

A valid signature shall not prove that the referenced session remains active.

JWT Principles

The JWT implementation shall comply with the following principles:

Short-lived
Stateless
Digitally signed
Tamper evident
Independently verifiable
Transportable across approved clients

JWTs shall never be treated as session records.

JWT Responsibilities

JWT Access Tokens shall only:

Represent an authenticated identity
Carry approved authentication claims
Identify the current session
Provide authentication context for Module 02 authorization evaluation

JWTs shall never contain:

Passwords
OTP values
Bank information
Personal documents
Payment information
Business data
Sensitive personal information

Approved JWT Claims

JWT Access Tokens may contain only approved authentication claims.

Approved claims include:

• sub (Identity Identifier)
• sid (Session Identifier)
• jti (JWT Identifier)
• iss (Issuer)
• aud (Audience)
• iat (Issued At)
• exp (Expiration Time)
• nbf (Not Before)
• amr (Authentication Method Reference)
• acr (Authentication Assurance Context)
• mfaState (Current MFA State)
• deviceSessionId (Device-Session Identifier)
• correlationId (Correlation Identifier)
• sessionVersion (Authoritative Session Version)

JWTs shall never contain:

• Passwords
• OTP values
• Financial information
• Personal documents
• Sensitive personal information
• Business data


Session-Version Claim

The sessionVersion claim shall contain the session version that existed when the Access Token was issued.

The Authentication Service shall compare sessionVersion with the authoritative current session version during every protected request.

The request shall be rejected when the values do not match.

The session version shall be incremented whenever a security event invalidates previously issued Access Tokens for that session.

The sessionVersion claim is authentication metadata.

It shall not represent a role, permission, profile or authorization decision.
C7 JWT Header and Cryptographic Policy

Approved Phase 1 signing algorithm: ES256.
Approved production signing service: AWS KMS asymmetric ECC_NIST_P256 key with ECDSA_SHA_256 signing operation.
Production private signing key material is non-exportable.

Required protected header fields:

alg = ES256
kid = unique approved signing-key identifier
typ = JWT

Module 01 shall reject alg=none, missing kid, unknown kid, duplicate/ambiguous kid, unexpected algorithm, unexpected token type and any algorithm selected outside the server-controlled allowlist.

Issuer and audience shall be exact, environment-specific approved values. Their literal values remain deployment-configuration TBD until domains/environments are approved.

The approved C2 authentication-only claim set remains unchanged. Roles, permissions, profiles and user-type claims remain prohibited.
JWT Claim Ownership Rules

JWT access tokens shall not contain:

Customer profile classification
Seller profile classification
Admin role
Super Admin role
Any Module 02 role
Any Module 02 permission
Business-profile state
Seller approval state
Precomputed authorization decisions

The acr claim shall represent the Authentication Assurance Level achieved for the current session.

The amr claim shall represent the authentication methods successfully completed for the current session.

Neither acr nor amr shall represent role assignment or business-profile status.

Authentication-security classification shall remain server-controlled and shall not be exposed as a business role claim.

[WEMP-M01-003C Section 12] 12. Access Token Strategy
Objective

Access Tokens provide short-lived proof of authentication for protected requests. Module 02 independently evaluates authorization.

They shall be issued only after successful authentication.

Access Token Principles

Access Tokens shall:

Be short-lived
Be digitally signed
Be independently verifiable
Be non-persistent
Be replaceable

Access Tokens shall only be renewed through the approved Refresh Token workflow.

Access Token Lifecycle

Authentication Completed
        ↓
Authoritative Session Created
        ↓
Access Token Issued with sid and sessionVersion
        ↓
Protected Request Received
        ↓
JWT Signature and Claims Validated
        ↓
Authoritative Session State and Version Validated
        ↓
Authenticated Identity Context Established
        ↓
Access Token Expires or Session Becomes Invalid
        ↓
Refresh Token Exchange where permitted
        ↓
New Access Token Issued with Current Session Version

An expired Access Token shall never be reactivated.

An Access Token referencing an invalid session shall be rejected even when its signature and expiration claim remain valid.

An Access Token with a stale sessionVersion shall be rejected.

Access Token renewal shall require successful Refresh Token and authoritative session validation.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 13] 13. Refresh Token Strategy
Objective

Refresh Tokens provide controlled renewal of Access Tokens.

Refresh Tokens shall remain server-controlled.

Refresh Token Principles

Refresh Tokens shall:

Be generated using an approved cryptographically secure random source
Remain opaque to clients
Be independently unique
Be stored only as approved protected representations
Support atomic single-use rotation
Support reuse detection
Support family revocation
Be bound to one Identity
Be bound to one Session
Be bound to one Device-Session
Belong to one Refresh Token Family

Refresh Tokens shall never function as Access Tokens.

Possession of a Refresh Token shall not be sufficient to issue new credentials unless:

The protected stored representation matches;
The token is the current active member of its family;
The referenced session is ACTIVE;
The session has not expired;
The session has not been revoked;
The Identity may authenticate; and
The rotation transaction succeeds atomically.

Refresh Token Ownership

Every Refresh Token shall belong to:

One Identity
One Session
One Device-Session
One Refresh Token Family

Sharing Refresh Tokens across sessions is prohibited.

Persisted Refresh Token State

The persisted state shall distinguish:

ACTIVE
USED
REVOKED
EXPIRED

Raw Refresh Tokens shall never be persisted.

The stored record shall preserve the predecessor and successor relationship required for rotation and reuse investigation.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 14] 14. Refresh Token Rotation
Objective

Every successful refresh operation shall atomically consume the presented Refresh Token and create exactly one successor.

Approved Rotation Workflow

Refresh Request Received
        ↓
Hash Presented Refresh Token
        ↓
Begin Authoritative Database Transaction
        ↓
Lock Refresh Token Family and Presented Token Record
        ↓
Validate Identity, Session, Device-Session, Family and Token State
        ↓
Confirm Presented Token State is ACTIVE
        ↓
Mark Presented Token USED
        ↓
Create Exactly One Successor Refresh Token
        ↓
Record Predecessor and Successor Relationship
        ↓
Commit Transaction
        ↓
Return New Access Token and New Refresh Token

Only one transaction shall successfully consume an ACTIVE Refresh Token.

Only the newest unconsumed Refresh Token in the family shall remain ACTIVE.

A concurrent request presenting the same predecessor after it has been consumed shall be processed as reuse according to §15.

The system shall never issue two valid successors for the same Refresh Token.

Rotation Benefits

Refresh Token Rotation provides:

Replay protection
Session integrity
Device isolation
Credential lifecycle management
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 15] 15. Refresh Token Reuse Detection
Objective

The authentication platform shall detect attempts to reuse previously consumed, revoked or superseded Refresh Tokens.

Approved Workflow

Presented Refresh Token Located
        ↓
Token State Is USED, REVOKED or Superseded
        ↓
Reuse Detected
        ↓
Begin Authoritative Revocation Transaction
        ↓
Revoke Entire Refresh Token Family
        ↓
Revoke the Referenced Session
        ↓
Increment the Authoritative Session Version
        ↓
Commit Revocation
        ↓
Invalidate or Update Session Validation Projection
        ↓
Generate Security Audit Event
        ↓
Trigger User Security Notification

Security Response

Confirmed Refresh Token reuse shall:

Revoke the complete Refresh Token Family;
Revoke the associated Session;
Increment the authoritative Session Version;
Invalidate previously issued Access Tokens through session-version validation;
Prevent further refresh operations;
Generate a security audit event;
Generate security monitoring telemetry; and
Trigger the approved user notification.

Reuse detection and family revocation shall be atomic with respect to authoritative Refresh Token state.

The reuse response shall not expose token-family or session internals to the client.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 16] 16. Session and Token Revocation
Objective

Module 01 shall support authoritative revocation of Sessions and Refresh Token Families.

Access Tokens shall be invalidated through authoritative Session state and Session Version validation.

Revocation Triggers

Revocation may be initiated because of:

Current-session logout
Logout All Devices
Selected-session revocation
Password Change
Password Reset
Identity Lock
Identity Suspension
Identity Disablement
Device revocation
Confirmed Refresh Token reuse
Authorized administrative request
Security incident

Authoritative Revocation Workflow

Revocation Requested
        ↓
Validate Revocation Authority
        ↓
Begin Authoritative Database Transaction
        ↓
Set Applicable Session State to REVOKED
        ↓
Increment Applicable Session Version
        ↓
Revoke Applicable Refresh Token Family
        ↓
Record Revocation Reason and Timestamp
        ↓
Commit Transaction
        ↓
Invalidate or Update Session Validation Projection
        ↓
Generate Audit and Security Events
        ↓
Return Success

Revocation shall be considered committed only after the authoritative PostgreSQL transaction succeeds.

A revocation operation shall not report success until the session-validation projection has been invalidated or updated sufficiently to prevent subsequent acceptance of the revoked session.

Revocation Effect

After committed revocation:

No Refresh Token in the revoked family shall be accepted.
No subsequent protected request using an Access Token for the revoked Session and earlier Session Version shall be accepted.
The revoked Session shall never return to ACTIVE.
New access shall require a new approved authentication workflow.

Immediate Revocation Definition

Immediate revocation means that every protected request beginning session validation after committed revocation shall be rejected.

A request that completed authoritative session validation before the revocation transaction committed is an in-flight request and is not retroactively cancelled by this definition.

If current Session state cannot be established safely, the protected request shall be denied.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 17] 17. Cryptographic Key, JWT Signing and Secret Lifecycle Architecture
Objective

Module 01 shall provide deterministic cryptographic ownership, lifecycle, rotation, compromise and recovery controls without distributing private signing material.

Cryptographic Asset Inventory

Asset	Owner	Purpose	Authoritative Storage	Exportability
JWT signing private key	Module 01 Authentication Service/Security Operations	ES256 signing	AWS KMS asymmetric ECC_NIST_P256 key	Non-exportable in production
JWT public verification key	Module 01	JWT verification	Versioned JWKS derived from KMS public key	Public
Password hash	Module 01 Identity Aggregate	Password verification	PostgreSQL protected hash	Non-reversible
Refresh Token digest	Module 01 Session Aggregate	Opaque token lookup/single use	PostgreSQL digest	Non-reversible
OTP digest	Module 01 Verification Aggregate	Challenge verification	PostgreSQL protected digest	Non-reversible
Recovery Code digest	Module 01 Identity Aggregate	Emergency Recovery evidence	PostgreSQL digest	Non-reversible
MFA factor secret	Module 01 Identity Aggregate	TOTP verification	Envelope-encrypted ciphertext in PostgreSQL; KEK in KMS	Plaintext non-exportable from service boundary
Personal-data encryption key	Module 01/Security Operations	Approved reversible personal-data protection	KMS-backed envelope encryption	Non-exportable KEK
Deterministic lookup/HMAC key	Module 01/Security Operations	Protected identifier lookup	Secrets Manager or approved KMS-backed secret	Non-exportable outside Module 01
CSRF secret	Module 01 Web authentication boundary	C5 Web CSRF protection	Secrets Manager	Non-exportable to other purposes
Provider/database credentials	Owning integration/Operations	Dependency authentication	AWS Secrets Manager	Non-exportable to clients
Audit integrity key where used	Security Operations	Approved audit-integrity mechanism	Separate KMS key	Non-exportable

Key-Use Separation

A key or secret shall have one cryptographic purpose. JWT signing, MFA encryption, personal-data encryption, deterministic lookup, CSRF protection, audit integrity and provider credentials shall use separate keys/secrets and separate access policies.

JWT Signing Model

Only the Module 01 Authentication Service signing role may request ES256 signatures from the approved KMS key.
Business modules, Module 02, Web clients and Flutter clients shall never receive private signing material.
The algorithm allowlist is server policy and shall not be selected from token input.

Canonical Signing-Key Lifecycle

GENERATED
ACTIVE_SIGNING
VERIFY_ONLY
RETIRED
DESTROYED
COMPROMISED

Exactly one normal ACTIVE_SIGNING key shall exist per environment and issuer.
Multiple VERIFY_ONLY keys may exist during approved rotation overlap.
Only ACTIVE_SIGNING may sign.
VERIFY_ONLY may verify but never sign.
RETIRED, DESTROYED and COMPROMISED keys shall neither sign nor verify.
Lifecycle transitions require separation of duties, immutable audit and approved change control.

Normal Rotation

[WEMP-M01-003C Section 1] 1. Generate a new non-exportable KMS key and unique kid.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 2] 2. Publish its public key in the environment-specific JWKS while the key is GENERATED.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 3] 3. Verify every required consumer can resolve and validate the new public key.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 4] 4. Promote the new key to ACTIVE_SIGNING.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 5] 5. Move the previous key to VERIFY_ONLY.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 6] 6. Retain verification overlap for at least maximum Access Token lifetime plus approved clock skew and propagation allowance.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 7] 7. Move the old key to RETIRED only after no valid Access Token can depend on it.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 8] 8. Destroy or retain key metadata only under the approved key-retention policy.

Access Token lifetime, clock skew, propagation allowance and key-retention duration remain explicit Security/Operations-TBD values. Production token issuance is blocked until approved.

Emergency Compromise

On suspected or confirmed private-key compromise:

Mark the key COMPROMISED immediately.
Stop signing with it.
Reject every token carrying the compromised kid even if signature and C2 Session state otherwise validate.
Activate an approved standby/new key through emergency change control.
Invalidate verifier/JWKS caches through bounded refresh and incident procedures.
Evaluate and execute platform-wide Session Version or authentication-epoch invalidation according to Security incident scope.
Preserve non-secret forensic metadata.
Reconcile every verifier and JWKS consumer.
Prevent the compromised key from returning after restoration.
Require Security validation before normal readiness resumes.

JWKS Contract

Operation identifier: M01-KEY-001.
Method/path: GET /api/v1/.well-known/jwks.json.
Exposure: PUBLIC_ANONYMOUS.
Response: keys containing only approved public kty, crv, use, alg, kid, x and y values.
For ES256: kty=EC, crv=P-256, use=sig and alg=ES256.
Private parameters and secret metadata are prohibited.
HTTPS is mandatory.
Cache-Control shall be bounded by the approved rotation/propagation policy.
Unknown kid may trigger one bounded, rate-limited refresh; request storms shall be coalesced.
Failure to resolve a trusted key fails verification closed.
Retirement/compromise status shall propagate independently of ordinary cache age where incident controls require it.

Verification Contract

Middleware shall validate token size and structure; header allowlist; alg=ES256; typ=JWT; mandatory trusted kid and lifecycle status; signature; exact environment issuer and audience; token type and time claims; required C2 authentication claims; compromised-key status; authoritative Session state; and Session Version, in that order before creating the Authenticated Identity Context.

Envelope Encryption

Reversible Module 01 secrets shall use authenticated envelope encryption.
AWS KMS owns the non-exportable Key Encryption Key.
The service obtains an approved Data Encryption Key, uses authenticated encryption and stores only ciphertext plus nonce/IV, authentication tag, algorithm version, encrypted DEK/reference and key version.
Encryption context shall bind environment, Module 01, record type and record identifier.
Decrypt permissions use least privilege.
Wrong context, altered ciphertext/tag or unavailable required key fails closed.
Plaintext shall exist only in bounded process memory and shall never be persisted or logged.
Rotation shall support DEK rewrap or record re-encryption with idempotent progress and verification.

Digest and Random-Value Governance

Refresh Tokens, OTPs and Recovery Codes shall use approved CSPRNG generation, purpose binding, protected digest storage and safe comparison.
Raw values shall never be logged or persisted.
HMAC use shall include explicit domain/purpose separation and a dedicated key.
Entropy lengths and digest profiles remain Security-TBD and block the affected credential/challenge implementation until approved.

Environment Isolation

Development, test, staging and production shall use distinct KMS keys, issuers, audiences, JWKS, encryption contexts, provider credentials and database credentials.
Production secrets and keys shall never be copied to lower environments.
Cross-environment tokens shall fail issuer, audience or key-trust validation.

Audit Events

SigningKeyGenerated
SigningKeyPublished
SigningKeyActivated
SigningKeyMovedToVerifyOnly
SigningKeyRetired
SigningKeyDestroyed
SigningKeyCompromiseSuspected
SigningKeyCompromised
SigningKeyEmergencyRotationCompleted
CryptographicPolicyRejected
KmsAccessDenied
DecryptionFailed
ReencryptionStarted
ReencryptionCompleted

No audit event shall contain key material, plaintext secrets or token values.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 18] 18. Session Architecture
Objective

Authentication sessions shall represent authenticated interactions between identities and approved client devices.

PostgreSQL Session records are the authoritative records of authenticated session state.

JWT Access Tokens reference Session state but do not contain authoritative Session state.

A protected request is authenticated only when:

The JWT is cryptographically and semantically valid;
The referenced Session exists;
The Session state is ACTIVE;
The Session has not exceeded its idle or absolute expiration;
The Session Version matches the Access Token sessionVersion claim;
The referenced Identity may authenticate; and
All mandatory authentication-policy checks succeed.

JWT Access Tokens do not replace authoritative Session records.

Session Responsibilities

Sessions shall maintain:

Session Identifier
Identity Identifier
Device-Session Identifier
Authentication Timestamp
Last Activity Timestamp
Refresh Token Family Identifier
Current Authentication State
Authentication Methods Used
Authentication Assurance Level
MFA Verification Timestamp where applicable
Authentication-Security Policy Reference
Session Version
Idle Expiration Timestamp
Absolute Expiration Timestamp
Revocation Timestamp where applicable
Revocation Reason where applicable

Sessions shall not contain:

Customer or Seller profile state
Admin or Super Admin roles
Permissions
Authorization policies
Authorization decisions

The Authentication-Security Policy Reference shall identify the Module 01 authentication controls applicable to the session and shall not represent a Module 02 role.
JWTs reference sessions but do not replace them.

Session Authority

PostgreSQL shall remain the authoritative session datastore.

Session Identifier

Every session shall use a globally unique UUID Version 7.

Session identifiers shall:

• Never be sequential
• Never be reused
• Never expose internal database identifiers.

Device Identifier

Every authenticated session shall maintain a unique device identifier.

Device identifiers support:

• Device Management
• Session Tracking
• Logout by Device
• Security Monitoring
• Authentication Audit Logging

Device identifiers shall not expose device-sensitive information.

Session Validation Projection

Redis may hold a temporary Session Validation Projection containing only the minimum state required for high-frequency validation.

The projection shall include:

Session Identifier
Identity Identifier
Session State
Session Version
Idle Expiration Timestamp
Absolute Expiration Timestamp
Authentication Assurance Level
Authentication-Security Policy Reference
Projection Timestamp

The Redis projection shall not become authoritative.

A cache miss shall require authoritative lookup before authentication is accepted.

A stale, inconsistent or unverifiable projection shall not grant access.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 19] 19. Session Lifecycle

Canonical Session States

Session State	Meaning
ACTIVE	Session may be accepted when all validation requirements succeed
REVOKED	Session was explicitly invalidated before natural expiration
EXPIRED	Session exceeded its approved idle or absolute lifetime

Lifecycle

Session Created as ACTIVE
        ↓
ACTIVE
        ↓
REVOKED

OR

ACTIVE
        ↓
EXPIRED

Lifecycle Rules

Session creation shall occur only after successful authentication.

Idle is activity metadata and shall not be a Session state.

Refresh is a Session event and shall not be a Session state.

Identity LOCKED, SUSPENDED, DISABLED and DELETED are Identity states and shall not be Session states.

When an Identity state prevents authentication, affected Sessions shall be rejected and revoked according to the approved security workflow.

Session transitions shall:

Be backend controlled;
Be auditable;
Preserve historical records;
Increment Session Version when previously issued Access Tokens must be invalidated; and
Prevent invalid transitions.

REVOKED and EXPIRED Sessions shall never return to ACTIVE.

Concurrent Session Policy

Multiple concurrent sessions are permitted.

The maximum number of concurrent sessions shall remain centrally configurable.

Administrative users may terminate individual sessions according to approved authorization policies.

Session Timeout Policy

Authentication sessions shall support:

Idle Timeout
Absolute Timeout
Administrative Timeout

Timeout values shall remain centrally configurable.

Expired sessions shall require re-authentication.
[WEMP-M01-003C Section 20] 20. Session Storage Strategy
Objective

Session persistence shall support enterprise security, operational visibility and deterministic revocation.

PostgreSQL Responsibilities

PostgreSQL shall remain authoritative for:

Session state
Session Version
Idle and absolute expiration
Revocation state
Revocation reason
Refresh Token Family state
Refresh Token rotation history
Session history
Authentication timestamps
Device-Session metadata

Redis Responsibilities

Redis may maintain a temporary Session Validation Projection for high-frequency request validation.

Redis may also maintain:

Rate-limit counters
Temporary security locks
Short-lived activity data
Approved temporary security state

Redis shall never become the authoritative Session datastore.

Consistency Rules

Session creation, revocation, expiration and security invalidation shall be committed to PostgreSQL first.

After authoritative state changes, the Redis Session Validation Projection shall be updated or invalidated.

A Redis projection shall never extend a Session beyond authoritative expiration.

A missing projection shall not imply that a Session is valid.

An inconsistent or unverifiable projection shall not grant authentication.

If current Session state cannot be established safely, the protected request shall be denied.
C4 Persistence Reference

All persistent fields, relationships, constraints, data protection, retention and transaction boundaries for this Part are owned by the authoritative Part 7.2 and Part 7.3 Logical Data Model. This Part shall not introduce alternate schema ownership.
C6 Authentication Dependency Failure Contract

PostgreSQL unavailable or authoritative state uncertain: protected authentication, login completion, Refresh Token rotation, security mutation and Recovery execution shall fail closed and shall not report success.
Redis unavailable or stale: use authoritative PostgreSQL fallback only when capacity and safety controls permit; otherwise fail closed. Redis shall never replace authoritative C2 Session state.
Signing-key/KMS unavailable: token issuance fails closed; verification may continue only with an already trusted, unexpired verification key under the approved key policy.
Secrets Manager unavailable: an already running instance may use only securely loaded in-memory configuration within its approved lifecycle; startup or secret refresh requiring unavailable secrets fails readiness.
Uncertain PostgreSQL commit: return a retry-safe temporary/indeterminate outcome and reconcile through C5 idempotency; never repeat a security mutation blindly.
Read-only or lagging replicas shall not execute mutations or provide revocation-sensitive acceptance.
Session Version and Refresh Token state shall remain monotonic through failover.
Part 3.2 Acceptance Criteria

This section is complete when:

JWT architecture is approved.
Access Token strategy is approved.
Refresh Token strategy is approved.
Refresh Token Rotation is approved.
Refresh Token Reuse Detection is approved.
Token Revocation rules are approved.
Signing Key Management is approved.
Session Architecture is approved.
Session Lifecycle is approved.
Session Storage Strategy is approved.
No authentication middleware implementation has been introduced.
No API implementation details have been introduced.





WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform
Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 3.3 – Authentication Enforcement, Security Controls & Governance

Document ID: WEMP-M01-003D

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-003D Section 21] 21. Token Storage Strategy
Objective

This section defines how authentication tokens shall be stored across approved client platforms.

Improper token storage is prohibited.

Web Applications

Access Tokens shall:

Be stored only in approved secure mechanisms.
Never be stored in Local Storage.
Never be exposed to JavaScript when secure alternatives are available.

Refresh Tokens shall:

Be stored separately from Access Tokens.
Be protected against unauthorized client access.
Never be exposed through browser APIs.
Mobile Applications

Authentication tokens shall be stored only within the approved secure platform storage mechanisms.

Examples include:

Android Keystore
Apple Keychain

Authentication tokens shall never be stored in plain application preferences.

Storage Principles

Authentication tokens shall:

Never appear in logs
Never appear in URLs
Never appear in browser history
Never appear in screenshots generated by the application
Never be transmitted through unsecured channels
[WEMP-M01-003D Section 22] 22. Authentication Middleware
Objective

Authentication middleware provides centralized identity validation before protected resources are accessed.

Responsibilities

Authentication middleware shall:

Extract the Access Token from the approved transport;
Reject an absent or malformed token;
Validate the approved signing algorithm;
Resolve the approved signing key;
Validate the token signature;
Validate issuer and audience;
Validate expiration and not-before claims;
Validate the JWT Identifier;
Validate the Identity Identifier;
Validate the Session Identifier;
Validate the sessionVersion claim;
Load the current Session Validation Projection or authoritative Session state;
Validate that the Session state is ACTIVE;
Validate idle and absolute Session expiration;
Compare token sessionVersion with the current Session Version;
Validate the current Identity state;
Validate the applicable authentication-security policy;
Establish the Authenticated Identity Context only after all checks succeed.

Canonical Request Authentication Workflow

Protected Request
        ↓
JWT Cryptographic Validation
        ↓
JWT Claim Validation
        ↓
Session Identifier Resolution
        ↓
Current Session State and Version Resolution
        ↓
Identity State Validation
        ↓
Authentication Assurance and Security-Policy Validation
        ↓
Authenticated Identity Context Established

Failure of any required check shall reject the request.

A valid JWT signature shall never bypass Session or Identity validation.
Authentication middleware shall not evaluate business permissions.

Authorization remains the responsibility of Module 02.

Authenticated Identity Context

Upon successful authentication, the Authentication Service shall establish an Authenticated Identity Context containing:

• Identity Identifier
• Session Identifier
• Authentication Timestamp
• Authentication Method
• Authentication Assurance Level
• Current MFA State
• Device-Session Identifier
• Correlation Identifier

The context shall not contain business roles, business permissions, authorization policies, or authorization decisions.

The Authenticated Identity Context shall describe authentication only.

Module 02 shall resolve roles and permissions from its own authoritative authorization state using the Identity Identifier.

Customer and Seller profile information shall be resolved from their owning modules and shall not be inferred from the Authenticated Identity Context.
Module 02 and future platform modules shall consume the Authenticated Identity Context through approved contracts rather than re-authenticating the identity or coupling directly to the Module 01 database.

C7 Cryptographic Validation Order

Authentication middleware shall validate:

Token size, encoding and structure
Protected header allowlist
Exact ES256 algorithm
typ=JWT
Mandatory trusted kid
Signing-key lifecycle and compromise status
Cryptographic signature
Exact environment issuer and audience
Token type and temporal claims
Approved C2 authentication-only claims
Authoritative Session state and Session Version
Authentication Assurance and route requirements

The Authenticated Identity Context shall be created only after every applicable validation succeeds.
[WEMP-M01-003D Section 23] 23. Route Protection
Objective

Protected resources shall require successful authentication before access.

Route Categories
Public

Examples:

Login
Registration
Password Reset
Health Endpoints
Authenticated

Accessible only to authenticated identities.

Examples:

Customer Dashboard
Seller Dashboard
Profile
Orders
Administrative

Accessible only after successful authentication and authorization.

Examples:

Admin Portal
Super Admin Portal
System Configuration

Authentication alone shall never grant administrative access.

[WEMP-M01-003D Section 24] 24. Logout Strategy
Objective

Logout shall authoritatively revoke the current Session without affecting unrelated Sessions.

Canonical Logout Workflow

Authenticated Session
        ↓
Logout Request
        ↓
Validate Current Session and Request Authority
        ↓
Begin Authoritative Revocation Transaction
        ↓
Set Current Session to REVOKED
        ↓
Increment Current Session Version
        ↓
Revoke Current Refresh Token Family
        ↓
Commit Transaction
        ↓
Invalidate or Update Session Validation Projection
        ↓
Generate Audit Event
        ↓
Return Success

Logout shall invalidate:

All future refresh operations for the current Session;
All subsequent protected requests using an earlier Session Version; and
All Refresh Tokens belonging to the current Session family.

Logout shall not wait for the Access Token to expire before rejecting subsequent requests.

Unrelated Sessions shall remain unchanged.
[WEMP-M01-003D Section 25] 25. Logout All Devices
Objective

The platform shall support authoritative revocation of every current Module 01 Session belonging to an Identity.

Canonical Logout-All Workflow

Logout All Devices Requested
        ↓
Validate Identity and Request Authority
        ↓
Locate All ACTIVE Sessions for the Identity
        ↓
Begin Authoritative Revocation Transaction
        ↓
Set Every Applicable Session to REVOKED
        ↓
Increment Every Applicable Session Version
        ↓
Revoke Every Applicable Refresh Token Family
        ↓
Commit Transaction
        ↓
Invalidate or Update Every Session Validation Projection
        ↓
Generate Audit Event
        ↓
Return Success

Logout All Devices shall invalidate every current Module 01 Session belonging to the Identity, including the Session that initiated the request.

After committed logout-all revocation:

No previous Refresh Token shall be accepted.
No subsequent protected request using an earlier Session Version shall be accepted.
Fresh authentication shall be required.

Administrative identities may revoke sessions according to approved authorization policies.
[WEMP-M01-003D Section 26] 26. Authentication Security Controls
Objective

Authentication shall implement layered security controls to reduce identity-related risks.

Required Controls

The authentication service shall support:

Rate Limiting
Account Lockout
Enumeration Protection
Brute Force Detection
Replay Protection
Refresh Token Rotation
Token Reuse Detection
Secure Session Management
Credential Revocation
Audit Logging

Future modules may introduce additional adaptive authentication controls.

[WEMP-M01-003D Section 27] 27. Authentication Audit Logging
Objective

Every security-sensitive authentication event shall be recorded.

Logged Events

The authentication platform shall generate audit records for:

Login Success
Login Failure
Logout
Logout All Devices
Password Change
Password Reset
Session Creation
Session Revocation
Token Refresh
Token Reuse Detection
SessionVersionIncremented
SessionValidationFailed
SessionProjectionInvalidated
RefreshTokenConsumed
RefreshTokenRotated
RefreshTokenReuseDetected
RefreshTokenFamilyRevoked
LogoutCommitted
LogoutAllCommitted
Administrative Authentication Events
Security Lockouts
Audit Record Fields

Every authentication audit record shall include:

Timestamp
Identity Identifier
Session Identifier
Correlation Identifier
Event Type
Source IP Address
User Agent
Device Identifier

Authentication audit records shall be immutable.

Authentication Event Categories

Authentication events shall be classified as:

• Informational
• Warning
• Critical
• Security Incident

Event categories shall support monitoring, alerting, incident response, and security analytics.

[WEMP-M01-003D Section 28] 28. Cross-Module Outputs
Objective

Module 01 – Part 3 establishes the authentication artifacts required by future platform modules.

Produces
Authenticated Identity Context
Access Tokens
Refresh Token Families
Authentication Sessions
Authentication Audit Records
Revocation Events
Authentication Security Events
Consumed By

The outputs of Part 3 shall be consumed by:

Module 01 – Part 4

Module 04 – Product Catalog
Module 05 – Inventory Management
Module 06 – Customer Management
Module 07 – Shopping Cart
Module 08 – Orders
Module 09 – Payments
Module 10 – Shipping & Logistics

Future modules shall not implement independent authentication mechanisms.

[WEMP-M01-003D Section 29] 29. Part 3 Acceptance Criteria

Module 01 – Part 3 shall be considered complete when:

Authentication architecture is approved.
Credential architecture is approved.
Password architecture is approved.
JWT architecture is approved.
Access Token strategy is approved.
Refresh Token strategy is approved.
Session architecture is approved.
Route protection model is approved.

Cross-module dependencies are approved.

No implementation-specific source code shall be included in this specification.

[WEMP-M01-003D Section 30] 30. Part 3 Final Approval
Module Completion Statement

Module 01 – Part 3 establishes the complete authentication architecture for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

The following areas are approved:

Authentication Principles
Hybrid Authentication Architecture

Session Architecture

Part 3 serves as the authoritative authentication architecture for all subsequent implementation activities.

No authentication implementation shall begin until Module 01 Parts 4–10 have been completed, reviewed, and formally approved.

Part 4.1 – Verification Foundation

Document ID: WEMP-M01-004A
Version: 1.0
Status: Draft
Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-004A Section 1] 1. Objective
Purpose

Module 01 – Part 4 defines the verification and multi-factor authentication architecture for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

This part establishes how the platform shall:

Verify ownership of email addresses
Verify ownership of mobile numbers
Generate and validate one-time passwords
Protect OTP workflows from abuse
Enroll privileged identities in multi-factor authentication
Verify additional authentication factors
Recover access when an MFA factor becomes unavailable
Record verification and MFA security events
Support future adaptive authentication controls

The verification architecture shall support:

Customer Web Portal
Seller Web Portal
Admin Web Portal
Super Admin Web Portal
Customer Mobile Application
Seller Mobile Application
Backend APIs

Verification shall establish ownership or control of an approved communication channel or authentication factor.

Verification shall not grant business permissions.

Authorization shall remain governed by Module 02 – Role and Permission Management.

[WEMP-M01-004A Section 2] 2. Scope
Included

Module 01 – Part 4 includes:

Verification architecture
Email verification
Mobile number verification
Email OTP
SMS OTP
OTP generation
OTP delivery
OTP validation
OTP expiration
OTP resend controls
OTP attempt limits
OTP replay protection
Verification state management
Verification lifecycle
Verification failure handling
Multi-factor authentication architecture
MFA enrollment
MFA verification
MFA recovery codes
Trusted-device foundation
Verification rate limiting
Verification audit logging
Verification notifications
Standardized verification errors
Future adaptive verification foundation
Excluded

The following items are outside Part 4:

Password authentication
Password hashing
JWT issuance
Refresh-token rotation
General session architecture
Role and permission assignment
Seller business verification
GST verification
PAN verification
Bank-account verification
KYC processing
Product management
Inventory
Orders
Payments
Shipping
Production SMS-provider commercial configuration
Production email-provider commercial configuration
Social login
Passkeys
Hardware security keys
Enterprise SSO
Biometric identity verification

Seller KYC, GST, PAN, bank, and business verification shall be defined in the Seller Management module.

Part 4 verifies authentication factors and communication channels only.

[WEMP-M01-004A Section 3] 3. Verification Principles

The verification platform shall follow the principles below.

3.1 Backend Authority

Verification decisions shall be made exclusively by the backend.

Client applications shall not:

Mark an email as verified
Mark a mobile number as verified
Approve an OTP
Activate MFA
Generate recovery codes
Modify verification state directly

Client-side validation shall improve usability only.

3.2 Purpose-Bound Verification

Every verification request shall have one approved purpose.

Examples include:

Registration email verification
Registration mobile verification
Password-reset verification
Contact-change verification
Admin invitation verification
MFA verification
Sensitive-operation confirmation

An OTP or verification challenge created for one purpose shall not be accepted for another purpose.

3.3 Identity Binding

Every verification challenge shall be bound to:

One identity or pending registration
One destination
One verification purpose
One verification channel
One expiration time

Verification challenges shall not be transferable between identities.

3.4 Short-Lived Verification

OTPs and verification challenges shall remain valid only for a limited, centrally configurable duration.

Expired challenges shall never be reactivated.

A new challenge shall be generated when verification is attempted after expiration.

3.5 Single Use

Every successfully used OTP, recovery code, or verification challenge shall become invalid immediately.

Successful verification shall prevent replay of the same challenge.

3.6 Minimal Disclosure

Verification responses shall not expose whether an unrelated identity exists.

Public responses shall not reveal:

Registered email addresses
Registered mobile numbers
OTP values
MFA secrets
Recovery codes
Internal verification counters
Internal risk scores
Provider credentials
Administrative account details
3.7 Rate Limiting

Verification requests, OTP generation, OTP validation, and resend operations shall be rate limited.

Limits shall be enforceable using combinations of:

Identity identifier
Pending-registration identifier
Email address
Mobile number
IP address
Device identifier
Verification purpose

Rate-limit values shall remain centrally configurable.

3.8 Auditability

Security-sensitive verification actions shall generate audit records.

Audit events shall include:

Verification requested
Delivery initiated
Delivery failed
Verification succeeded
Verification failed
OTP expired
OTP resend requested
Attempt limit exceeded
MFA enrolled
MFA challenged
MFA succeeded
MFA failed
Recovery code used
Trusted device added
Trusted device revoked

OTP values, MFA secrets, and raw recovery codes shall never appear in audit logs.

3.9 Secure Failure

When verification cannot be completed safely, the system shall deny the operation.

Provider failures, Redis failures, unexpected exceptions, or validation uncertainty shall not result in automatic verification.

3.10 Channel Independence

Email verification and mobile verification shall be tracked independently.

Verification of one channel shall not automatically verify another channel.

Examples:

Verified email does not imply verified mobile.
Verified mobile does not imply verified email.
MFA enrollment does not imply verification of an unrelated contact method.
[WEMP-M01-004A Section 4] 4. Enterprise Verification Architecture
Objective

The WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform shall use the approved Enterprise Adaptive Verification Architecture defined by ADR-M01-002.

This architecture combines:

Email verification
SMS verification
OTP controls
Mandatory MFA for privileged identities
Recovery mechanisms
Trusted-device support
Future risk-based verification
Architecture Decision Reference

ADR: ADR-M01-002
Decision: Enterprise Adaptive Verification Architecture
Status: Approved

All Part 4 implementation shall comply with ADR-M01-002.
C1 Ownership Clarification

ADR-M01-002 shall select MFA requirements from Module 01 authentication-security classification rather than Module 02 roles or business profiles.

Admin and Super Admin roles shall not constitute authentication assurance. AAL2 requires successful MFA verification for the applicable authentication or step-up event.

Architecture Overview
Web and Mobile Clients
          |
          v
Authentication Service
          |
          v
Verification Orchestrator
     |             |
     v             v
OTP Service     MFA Service
     |             |
     v             v
Email/SMS       Authenticator
Providers       Verification
     |
     v
Redis Temporary Security State
     |
     v
PostgreSQL Authoritative Records
     |
     v
Audit and Notification Services
Authentication Service

The Authentication Service shall:

Receive verification requests
Validate identity and purpose
Authorize verification initiation
Coordinate verification completion
Update trusted verification state
Prevent unauthorized state transitions
Verification Orchestrator

The Verification Orchestrator shall:

Select the approved verification method
Create verification challenges
Enforce expiration
Enforce resend limits
Enforce attempt limits
Validate submitted responses
Prevent replay
Generate verification events
OTP Service

The OTP Service shall:

Generate secure OTP challenges
Bind OTPs to identity, purpose, channel, and destination
Store only approved protected representations
Validate OTP submissions
Expire used or invalid challenges
Prevent challenge reuse
MFA Service

The MFA Service shall:

Support MFA enrollment
Validate MFA challenges
Manage MFA activation state
Manage recovery codes
Support MFA reset and recovery workflows
Enforce privileged-account MFA requirements
PostgreSQL Responsibilities

PostgreSQL shall remain the authoritative datastore for:

Email verification timestamps
Mobile verification timestamps
MFA enrollment state
MFA method metadata
Recovery-code state
Trusted-device records
Verification history
Verification audit references
Redis Responsibilities

Redis shall support temporary security state, including:

Active OTP challenges
OTP expiration
Attempt counters
Resend counters
Temporary verification locks
Short-lived MFA challenge state
Rate-limit counters

Redis shall not become the authoritative source of permanent verification status.

Notification Providers

Email and SMS providers shall deliver verification messages.

Providers shall not decide whether an identity is verified.

Successful provider delivery does not equal successful verification.

Only the backend verification service may update verification state.

[WEMP-M01-004A Section 5] 5. Verification Methods
Objective

This section defines the approved verification methods for Phase 1 and identifies methods reserved for future phases.

Approved Phase 1 Methods
Verification Method	Purpose	Status
Email OTP	Email ownership verification	Approved
SMS OTP	Mobile ownership verification	Approved
Authenticator Application	Privileged-account MFA	Approved
Recovery Codes	MFA account recovery	Approved
Trusted Device	Reduce repeated challenges under approved policy	Approved foundation
Deferred Methods
Verification Method	Status
Passkeys	Deferred
Hardware Security Keys	Deferred
Push Authentication	Deferred
Magic Links	Deferred
Voice OTP	Deferred
Social Identity Verification	Deferred
Enterprise SSO Verification	Deferred
Biometric Verification	Deferred

Deferred methods shall not be implemented without architecture approval.

Email OTP

Email OTP shall be used for:

Email registration verification
Admin invitation verification
Email contact-change confirmation
Approved password-recovery workflows
Additional verification challenges where permitted

Email OTP shall prove control of an email inbox only.

It shall not prove legal identity.

SMS OTP

SMS OTP shall be used for:

Mobile registration verification
Mobile contact-change confirmation
Approved account-recovery workflows
Additional verification challenges where permitted

SMS OTP shall prove temporary control of a mobile number only.

It shall not prove legal ownership of the subscriber account.

Authenticator Application

Time-based authenticator applications shall be the approved primary MFA method for Admin and Super Admin identities in Phase 1.

MFA shall be mandatory for:

Admin
Super Admin

STANDARD_AUTHENTICATION sessions may use optional MFA until an approved policy requires MFA or step-up authentication.

Recovery Codes

Recovery codes shall provide controlled access recovery when the primary MFA method is unavailable.

Recovery codes shall be:

Cryptographically secure
Single-use
Protected in storage
Regenerable only through an authenticated security workflow
Invalidated when replaced
Trusted Devices

Trusted-device capability shall support approved reduction of repeated MFA prompts.

Trusted-device status shall:

Be associated with one identity
Be associated with one device record
Have an expiration time
Be revocable
Be auditable
Never bypass mandatory high-risk verification where required

Trusted-device status shall not permanently disable MFA.

C3 Recovery Assurance Clarification

ADR-M01-002 shall distinguish Recovery Assurance from Authentication Assurance. Recovery evidence, including a Recovery code, shall not automatically establish AAL2. AAL2 shall require fresh authentication using a qualifying approved MFA factor after Recovery completion.
Part 4.1 Acceptance Criteria

Part 4.1 is complete when:

Scope and exclusions are approved.

Verification architecture is approved.
PostgreSQL and Redis responsibilities are approved.
Verification methods are approved.
Email OTP is approved.
SMS OTP is approved.
Authenticator-app MFA is approved.
Recovery-code architecture is approved in principle.
Trusted-device architecture is approved in principle.
Deferred verification methods are documented.
No implementation source code has been introduced.

Part 4.2 – OTP Security

Document ID: WEMP-M01-004B

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-004B Section 6] 6. OTP Architecture
Objective

This section defines the enterprise One-Time Password (OTP) architecture for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

OTPs shall provide short-lived verification challenges for approved verification workflows.

OTP architecture shall ensure:

Confidentiality
Integrity
Replay resistance
Expiration
Auditability
Rate limiting
Secure validation

OTPs shall never function as authentication credentials.

They exist solely to verify ownership of an approved communication channel or authentication factor.

OTP Principles

The OTP service shall follow these principles:

Purpose-specific
Single-use
Time-limited
Identity-bound
Channel-bound
Destination-bound
Replay resistant
Audit logged

OTPs shall never be reusable.

OTP Components

The OTP architecture consists of:

OTP Generator
OTP Store
OTP Validator
OTP Expiration Service
OTP Rate Limiter
OTP Audit Logger
Notification Dispatcher
OTP Binding

Every OTP shall be bound to:

Identity Identifier
Verification Purpose
Verification Method
Delivery Destination
Creation Timestamp
Expiration Timestamp

OTP values shall never be transferable.

[WEMP-M01-004B Section 7] 7. OTP Generation Policy
Objective

OTP generation shall produce cryptographically secure verification challenges.

Generation Principles

Every OTP shall be:

Cryptographically secure
Random
Non-sequential
Non-predictable
Single-use

OTP generation shall never rely upon:

System time
Sequential counters
Identity identifiers
Email addresses
Mobile numbers
OTP Length

OTP length shall remain centrally configurable.

Approved defaults may vary by verification method.

Length values shall never be hardcoded into business modules.

Generation Authority

Only the Verification Service may generate OTPs.

Clients shall never generate verification OTPs.

OTP Storage

OTP values shall never be stored in plaintext.

Only approved protected representations shall be stored.

OTP storage shall support:

Expiration
Replay detection
Validation
Audit correlation
[WEMP-M01-004B Section 8] 8. OTP Delivery Policy
Objective

The Verification Service shall coordinate OTP delivery through approved notification providers.

Approved Channels

Phase 1 supports:

Email
SMS

Future channels:

Push Notification
Voice
Authenticator App Challenge
Delivery Principles

OTP delivery shall:

Use encrypted transport
Be independently auditable
Support retries
Detect provider failures

Provider delivery shall not imply successful verification.

Delivery Failure

Provider failures shall:

Generate audit events
Support retry policies
Never automatically verify an identity
[WEMP-M01-004B Section 9] 9. OTP Validation Policy
Objective

OTP validation shall verify that the submitted OTP matches the active verification challenge.

Validation Workflow
Receive OTP
      ↓
Locate Active Challenge
      ↓
Validate Purpose
      ↓
Validate Destination
      ↓
Validate Identity
      ↓
Validate Expiration
      ↓
Validate Attempt Count
      ↓
Validate OTP
      ↓
Verification Decision
Successful Validation

Successful validation shall:

Complete verification
Invalidate the OTP
Record audit events
Update verification state
Failed Validation

Failed validation shall:

Increment failure counters
Generate audit records
Preserve replay protection
Return standardized verification errors

OTP values shall never be revealed.

[WEMP-M01-004B Section 10] 10. OTP Expiration & Retry Policy
Objective

OTP validity shall remain strictly time-limited.

OTP Expiration

Every OTP shall expire after a centrally configurable duration.

Expired OTPs:

Cannot be validated
Cannot be reactivated
Cannot be reused

Expired OTPs shall require generation of a new verification challenge.

Retry Policy

OTP validation attempts shall remain centrally configurable.

Attempt limits shall be enforced per:

Identity
Destination
Verification Purpose
Device
IP Address
Attempt Limit Exceeded

When the maximum attempt limit is reached:

OTP becomes invalid
Verification challenge expires
Audit event generated
Security counters updated

Future verification requires a newly generated OTP.

Replay Protection

Previously used OTPs shall never be accepted.

Replay attempts shall:

Generate security audit events
Update security monitoring
Support future incident response policies
Rate Limiting

OTP generation, resend requests, and validation shall be independently rate limited.

Limits shall remain centrally configurable.

Part 4.2 Acceptance Criteria

Part 4.2 is complete when:

OTP architecture is approved.
OTP generation policy is approved.
OTP delivery policy is approved.
OTP validation policy is approved.
OTP expiration policy is approved.
OTP retry policy is approved.
Replay protection is approved.
OTP storage principles are approved.
OTP audit requirements are approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.
