Part 4.3 – Verification Channels & Verification Lifecycle

Document ID: WEMP-M01-004C

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-004C Section 11] 11. Email Verification
Objective

Email verification establishes ownership of an email address associated with an identity.

Successful verification confirms that the identity has control of the specified email inbox.

Email verification shall not establish legal identity or business approval.

Approved Uses

Email verification may be required for:

Customer registration
Seller registration
Admin invitation acceptance
Email address changes
Password recovery
Sensitive account operations
Security confirmation requests
Verification Workflow
Identity Created
        ↓
Generate Email OTP
        ↓
Send Verification Email
        ↓
Identity Submits OTP
        ↓
OTP Validation
        ↓
Email Verified
Email Verification Rules

Email verification shall:

Be identity-bound
Be destination-bound
Be single-use
Be time-limited
Be replay-resistant
Generate audit records

Verification state shall only be updated after successful backend validation.

Email Verification Status

Email verification may exist in one of the following states:

Pending
Verified
Expired
Failed
Cancelled

Only the Verified state permits email-dependent operations.

[WEMP-M01-004C Section 12] 12. SMS Verification
Objective

SMS verification establishes temporary control of a mobile number.

Successful SMS verification shall confirm that the identity can receive verification messages at the registered mobile number.

SMS verification does not establish subscriber ownership or legal identity.

Approved Uses

SMS verification may be required for:

Mobile registration
Mobile number changes
Password recovery
Additional verification challenges
Sensitive account operations
Verification Workflow
Identity Created
        ↓
Generate SMS OTP
        ↓
Send SMS
        ↓
Identity Submits OTP
        ↓
OTP Validation
        ↓
Mobile Verified
SMS Verification Rules

SMS verification shall:

Be identity-bound
Be destination-bound
Be single-use
Be time-limited
Support retry policies
Support audit logging
SMS Verification Status

SMS verification shall support:

Pending
Verified
Expired
Failed
Cancelled

Verification status shall remain independent of email verification.

[WEMP-M01-004C Section 13] 13. Verification State Machine
Objective

Verification state transitions shall follow a controlled lifecycle.

Verification states shall not be modified directly by client applications.

Approved State Model
Created
      ↓
Pending
      ↓
Challenge Issued
      ↓
Verified

OR

Expired

OR

Failed

OR

Cancelled

State transitions shall occur only through approved backend workflows.

State Transition Rules

Verification state transitions shall:

Be auditable
Preserve historical records
Prevent invalid transitions
Support recovery workflows where approved

A completed verification shall never return to the Pending state.

[WEMP-M01-004C Section 14] 14. Verification Lifecycle
Objective

Verification shall remain a controlled lifecycle rather than a single event.

Lifecycle
Verification Requested
          ↓
Challenge Generated
          ↓
Challenge Delivered
          ↓
Challenge Submitted
          ↓
Challenge Validated
          ↓
Verification Completed
Lifecycle Principles

The verification lifecycle shall:

Record each major event
Prevent duplicate completion
Support expiration
Support cancellation
Support replay protection

Every lifecycle transition shall generate an auditable event.

[WEMP-M01-004C Section 15] 15. Verification Failure Handling
Objective

Verification failures shall be handled securely without exposing internal verification information.

Failure Types

Verification may fail because of:

Invalid OTP
Expired OTP
Maximum attempts exceeded
Rate limiting
Provider failure
Identity mismatch
Destination mismatch
Replay attempt
Failure Response

Verification failures shall:

Generate audit events
Preserve security counters
Prevent unauthorized verification
Return standardized error responses

Internal validation details shall never be disclosed to clients.

Lockout Handling

Repeated verification failures may trigger:

Temporary verification lock
Temporary resend restriction
Additional verification requirements
Future adaptive authentication policies

Lockout thresholds shall remain centrally configurable.

Recovery

After a verification failure, the platform may allow:

OTP regeneration
New verification challenge
Approved recovery workflow

Recovery shall not bypass verification requirements.

Part 4.3 Acceptance Criteria

Part 4.3 is complete when:

Email verification architecture is approved.
SMS verification architecture is approved.
Verification state machine is approved.
Verification lifecycle is approved.
Verification failure handling is approved.
Verification status transitions are approved.
Recovery principles are approved.
No implementation-specific code has been introduced.


WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform
Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 4.4 – Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA)

Document ID: WEMP-M01-004D

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-004D Section 16] 16. Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) Architecture
Objective

This section defines the enterprise Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) architecture for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

MFA provides an additional layer of identity verification beyond the primary authentication credential.

The MFA architecture shall strengthen account security by requiring an additional authentication factor before granting access to protected resources.

MFA Principles

The MFA service shall comply with the following principles:

Independent authentication factors
Backend-controlled verification
Secure enrollment
Replay resistance
Recovery support
Auditability
Revocability

MFA shall supplement password authentication and shall never replace identity verification.

Approved Authentication Factors

Phase 1 supports:

Factor	Status
Password	Approved
Authenticator Application (TOTP)	Approved
Recovery Codes	Approved

Future authentication factors:

Factor	Status
Passkeys	Deferred
Hardware Security Keys	Deferred
Push Authentication	Deferred
Biometric Authentication	Deferred
MFA Policy Requirements

Module 01 shall determine mandatory MFA from the applicable authentication-security classification, not from a business profile or Module 02 role embedded in the Identity.

Authentication-Security Classification	MFA Requirement
STANDARD_AUTHENTICATION	Optional unless an approved security policy requires step-up authentication
PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION	Mandatory
SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION	Mandatory

Customer and Seller profiles shall remain outside the Module 01 Identity model and shall not determine Module 01 authentication policy directly.

Module 02 roles shall not be stored in Module 01.

When Module 02 assigns or revokes a privileged role, the coordinated cross-module workflow shall ensure that the corresponding Module 01 authentication-security classification remains consistent before privileged access is permitted.

Successful MFA verification for the current session shall establish AAL2.

MFA enrollment without successful verification shall not establish AAL2.

Future policies may require MFA or step-up authentication for STANDARD_AUTHENTICATION sessions.

[WEMP-M01-004D Section 17] 17. MFA Enrollment
Objective

Enrollment establishes an identity's association with an approved MFA method.

Enrollment shall occur only after successful primary authentication.

Enrollment Workflow
Authenticated Identity
        ↓
Request MFA Enrollment
        ↓
Generate MFA Secret
        ↓
Display QR Code / Setup Information
        ↓
Authenticator App Configured
        ↓
Verification Challenge
        ↓
Enrollment Confirmed
        ↓
MFA Activated
Enrollment Rules

Enrollment shall:

Require an authenticated session
Require identity verification
Require successful MFA verification before activation
Generate audit records

Incomplete enrollment shall not activate MFA.

[WEMP-M01-004D Section 18] 18. MFA Verification Flow
Objective

The MFA verification workflow validates the second authentication factor after successful primary authentication.

Verification Workflow
Password Authentication
        ↓
MFA Required
        ↓
Submit MFA Code
        ↓
Validate MFA Challenge
        ↓
Authentication Complete
Successful Verification

Successful MFA verification shall:

Complete authentication
Establish the authenticated session
Generate audit events
Failed Verification

Failed MFA verification shall:

Increment security counters
Generate audit events
Return standardized verification errors

MFA secrets shall never be exposed.

Replay Protection

Previously accepted MFA verification codes shall never be accepted again.

Replay attempts shall generate security audit events.

[WEMP-M01-004D Section 19] 19. Recovery Codes
Objective

Recovery codes provide controlled evidence for a purpose-bound Recovery workflow when the primary MFA device is unavailable.

Recovery codes are emergency Recovery factors. They are not Module 02 roles or permissions and do not independently create an ordinary authenticated Session.

Recovery Code Principles

Recovery codes shall be:

Cryptographically secure
Random
Single-use
Identity-bound
Protected in storage
Regenerable only through an approved authenticated or Recovery workflow
Excluded from logs and telemetry

Recovery Code Lifecycle
Generated
      ↓
Displayed Once
      ↓
Securely Stored by Identity Owner
      ↓
Presented to Purpose-Bound Recovery Workflow
      ↓
Atomically Consumed
      ↓
Invalidated

Recovery codes shall never be reusable.

Recovery Assurance Rule

Successful Recovery-code validation contributes only the Recovery evidence explicitly permitted by the Recovery policy matrix.

Recovery-code validation shall not establish AAL2 automatically.

An Identity shall establish AAL2 only after fresh authentication with a qualifying approved MFA factor.

Regeneration

Generating new Recovery codes shall invalidate all previously unused Recovery codes.

Recovery-code regeneration shall require either:

An ordinary authenticated Session satisfying the approved Authentication Assurance policy; or
An approved purpose-bound Recovery Session whose permitted operation explicitly includes Recovery-code regeneration.

A Recovery Session used for regeneration shall remain restricted and shall not become an ordinary authenticated Session automatically.

Privileged Recovery-Code Use

Recovery-code use by an Identity subject to PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION or SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION shall apply the privileged policy row in Part 5.5, revoke or restrict prior authentication state as required, and require fresh MFA enrollment or verification before privileged authentication resumes.
[WEMP-M01-004D Section 20] 20. Trusted Devices
Objective

Trusted devices reduce repeated MFA challenges for approved devices while maintaining enterprise security.

Trusted Device Principles

Trusted devices shall:

Be identity-bound
Be device-bound
Be revocable
Be auditable
Have configurable expiration

Trusted devices shall never permanently bypass MFA.

Trusted Device Workflow
Successful MFA
        ↓
Trust Device Requested
        ↓
Backend Validation
        ↓
Trusted Device Created
        ↓
Future Logins Evaluated
Revocation

Trusted devices may be revoked by:

Identity Owner
Administrator (where authorized)
Security Policy
Device Expiration

Revoked trusted devices shall require MFA during the next authentication.

Trusted Device Metadata

Each trusted device record shall maintain:

Device Identifier
Identity Identifier
Device Name (if available)
Platform
Browser or Application
Creation Timestamp
Expiration Timestamp
Last Authentication Timestamp
Trust Status

Trusted device metadata shall not include sensitive device information beyond what is necessary for security and session management.

C7 Verification and MFA Cryptographic Storage

OTP and Recovery Code raw values shall never be persisted; only purpose-bound protected digests are stored and compared safely.
MFA shared secrets require C7 authenticated envelope encryption with encryption context bound to environment, Module 01, Identity, factor type and factor identifier.
Trusted Device fingerprints use a dedicated protected representation and shall not reuse an OTP, Recovery, CSRF or encryption key.
Every ciphertext/digest record shall carry the approved cryptographic policy/key version metadata required by C4.
Cryptographic failure or uncertainty shall never verify an OTP, Recovery Code, MFA factor or Trusted Device.
Part 4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Part 4.4 is complete when:

MFA architecture is approved.
MFA enrollment workflow is approved.
MFA verification workflow is approved.
Recovery code architecture is approved.
Trusted device architecture is approved.
MFA requirements for authentication-security classifications are approved.
Replay protection requirements are approved.
Trusted device lifecycle is approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.


WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform
Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 4.5 – Enterprise Verification Security

Document ID: WEMP-M01-004E

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-004E Section 21] 21. Adaptive Verification (Future)
Objective

Adaptive Verification provides a framework for dynamically increasing verification requirements based on assessed authentication risk.

This capability is reserved for future implementation and establishes the architectural foundation for risk-based authentication.

Adaptive Verification Principles

Adaptive Verification shall:

Evaluate authentication risk
Increase verification requirements when necessary
Never reduce mandatory verification requirements
Operate independently of business authorization
Generate audit records
Remain centrally configurable
Future Risk Signals

Future implementations may evaluate:

New device detection
Unusual geographic location
Impossible travel detection
IP reputation
Anonymous proxy or VPN detection
High-risk login behavior
Device fingerprint changes
Multiple failed authentication attempts
Administrative policy requirements

Adaptive verification shall only increase security requirements and shall never bypass mandatory verification.

[WEMP-M01-004E Section 22] 22. Verification Rate Limiting
Objective

Verification endpoints shall implement rate limiting to prevent abuse, automated attacks, and denial-of-service attempts.

Rate Limiting Scope

Rate limiting shall apply independently to:

OTP generation
OTP validation
OTP resend requests
Email verification
SMS verification
MFA verification
Recovery code validation
Rate Limiting Dimensions

Limits may be enforced using combinations of:

Identity Identifier
Email Address
Mobile Number
Device Identifier
IP Address
Session Identifier
Verification Purpose

All limits shall remain centrally configurable.

Rate Limit Response

When limits are exceeded:

Verification requests shall be rejected.
Security counters shall be updated.
Audit events shall be generated.
Temporary restrictions may be applied.

Rate limiting shall not permanently disable an identity.

[WEMP-M01-004E Section 23] 23. Verification Security Controls
Objective

Verification services shall implement layered security controls to protect against unauthorized verification attempts.

Required Controls

Verification services shall support:

Replay protection
Enumeration protection
Brute-force detection
Rate limiting
Attempt tracking
Secure OTP storage
Secure MFA secret storage
Trusted device validation
Session validation
Audit logging
Secure Failure

When verification cannot be completed safely:

Verification shall fail securely.
Verification state shall not change.
Temporary security counters shall be updated.
Audit records shall be generated.

Unexpected errors shall never result in automatic verification.

[WEMP-M01-004E Section 24] 24. Verification Audit Logging
Objective

All security-sensitive verification events shall be recorded to support monitoring, investigations, and compliance.

Logged Events

Verification audit records shall include:

Verification Requested
OTP Generated
OTP Delivered
OTP Delivery Failed
OTP Validation Success
OTP Validation Failure
OTP Expired
OTP Resent
Email Verified
Mobile Verified
MFA Enrollment
MFA Verification Success
MFA Verification Failure
Recovery Code Used
Trusted Device Added
Trusted Device Removed
Verification Lock Applied
Verification Lock Removed
Audit Record Fields

Every verification audit record shall include:

Timestamp
Identity Identifier
Session Identifier
Correlation Identifier
Event Type
Source IP Address
User Agent
Device Identifier (where available)

Verification audit records shall be immutable.

Sensitive verification values shall never be stored in audit logs.

Event Classification

Verification events shall be classified as:

Informational
Warning
Critical
Security Incident

Classification supports monitoring, alerting, and future SIEM integration.

[WEMP-M01-004E Section 25] 25. Verification Notifications
Objective

Verification notifications provide communication to identities regarding verification and security-related events.

Notification Types

The platform may notify identities of:

Email verification requests
Mobile verification requests
Successful verification
Failed verification
MFA enrollment
MFA removal
Recovery code regeneration
Trusted device registration
Trusted device removal
Suspicious verification activity
Notification Principles

Notifications shall:

Be generated by backend services.
Contain only necessary information.
Avoid exposing sensitive security details.
Be independently auditable.

Notifications shall never include:

OTP values after expiration
Recovery codes
MFA secrets
Passwords
Authentication tokens
Delivery

Notifications may be delivered through:

Email
SMS
Mobile Push Notifications (Future)
In-App Notifications (Future)

Delivery mechanisms shall remain configurable.

C6 Verification Provider Failure Matrix

Provider State	Required Behavior
Available	Process through approved workflow
Timeout before acceptance known	Record UNCERTAIN; do not claim delivery; reconcile before retry where provider semantics permit
Explicit rejection	Record FAILED; expose enumeration-safe temporary outcome
Accepted by provider	Record ACCEPTED; do not claim end-user delivery
Confirmed delivery callback	Record DELIVERED idempotently where provider supports confirmation
Duplicate callback	Deduplicate by provider message/event identifier
Provider outage	Record/return VERIFICATION_DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE; preserve challenge expiry and security counters
Retry exhausted	Record FAILED; alert and reconcile

Canonical delivery states are REQUESTED, ACCEPTED, DELIVERED where confirmed, FAILED, UNCERTAIN and EXPIRED.

Retry shall not extend OTP expiry or regenerate evidence silently. A new OTP requires a new approved challenge. Channel fallback requires explicit workflow approval and shall preserve C3 channel independence and enumeration protection.
Part 4.5 Acceptance Criteria

Part 4.5 is complete when:

Adaptive Verification architecture is approved.
Verification Rate Limiting is approved.
Verification Security Controls are approved.
Verification Audit Logging is approved.
Verification Notifications are approved.
Event classification is approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

[WEMP-M01-004F Section 26] 26. Verification Error Model
Objective

The Verification Error Model establishes standardized error handling for all verification workflows.

Verification errors shall provide sufficient information for legitimate clients while preventing disclosure of sensitive internal implementation details.

Error Handling Principles

Verification errors shall:

Be standardized across all verification services
Avoid exposing sensitive identity information
Prevent user enumeration
Support localization
Be auditable
Remain implementation-independent
Standard Error Categories

Verification errors shall be classified into the following categories:

Category	Description
Validation Error	Invalid request format or missing required data
Verification Failure	Incorrect OTP or verification factor
Expired Challenge	Verification challenge has expired
Rate Limited	Verification request temporarily blocked
Security Restriction	Verification blocked due to security policy
Provider Failure	External delivery provider unavailable
System Error	Internal verification service failure
Error Response Principles

Verification responses shall:

Return standardized error codes
Return localized user-facing messages
Avoid revealing internal verification state
Avoid exposing provider information
Avoid exposing account existence

Internal diagnostic information shall remain available only through authorized logging and monitoring systems.

Part 5.1 – Session Foundation

Document ID: WEMP-M01-005A

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-005A Section 1] 1. Objective
Purpose

Module 01 – Part 5 establishes the enterprise session management architecture for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

Following successful authentication and verification, authenticated identities shall interact with the platform through approved session management mechanisms.

This part defines how authenticated sessions are:

Created
Maintained
Validated
Monitored
Revoked
Recovered
Audited

The session architecture shall support:

Customer Web Portal
Seller Web Portal
Admin Web Portal
Super Admin Web Portal
Customer Mobile Application
Seller Mobile Application
Backend APIs

Session management shall maintain authenticated identity state.

Sessions shall never replace authentication or authorization.

Authentication is defined in Part 3.

Authorization is defined in Module 02.

[WEMP-M01-005A Section 2] 2. Scope
Included

Module 01 – Part 5 includes:

Enterprise session architecture
Session lifecycle
Session creation
Session validation
Session expiration
Session storage
Device association
Session monitoring
Session revocation
Logout architecture
Identity recovery foundation
Session audit logging
Recovery notifications
Cross-module session outputs
Excluded

The following are outside Part 5:

Registration
Password management
Password hashing
OTP generation
MFA enrollment
JWT architecture
Role assignment
Permission management
Seller onboarding
Business verification
Product management
Inventory
Orders
Payments
Shipping
Enterprise SSO
Passkeys

These responsibilities remain governed by previous or future modules.

[WEMP-M01-005A Section 3] 3. Session Principles

The session platform shall comply with the following principles.

3.1 Backend Authority

Only the backend Authentication Service shall:

Create sessions
Validate sessions
Extend sessions
Revoke sessions
Expire sessions

Client applications shall never directly modify session state.

3.2 Identity Binding

Every session shall belong to:

One Identity
One Device
One Authentication Event
One Refresh Token Family

Sessions shall never be shared between identities.

3.3 Session Independence

Each authenticated session shall remain independent.

Revoking one session shall not revoke unrelated sessions unless an approved security policy requires global revocation.

3.4 Session Integrity

Every session shall maintain:

Integrity
Confidentiality
Authenticity
Auditability

Unauthorized modification of session state shall be prevented.

3.5 Controlled Lifecycle

Sessions shall transition only through approved lifecycle states.

Client applications shall never force session state transitions.

3.6 Minimal Exposure

Session information exposed to clients shall be limited to approved metadata.

Clients shall never receive:

Refresh Token secrets
Internal security counters
Internal risk scores
Authentication secrets
Server-side validation metadata
3.7 Secure Failure

When session integrity cannot be guaranteed:

The session shall fail securely.
Session state shall not be promoted.
Security events shall be recorded.

Unexpected failures shall never automatically authenticate an identity.

3.8 Auditability

Every security-sensitive session event shall generate audit records.

Events include:

Session Created
Session Validated
Session Refreshed
Session Expired
Session Revoked
Logout
Logout All Devices
Session Recovery
Session Suspended

Audit records shall never contain authentication secrets.

[WEMP-M01-005A Section 4] 4. Enterprise Session Architecture
Objective

The WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform shall implement a centralized enterprise session architecture.

Session management shall operate independently from business modules.

Architecture Decision Reference

ADR: ADR-M01-003

Decision: Enterprise Session Management Architecture

Status: Approved

All session management implementation shall comply with ADR-M01-003.
C1 Ownership Clarification

ADR-M01-003 shall classify sessions by authentication channel or purpose rather than Customer, Seller, Admin or Super Admin role.

Sessions shall store authentication assurance and the applicable Module 01 authentication-security policy. Sessions shall not store Module 02 roles or permissions.

C2 Session Validation Clarification

ADR-M01-003 shall use PostgreSQL as the authoritative Session and Refresh Token state store.

JWT cryptographic validation shall remain stateless, while acceptance of every protected request shall require current Session state and Session Version validation.

Redis shall contain only a non-authoritative Session Validation Projection.

Canonical Session states shall be ACTIVE, REVOKED and EXPIRED.
Architecture Overview
Web / Mobile Clients
          │
          ▼
Authentication Service
          │
          ▼
Session Management Service
     │              │
     ▼              ▼
Session Store   Device Manager
     │              │
     ▼              ▼
Redis Cache   PostgreSQL
     │
     ▼
Audit & Monitoring Services
Authentication Service

The Authentication Service shall:

Authenticate identities
Create authenticated sessions
Validate authentication state
Coordinate session creation
Initiate session termination
Session Management Service

The Session Management Service shall:

Create sessions
Validate sessions
Extend sessions
Monitor session activity
Expire inactive sessions
Revoke sessions
Generate session events
PostgreSQL Responsibilities

PostgreSQL shall remain the authoritative datastore for:

Session records
Device associations
Session history
Revocation history
Recovery history
Audit references
Redis Responsibilities

Redis shall maintain temporary session state including:

Active session cache
Session validation cache
Rate-limit counters
Temporary security locks
Session activity cache

Redis shall never become the authoritative source of permanent session state.

[WEMP-M01-005A Section 5] 5. Session Classification
Objective

Module 01 sessions shall represent authentication scenarios and client channels only.

Sessions shall not be classified by Customer, Seller, Admin or Super Admin roles.

Approved Session Classes

Session Class	Purpose	Status
INTERACTIVE_WEB	Human authentication through an approved web client	Approved
INTERACTIVE_MOBILE	Human authentication through an approved mobile client	Approved
RECOVERY	Purpose-bound temporary Recovery Session; never an ordinary authenticated Session and never automatically promoted to one	Approved
MACHINE	Reserved for future machine-to-machine authentication	Deferred

Authentication-Security Policy

Each session shall reference the applicable Module 01 authentication-security policy.

Approved policy classifications include:

STANDARD_AUTHENTICATION
PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION
SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION

The authentication-security policy controls authentication requirements only.

It shall not grant permissions and shall not replace Module 02 authorization.

Session Ownership

Every session shall have:

One Session Identifier
One Identity Identifier
One Device-Session Identifier
One Authentication Timestamp
One Authentication Assurance Level
One Current Authentication State
One Authentication-Security Policy Reference

Session records shall not contain business roles or permissions.

Session ownership shall never transfer between identities.

Session Isolation

Sessions shall remain independently manageable and revocable.

Authorization for actions performed through a session shall be evaluated separately by Module 02.
C3 Recovery Invalidation Clarification

ADR-M01-003 shall require approved security-sensitive Recovery completion to revoke applicable Sessions, increment Session Version, revoke applicable Refresh Token Families, invalidate Trusted Devices where compromise is possible, invalidate affected Recovery codes and expire outstanding Recovery challenges before success is reported.

Session principles are approved.
ADR-M01-003 is recorded.
Enterprise session architecture is approved.
PostgreSQL and Redis responsibilities are approved.
Session types are approved.

WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform
Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 5.2 – Session Management

Document ID: WEMP-M01-005B

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-005B Section 6] 6. Session Creation
Objective

A session shall be created only after successful completion of all required authentication and verification processes.

Session creation establishes an authenticated interaction between an identity and the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

Session Creation Requirements

Before creating a session, the Authentication Service shall verify:

Identity is ACTIVE
Required authentication completed
Required verification completed
MFA completed (where required)
Identity is not locked
Identity is not suspended
Security policies permit session creation

Failure of any prerequisite shall prevent session creation.

Session Creation Workflow
Identity Authenticated
        ↓
Verification Complete
        ↓
MFA Complete (if required)
        ↓
Security Policy Validation
        ↓
Session Created
        ↓
Audit Event Generated
Session Initialization

Each newly created session shall initialize:

Session Identifier
Identity Identifier
Device-Session Identifier
Authentication Timestamp
Session Status
Authentication Methods Used
Authentication Assurance Level
Authentication-Security Policy Reference
Refresh Token Family Identifier
Last Activity Timestamp
Session Version
Idle Expiration Timestamp
Absolute Expiration Timestamp
Revocation Timestamp where applicable
Revocation Reason where applicable

Session creation shall not copy Module 02 roles, permissions or authorization decisions into the Module 01 session.

The Authentication Service shall be the only component authorized to create sessions.

[WEMP-M01-005B Section 7] 7. Session Validation
Objective

Session validation ensures that an authenticated Session remains valid before a protected request is accepted.

Validation shall occur on every authenticated request.

Validation Checks

The Session Management Service shall validate:

Session exists;
Session state is ACTIVE;
Identity state permits authentication;
Idle expiration has not been exceeded;
Absolute expiration has not been exceeded;
Session has not been revoked;
Token sessionVersion matches authoritative Session Version;
Refresh Token Family remains valid where refresh is requested;
Authentication-security policy is satisfied; and
Authentication Assurance is sufficient for the authentication operation.

Validation Source

The service may use the Redis Session Validation Projection for high-frequency validation.

PostgreSQL shall remain authoritative.

A cache miss, stale projection, inconsistent projection or unverifiable Session state shall not grant authentication.

Validation Failure

If validation fails:

The request shall be denied.
Session state shall remain unchanged unless revocation is required.
An audit event shall be generated.
A standardized authentication error shall be returned.

Validation failures shall never expose internal Session details.
[WEMP-M01-005B Section 8] 8. Session Lifecycle

Canonical Session States

Session State	Meaning
ACTIVE	Session may be accepted when all validation requirements succeed
REVOKED	Session was explicitly invalidated before natural expiration
EXPIRED	Session exceeded its approved idle or absolute lifetime

Lifecycle

Session Created as ACTIVE
        ↓
ACTIVE
        ↓
REVOKED

OR

ACTIVE
        ↓
EXPIRED

Lifecycle Rules

Session creation shall occur only after successful authentication.

Idle is activity metadata and shall not be a Session state.

Refresh is a Session event and shall not be a Session state.

Identity LOCKED, SUSPENDED, DISABLED and DELETED are Identity states and shall not be Session states.

When an Identity state prevents authentication, affected Sessions shall be rejected and revoked according to the approved security workflow.

Session transitions shall:

Be backend controlled;
Be auditable;
Preserve historical records;
Increment Session Version when previously issued Access Tokens must be invalidated; and
Prevent invalid transitions.

REVOKED and EXPIRED Sessions shall never return to ACTIVE.

[WEMP-M01-005B Section 9] 9. Session Timeout Policy
Objective

Timeout policies protect inactive authenticated sessions.

Timeout values shall remain centrally configurable.

Timeout Types

The platform shall support:

Idle Timeout
Absolute Session Timeout
Administrative Timeout
Security Timeout
Timeout Behaviour

When a timeout occurs:

Session becomes invalid.
Refresh operations shall follow approved token policy.
Audit event shall be generated.
Identity shall re-authenticate when required.

Timeout shall never silently extend a session.

Administrative Timeout

Module 01 shall define timeout policies by authentication-security classification.

PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION and SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION may require shorter idle and absolute timeouts than STANDARD_AUTHENTICATION.

Timeout policy shall not be selected from Customer, Seller, Admin or Super Admin roles stored in a Module 01 session.

Module 02 authorization roles shall not control Module 01 session storage directly.

Timeout Validation

Idle and absolute expiration timestamps shall be persisted in authoritative Session state and included in the Session Validation Projection.

Expiration shall transition the Session to EXPIRED and shall prevent further Access Token or Refresh Token acceptance.

Expiration shall not rely solely on client behavior or cached activity state.
[WEMP-M01-005B Section 10] 10. Session Storage Strategy
Objective

Session persistence shall support enterprise security, operational visibility and deterministic revocation.

PostgreSQL Responsibilities

PostgreSQL shall remain authoritative for:

Session state
Session Version
Idle and absolute expiration
Revocation state
Revocation reason
Refresh Token Family state
Refresh Token rotation history
Session history
Authentication timestamps
Device-Session metadata

Redis Responsibilities

Redis may maintain a temporary Session Validation Projection for high-frequency request validation.

Redis may also maintain:

Rate-limit counters
Temporary security locks
Short-lived activity data
Approved temporary security state

Redis shall never become the authoritative Session datastore.

Consistency Rules

Session creation, revocation, expiration and security invalidation shall be committed to PostgreSQL first.

After authoritative state changes, the Redis Session Validation Projection shall be updated or invalidated.

A Redis projection shall never extend a Session beyond authoritative expiration.

A missing projection shall not imply that a Session is valid.

An inconsistent or unverifiable projection shall not grant authentication.

If current Session state cannot be established safely, the protected request shall be denied.
Part 5.2 Acceptance Criteria

Part 5.2 is complete when:

Session creation architecture is approved.
Session validation architecture is approved.
Session lifecycle is approved.
Session timeout policy is approved.
Session storage strategy is approved.
PostgreSQL responsibilities are approved.
Redis responsibilities are approved.
Session retention policy is approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

Part 5.3 – Device Management

Document ID: WEMP-M01-005C

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-005C Section 11] 11. Device Registration
Objective

This section defines how client devices become associated with authenticated identities within the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

Device registration establishes a managed relationship between an identity and the device used to access the platform.

Device registration shall support:

Session management
Trusted device management
Security monitoring
Device revocation
Authentication audit logging

Device registration shall not imply device trust.

Trust shall be established separately according to approved security policies.

Registration Principles

Device registration shall:

Occur after successful authentication
Associate one device with one identity
Generate a unique Device Identifier
Record device metadata
Generate audit records

Every registered device shall remain independently manageable.

Registration Workflow
Authenticated Identity
        ↓
Device Information Collected
        ↓
Device Identifier Generated
        ↓
Device Metadata Recorded
        ↓
Device Registration Completed
        ↓
Audit Event Generated
Registration Metadata

Each registered device shall maintain:

Device Identifier
Identity Identifier
Platform
Operating System
Browser or Application
Registration Timestamp
Last Activity Timestamp
Device Status

Collection of device metadata shall follow approved privacy policies.

[WEMP-M01-005C Section 12] 12. Device Identification
Objective

Device identification provides a consistent mechanism for recognizing previously registered devices.

Device Identification Principles

Every registered device shall have:

One immutable Device Identifier
One associated Identity Identifier
One registration record
One current status

Device identifiers shall:

Be globally unique
Never be sequential
Never be reused
Never expose internal database identifiers
Device Matching

The Session Management Service shall compare:

Device Identifier
Authentication Context
Session Context
Identity Context

Device matching shall support security monitoring and session management.

[WEMP-M01-005C Section 13] 13. Device Trust Model
Objective

The Device Trust Model determines whether a registered device may receive reduced verification requirements under approved security policies.

Device trust shall never replace authentication.

Trust Principles

Trusted devices shall:

Be identity-specific
Be device-specific
Be revocable
Be auditable
Have configurable expiration

Trust shall never bypass mandatory MFA policies for privileged identities.

Trust States

A device may exist in one of the following states:

Pending
Trusted
Expired
Revoked
Blocked

Only the Trusted state may qualify for reduced verification under approved policies.

Trust Evaluation

Device trust evaluation may consider:

Successful MFA
Device history
Security policy
Previous activity
Administrative decisions

Trust evaluation criteria shall remain centrally configurable.

[WEMP-M01-005C Section 14] 14. Device Lifecycle
Objective

Every registered device shall follow an approved lifecycle.

Device Lifecycle
Registered
      ↓
Verified
      ↓
Trusted
      ↓
Active

OR

Expired

OR

Revoked

OR

Blocked
Lifecycle Rules

Device lifecycle transitions shall:

Be backend controlled
Generate audit records
Preserve history
Prevent invalid transitions

A revoked or blocked device shall never return directly to the Trusted state without completing an approved re-verification workflow.

[WEMP-M01-005C Section 15] 15. Device Revocation
Objective

Device revocation removes a device's trusted status and terminates its ability to access protected resources through existing authenticated sessions.

Revocation Sources

Device revocation may be initiated by:

Identity Owner
Administrator (where authorized)
Security Policy
Automatic Security Controls
Device Expiration
Revocation Workflow
Revocation Requested
        ↓
Device Located
        ↓
Trust Revoked
        ↓
Associated Sessions Revoked
        ↓
Audit Event Generated
Revocation Effects

Device revocation shall:

Remove trusted status
Revoke active sessions associated with the device
Require re-authentication
Require MFA where applicable
Preserve device history

Revocation shall not delete device records.

Device Retention

Historical device records shall remain available according to approved retention policies.

Retention periods shall remain centrally configurable and comply with legal and operational requirements.

Part 5.3 Acceptance Criteria

Part 5.3 is complete when:

Device registration architecture is approved.
Device identification architecture is approved.
Device trust model is approved.
Device lifecycle is approved.
Device revocation architecture is approved.
Device metadata requirements are approved.
Device retention policy is approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 5.4 – Session Control

Document ID: WEMP-M01-005D

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-005D Section 16] 16. Logout
Objective

Logout shall terminate the current authenticated Session while preserving unrelated Sessions belonging to the same Identity.

Canonical Logout Workflow

Authenticated Session
        ↓
Logout Request
        ↓
Validate Current Session and Request Authority
        ↓
Begin Authoritative Revocation Transaction
        ↓
Set Current Session to REVOKED
        ↓
Increment Current Session Version
        ↓
Revoke Current Refresh Token Family
        ↓
Commit Transaction
        ↓
Invalidate or Update Session Validation Projection
        ↓
Generate Audit Event
        ↓
Return Success

Logout shall invalidate:

All future refresh operations for the current Session;
All subsequent protected requests using an earlier Session Version; and
All Refresh Tokens belonging to the current Session family.

Logout shall not wait for the Access Token to expire before rejecting subsequent requests.

Unrelated Sessions shall remain unchanged.
[WEMP-M01-005D Section 17] 17. Logout All Devices
Objective

The platform shall support authoritative revocation of every current Module 01 Session belonging to an Identity.

Canonical Logout-All Workflow

Logout All Devices Requested
        ↓
Validate Identity and Request Authority
        ↓
Locate All ACTIVE Sessions for the Identity
        ↓
Begin Authoritative Revocation Transaction
        ↓
Set Every Applicable Session to REVOKED
        ↓
Increment Every Applicable Session Version
        ↓
Revoke Every Applicable Refresh Token Family
        ↓
Commit Transaction
        ↓
Invalidate or Update Every Session Validation Projection
        ↓
Generate Audit Event
        ↓
Return Success

Logout All Devices shall invalidate every current Module 01 Session belonging to the Identity, including the Session that initiated the request.

After committed logout-all revocation:

No previous Refresh Token shall be accepted.
No subsequent protected request using an earlier Session Version shall be accepted.
Fresh authentication shall be required.

Administrative identities may revoke sessions according to approved authorization policies.
[WEMP-M01-005D Section 18] 18. Session Revocation
Objective

Session revocation authoritatively invalidates a selected Session before its natural expiration.

Revocation may be initiated manually or automatically according to approved authority and security policy.

Revocation Sources

Session revocation may occur due to:

User logout
Logout All Devices
Password change
Password reset
Identity lock
Identity suspension
Device revocation
Security policy
Authorized administrative action
Detected credential compromise
Confirmed Refresh Token reuse

Revocation Workflow

Revocation Requested
        ↓
Locate Authoritative Session
        ↓
Validate Revocation Authority
        ↓
Begin Authoritative Revocation Transaction
        ↓
Set Session to REVOKED
        ↓
Increment Session Version
        ↓
Revoke Associated Refresh Token Family
        ↓
Record Revocation Reason and Timestamp
        ↓
Commit Transaction
        ↓
Invalidate or Update Session Validation Projection
        ↓
Generate Audit and Security Events

Revocation Rules

A revoked Session:

Shall reject all subsequent protected requests;
Shall reject all future Refresh Token operations;
Shall never return to ACTIVE;
Shall require new authentication;
Shall preserve historical records; and
Shall remain available for auditing according to approved retention policies.

The revocation operation shall be idempotent.

Repeating an authorized revocation request for an already REVOKED Session shall not restore or alter its security state.
[WEMP-M01-005D Section 19] 19. Concurrent Session Management
Objective

The platform shall support multiple simultaneous authenticated sessions while maintaining enterprise security.

Concurrent Session Principles

Concurrent sessions shall:

Remain independent
Be individually manageable
Be individually revocable
Maintain separate device associations
Maintain separate refresh-token families

Actions affecting one session shall not affect unrelated sessions unless explicitly required.

Concurrent Session Policy

The maximum number of concurrent sessions shall remain centrally configurable.

Different authentication-security classifications may have different session limits according to approved Module 01 security policies.

Session Visibility

Authenticated identities shall be able to view their active sessions.

Displayed session information may include:

Device Name
Platform
Browser or Application
Last Activity
Location (if available)
Current Session Indicator

Sensitive internal metadata shall never be exposed.

[WEMP-M01-005D Section 20] 20. Session Monitoring
Objective

Session monitoring supports continuous visibility into authenticated session activity.

Monitoring enables security analysis, anomaly detection, and operational auditing.

Monitored Events

The platform shall monitor:

Session creation
Session validation
Session refresh
Session expiration
Logout
Logout All Devices
Session revocation
Device changes
Trust changes
Security policy actions
Monitoring Principles

Session monitoring shall:

Generate audit events
Preserve historical records
Support security investigations
Support compliance reporting
Support future adaptive security controls

Monitoring shall not modify session state directly.

Security Monitoring

Future monitoring capabilities may include:

Impossible travel detection
Concurrent geographic anomalies
Suspicious device changes
Abnormal session duration
Repeated revocations
High-risk authentication behavior

These capabilities remain configurable and subject to future architecture approval.

Session Validation Failure Categories

Monitoring shall distinguish:

JWT cryptographic validation failure
JWT claim validation failure
Session not found
Session not ACTIVE
Session expired
Session version mismatch
Identity state rejection
Refresh Token reuse
Session projection inconsistency
Revocation propagation failure
Part 5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Part 5.4 is complete when:

Logout architecture is approved.
Logout All Devices architecture is approved.
Session revocation architecture is approved.
Concurrent session management is approved.
Session monitoring architecture is approved.
Administrative session revocation is approved.
Session visibility principles are approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 5.5 – Identity Recovery

Document ID: WEMP-M01-005E

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-005E Section 21] 21. Identity Recovery and Recovery Assurance Architecture
Objective

Identity Recovery provides a controlled mechanism for restoring legitimate access when normal authentication is unavailable or unsafe.

Recovery is an alternative authentication-security workflow. Recovery shall not grant Module 02 roles or permissions, shall not produce an authorization decision and shall not create an ordinary authenticated Session automatically.

Authoritative Ownership

Module 01 exclusively owns:

Recovery policy evaluation
Recovery Request state
Recovery evidence validation
Recovery approval-evidence validation
Credential and MFA-factor changes
Recovery Session restrictions
Recovery Assurance
Session, Refresh Token, Trusted Device and Recovery-code invalidation
Recovery completion
The final authentication outcome

Module 02 exclusively owns authorization of identities permitted to act as Recovery approvers.

Module 02 shall not execute Recovery, reset credentials, reset MFA, modify Module 01 records or create an Authenticated Identity Context.

Recovery Operation Classes

Operation Class	Purpose
PASSWORD_RESET	Replace a forgotten or compromised password
MFA_FACTOR_REPLACEMENT	Remove and replace an unavailable or compromised MFA factor
RECOVERY_CODE_REGENERATION	Invalidate and regenerate Recovery codes
VERIFIED_EMAIL_CHANGE	Replace a verified email authentication or Recovery channel
VERIFIED_MOBILE_CHANGE	Replace a verified mobile authentication or Recovery channel
IDENTITY_UNLOCK	Restore an eligible locked Identity
COMPROMISED_CREDENTIAL_RECOVERY	Recover after suspected credential compromise
PRIVILEGED_ACCOUNT_RECOVERY	Recover an Identity subject to PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION
SUPER_ADMIN_EMERGENCY_RECOVERY	Recover an Identity subject to SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION through the controlled emergency path

Every Recovery Request shall contain exactly one approved primary operation class. Additional effects shall be explicitly derived from the selected policy row and shall not be inferred by clients.

Recovery Assurance

Recovery Assurance represents confidence that a Recovery Request is controlled by the legitimate Identity owner and has satisfied every required approval.

Recovery Assurance is request-scoped and shall not be copied into an ordinary Session as Authentication Assurance.

Recovery Assurance Levels

RA0	Recovery requested; no acceptable evidence verified
RA1	Approved baseline independent Recovery evidence verified
RA2	Enhanced independent evidence and every required approval verified

RA0, RA1 and RA2 shall never map automatically to AAL0, AAL1 or AAL2.

Recovery Session

A Recovery Session shall:

Use the RECOVERY Session class
Be bound to one Recovery Request and one permitted operation
Be short-lived
Have an absolute expiry and no renewable ordinary-session lifetime
Contain Recovery Assurance and permitted-operation metadata only
Exclude Module 02 roles, permissions and authorization decisions
Reject APIs outside the approved Recovery workflow
Terminate after completion, cancellation, rejection, expiry or secure failure
Never be promoted into an ordinary authenticated Session

Fresh Authentication

After Recovery completion, the Identity shall perform fresh ordinary authentication.

Recovery completion shall not establish AAL1 or AAL2 automatically.

AAL2 requires successful fresh authentication with a qualifying retained or newly enrolled MFA factor.

Approved Recovery Scenarios

Recovery may be used only for an approved Recovery operation class.

Recovery shall not be used for business approval, KYC, seller onboarding, profile authorization, role assignment or permission assignment.

[WEMP-M01-005E Section 22] 22. Deterministic Recovery Policy Matrix
Objective

Every Recovery Request shall be evaluated against the following authoritative minimum policy. Implementations may apply stronger controls but shall not apply weaker controls.

Policy Matrix

Authentication-Security Classification	Operation Class	Minimum Independent Evidence	Approval Requirement	Minimum Recovery Assurance	Mandatory Completion Effects
STANDARD_AUTHENTICATION	PASSWORD_RESET or IDENTITY_UNLOCK	One approved verified channel or one valid Recovery code, subject to compromise checks	None	RA1	Revoke affected Recovery challenges; revoke Sessions and Refresh Token Families when compromise is suspected; require fresh authentication
STANDARD_AUTHENTICATION	MFA_FACTOR_REPLACEMENT or RECOVERY_CODE_REGENERATION	Two independent approved evidence sources, or one ordinary authenticated Session meeting approved assurance plus one independent Recovery factor	None unless risk policy escalates	RA2	Revoke all Sessions and Refresh Token Families; increment Session Version; invalidate affected Trusted Devices and unused Recovery codes; require fresh authentication and MFA setup
STANDARD_AUTHENTICATION	VERIFIED_EMAIL_CHANGE or VERIFIED_MOBILE_CHANGE	Evidence independent of the channel being replaced plus one additional approved factor	None unless risk policy escalates	RA2	Expire challenges for the old channel; revoke all Sessions and Refresh Token Families; increment Session Version; notify the old channel when safely available; require fresh authentication
STANDARD_AUTHENTICATION	COMPROMISED_CREDENTIAL_RECOVERY	Two independent approved evidence sources not suspected of compromise	None unless risk policy escalates	RA2	Revoke all Sessions and Refresh Token Families; increment Session Version; invalidate affected Trusted Devices and Recovery codes; require credential replacement and fresh authentication
PRIVILEGED_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION	Any security-sensitive Recovery operation	Two independent strong evidence sources not suspected of compromise	No human approval when the approved strong self-service evidence set is fully satisfied; otherwise dual control by two distinct Module 02-authorized approvers	RA2	Revoke all Sessions and Refresh Token Families; increment Session Version; invalidate all Trusted Devices and unused Recovery codes; require credential and MFA re-enrollment; restrict privileged authentication until fresh AAL2
SUPER_ADMIN_AUTHENTICATION	Any Recovery operation	Two independent strong evidence sources not suspected of compromise and controlled bootstrap evidence where applicable	Dual control by two distinct Module 02-authorized approvers is mandatory	RA2	Revoke all Sessions and Refresh Token Families; increment Session Version; invalidate all Trusted Devices and unused Recovery codes; require credential and MFA re-enrollment; restrict Super Admin authentication until fresh AAL2

Channel Independence

A channel being replaced, unavailable or suspected of compromise shall not be the sole evidence authorizing its own replacement.

Evidence sources counted as independent shall not share the same compromise boundary.

Human Approval and Separation of Duties

Where dual control applies:

Two distinct approvers are required.
Each approver shall be authorized by Module 02 at decision time.
The requester shall not approve their own Recovery.
One approver shall not satisfy both approvals.
Approvals shall be bound to the Recovery Request Identifier, Identity Identifier, operation class, approval decision, approver Identity Identifier and expiration timestamp.
Approvals shall expire and shall not be reusable.
Module 01 shall validate both approval records before execution.
Approval shall not authenticate the recovered Identity.

[WEMP-M01-005E Section 23] 23. Canonical Recovery Workflow and Security Controls
Objective

Every Recovery Request shall follow one deterministic state machine and fail securely.

Canonical Recovery States

REQUESTED
EVIDENCE_PENDING
EVIDENCE_VERIFIED
APPROVAL_PENDING
APPROVED
EXECUTING
COMPLETED
REJECTED
CANCELLED
EXPIRED
FAILED_SECURELY

Canonical State Machine

REQUESTED
    → EVIDENCE_PENDING
    → EVIDENCE_VERIFIED
    → APPROVAL_PENDING where policy requires approval
    → APPROVED
    → EXECUTING
    → COMPLETED

Terminal alternatives are REJECTED, CANCELLED, EXPIRED and FAILED_SECURELY.

APPROVAL_PENDING shall be skipped only when the deterministic policy row does not require human approval.

Concurrency and Idempotency

Every state transition shall use authoritative concurrency control.

Recovery completion shall be idempotent.

Concurrent execution shall not apply credential, factor or contact changes more than once.

Only one execution may transition an approved Recovery Request from APPROVED to EXECUTING.

Required Security Controls

Recovery shall enforce:

Rate limiting
Enumeration protection
Replay protection
Challenge expiration
Single-use challenges and evidence
Evidence-attempt limits
Approval expiration
Request and operation binding
Secure audit logging
Temporary Recovery restrictions
Same-channel Recovery prohibition
Risk escalation where approved
Secure handling of partial failures

Recovery-Triggered Invalidation

Before a security-sensitive Recovery is reported as COMPLETED, Module 01 shall perform the effects required by the selected policy row, including where required:

Revoke every applicable Session
Increment Session Version
Revoke every applicable Refresh Token Family
Invalidate applicable Trusted Devices
Invalidate unused Recovery codes
Expire outstanding Recovery challenges
Invalidate the Recovery Session
Commit credential, factor or contact changes
Record immutable audit events
Create required security notifications

The operation shall use an atomic transaction where state shares one consistency boundary. Cross-boundary effects shall use an approved transactional outbox, idempotent consumers and reconciliation. Partial failure shall not grant access or report successful completion before mandatory security effects are durably committed.

Secure Failure

If Recovery cannot be completed safely:

The Recovery Request shall transition to FAILED_SECURELY or another applicable terminal state.
Uncommitted credential, factor and contact changes shall not become active.
The Identity shall not receive an authenticated Session.
Recovery events shall be logged.
Security counters shall be updated.
Required incident alerts shall be generated.

Recovery Restrictions

Recovery shall never:

Reveal authentication secrets, MFA secrets or Recovery codes
Bypass the deterministic policy matrix
Automatically trust a new device
Grant Module 02 roles or permissions
Produce an authorization decision
Create or promote an ordinary authenticated Session
Treat Module 02 approval as authentication evidence
Permit a channel to authorize its own replacement as the sole evidence
[WEMP-M01-005E Section 24] 24. Recovery Audit Logging
Objective

Every recovery-related activity shall be recorded for security, compliance, and forensic investigation.

Logged Events

Recovery audit records shall include:

Recovery Requested
Recovery Challenge Generated
Recovery Challenge Delivered
Recovery Challenge Expired
Recovery Verification Success
Recovery Verification Failure
Password Reset
MFA Reset
Trusted Device Reset
Recovery Cancelled
Recovery Completed
RecoveryPolicyEvaluated
RecoveryEvidenceAccepted
RecoveryEvidenceRejected
RecoveryApprovalRequested
RecoveryApprovalGranted
RecoveryApprovalRejected
RecoveryApprovalExpired
PrivilegedRecoveryStarted
RecoveryCredentialChanged
RecoveryMfaReset
RecoverySessionsRevoked
RecoveryRefreshTokenFamiliesRevoked
RecoveryTrustedDevicesInvalidated
RecoveryFailedSecurely
Audit Record Fields

Every recovery audit record shall include:

Timestamp
Identity Identifier
Session Identifier (if available)
Correlation Identifier
Source IP Address
User Agent
Device Identifier (if available)
Event Type

Recovery audit records shall be immutable.

Sensitive recovery information shall never be stored in audit logs.

[WEMP-M01-005E Section 25] 25. Recovery Notifications
Objective

Recovery notifications shall inform identities of recovery-related activity affecting their accounts.

Notification Types

The platform may notify identities of:

Recovery request initiated
Recovery challenge sent
Password reset completed
MFA reset completed
Trusted device removed
Contact information updated
Recovery completed
Suspicious recovery attempt
Notification Principles

Notifications shall:

Be generated by backend services
Be independently auditable
Avoid exposing sensitive information
Support localization

Notifications shall never include:

Passwords
OTP values
Recovery codes
MFA secrets
Authentication tokens
Delivery Channels

Recovery notifications may be delivered through:

Email
SMS
Mobile Push Notifications (Future)
In-App Notifications (Future)

Delivery mechanisms shall remain centrally configurable.

Privileged and Super Admin Recovery Notifications

Privileged Recovery events shall generate immediate security notifications and operational alerts.

Notifications shall be delivered through an independent previously verified channel when safely available. A channel suspected of compromise shall not be trusted as the only notification destination.

Notification delivery failure shall not reverse committed Recovery security effects and shall create an operational alert for reconciliation.
C6 Session, Revocation and Recovery Failure Outcomes

Session validation without current authoritative state: FAIL_CLOSED.
Logout with confirmed committed revocation: succeed and clear client credentials.
Logout with uncertain commit: return SECURITY_OPERATION_PENDING_RECONCILIATION; idempotent retry resolves the committed outcome.
Logout All with partial/uncertain processing: remain pending, reconcile every Session idempotently and do not claim completion.
Recovery evidence or approval dependency unavailable: TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE without advancing authoritative Recovery state.
Recovery execution unable to commit mandatory C3 invalidation: FAIL_CLOSED and transition/reconcile according to FAILED_SECURELY rules; never report completion.
Notification failure after committed Recovery: preserve committed security effects, record delivery failure, alert and reconcile.
Part 5.5 Acceptance Criteria

Part 5.5 is complete when:

Identity Recovery architecture is approved.
Account Recovery workflow is approved.
Recovery security controls are approved.
Recovery audit logging is approved.
Recovery notifications are approved.
Recovery validation requirements are approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

[WEMP-M01-005F Section 26] 26. Session & Recovery Error Model
Objective

The Session and Recovery Error Model establishes standardized error handling for all session management and identity recovery operations.

Error responses shall provide meaningful feedback to legitimate clients while protecting internal implementation details and preventing information disclosure.

Error Handling Principles

Session and recovery errors shall:

Be standardized across all services
Prevent identity enumeration
Avoid exposing internal session state
Support localization
Be fully auditable
Remain implementation-independent
Standard Error Categories
Category	Description
Validation Error	Invalid request or missing required data
Session Expired	Session has exceeded its lifetime
Session Revoked	Session is no longer valid
Recovery Failed	Recovery verification unsuccessful
Recovery Expired	Recovery challenge expired
Rate Limited	Request temporarily restricted
Security Restriction	Operation blocked by security policy
System Error	Internal platform error
Error Response Principles

Error responses shall:

Return standardized error codes
Return localized user-facing messages
Avoid exposing session identifiers
Avoid revealing account existence
Avoid exposing security policy details

Detailed diagnostic information shall be available only through authorized logging and monitoring systems.

Part 6 – Authentication-to-Authorization Integration Boundary

Document ID: WEMP-M01-006

Version: 1.1

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-006 Section 1] 1. Objective

Part 6 defines the integration boundary between Module 01 – Identity & Authentication and Module 02 – Role & Permission Management.

Module 01 authenticates an identity and produces a trusted authenticated identity context. Module 02 consumes that context and independently determines whether the identity is authorized to perform a requested action.

Authentication and authorization shall remain separate architectural responsibilities.

[WEMP-M01-006 Section 2] 2. Module 01 Ownership

Module 01 owns only:

Identity records and identity lifecycle
Authentication credentials
Registration and verification
OTP and MFA
Authentication sessions and devices
Access-token and refresh-token lifecycle
Identity recovery
Authenticated identity context creation
Authentication security events and authentication audit records

Module 01 shall not own or define roles, permissions, role assignments, permission assignments, role hierarchy, resource authorization policies, temporary permissions, delegated administration, or authorization decisions.

[WEMP-M01-006 Section 3] 3. Module 02 Ownership

Module 02 owns:

Roles and permissions
Role and permission assignments
Role hierarchy
Resource authorization policies
Temporary permissions
Delegated authorization administration
Authorization evaluation and decisions
Authorization audit events

The detailed authorization model, persistence model, APIs, events, caching strategy, testing strategy, monitoring rules, and governance shall be defined and approved in Module 02.
Module 02 role assignment and role revocation shall remain Module 02 operations.

When a privileged role assignment or revocation requires an authentication-security classification or session change, Module 02 shall send an approved versioned and idempotent request or event to Module 01.

Module 02 shall not modify Module 01 Identity or session records directly.

Partial or failed cross-module processing shall not grant privileged access.

C2 Authentication Context Validity

Module 01 shall create an Authenticated Identity Context only after successful JWT cryptographic validation, JWT claim validation, authoritative Session-state validation and Session-Version validation.

A cryptographically valid JWT referencing an invalid Session shall not produce an Authenticated Identity Context.
C3 Recovery Approval Boundary

Module 02 shall authorize identities permitted to act as Recovery approvers. Module 01 shall own Recovery requests, policy evaluation, approval-evidence validation, Recovery execution, credential and factor changes, invalidation and authentication outcomes.

Module 02 approval shall not directly reset authentication state or create an Authenticated Identity Context. Module 01 shall validate time-bound request-bound approval evidence through approved contracts without direct database access.
C6 Module 02 Availability Boundary

Authentication-only Module 01 operations may continue when their Module 01 authoritative dependencies are healthy and no Module 02 decision is required.
An operation requiring authorization shall not proceed without a current Module 02 authorization decision.
Module 02 unavailability shall return AUTHORIZATION_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE and shall never grant access.
Privileged Recovery approval shall not proceed without current approver-authorization evidence.
No Module 01 authorization cache is introduced. Any future bounded decision cache requires Module 02 ownership and explicit approval.
Cross-module retries remain versioned and idempotent and shall not create privileged access after partial failure.
C10 Privacy Ownership Boundary

Module 01 owns universal Identity privacy state, authentication-data inventory, Terms/Notice/Consent evidence, Module 01 privacy execution, identifier tombstones, authentication invalidation, Module 01 retention evaluation and anonymization/destruction evidence. Module 02 owns roles, authorization and privacy-operator/case authorization. Module 03 owns Seller data; Module 06 owns Customer data.

The authoritative enterprise privacy-request orchestrator is ENTERPRISE_PRIVACY_ORCHESTRATOR_TBD. Until approved, Module 01 executes only its data operations, never reports enterprise-wide completion, never modifies another module's records and uses approved C8 contracts.

Canonical Privacy Request Types

ACCESS, CORRECTION, DELETION, RESTRICTION, DATA_EXPORT, CONSENT_WITHDRAWAL, PROCESSING_OBJECTION and OTHER_APPROVED_REQUEST. Listing does not declare legal applicability. Legal/Privacy approve eligibility, verification, response timing, rejection, output, retention, appeal and scope.

Canonical Privacy Request States

RECEIVED, IDENTITY_VERIFICATION_REQUIRED, VALIDATED, SCOPE_RESOLUTION, DEPENDENCY_PROCESSING, RETENTION_HOLD, READY_FOR_EXECUTION, EXECUTING, PARTIALLY_COMPLETED, COMPLETED, CANCELLED, REJECTED and FAILED_SECURELY. Partial completion identifies outstanding owners and is never reported as completion.
C9 Audit Ownership Boundary

Module 01 owns authentication/security evidence only. Module 02 owns authorization evidence. A protected Module 02 decision reference may be recorded but roles, permissions, policies and decision internals shall not be copied. Audit access requires Module 02 authorization and cannot be granted by Module 01 authentication-security classification.
C8 Asynchronous Ownership Boundary

Cross-module Commands do not transfer data ownership. Module 01 may request Module 02, Module 03 or Module 06 to perform an operation owned by that target module, but command acceptance is not completion and shall not be interpreted as authorization, profile creation or business approval. Module 01 Integration Events expose committed authentication facts only through approved subscriptions. No asynchronous contract may bypass the C1 authorization/profile boundaries, C2 authoritative Session checks, C3 Recovery approval boundary or C7 secret controls.
[WEMP-M01-006 Section 4] 4. Authenticated Identity Context

After successful authentication, Module 01 shall provide a trusted authenticated identity context containing only:

Identity Identifier
Session Identifier
Authentication Timestamp
Authentication Method
Authentication Assurance Level
Current MFA State
Device-Session Identifier
Correlation Identifier

The authenticated identity context shall not contain business roles, business permissions, permission matrices, resource policies, or precomputed authorization decisions.

[WEMP-M01-006 Section 5] 5. Request Processing Boundary

The required processing sequence is:

Client Request
        ↓
Module 01 Authentication Validation
        ↓
Authenticated Identity Context
        ↓
Module 02 Authorization Evaluation
        ↓
Business Module Execution only when Module 02 grants access

Module 01 authentication middleware and guards shall authenticate requests only. They shall not grant business access or evaluate business permissions.

Module 02 shall consume the authenticated identity context through approved service contracts. Direct database coupling between Module 01 and Module 02 is prohibited.

[WEMP-M01-006 Section 6] 6. Failure Boundary

If Module 01 cannot authenticate the request safely, the request shall be rejected before authorization evaluation.

If Module 02 cannot evaluate authorization safely, Module 02 shall deny access according to its approved secure-failure policy.

An authentication success shall never imply authorization success.

[WEMP-M01-006 Section 7] 7. Audit and Observability Boundary

Module 01 owns authentication, session, verification, MFA, device, and recovery audit events and telemetry.

Module 02 owns authorization-policy, role, permission, assignment, and authorization-decision audit events and telemetry.

Correlation identifiers shall enable cross-module tracing without transferring data ownership.

[WEMP-M01-006 Section 8] 8. Testing Boundary

Module 01 shall test authentication enforcement and the correctness, integrity, and secure handoff of the authenticated identity context.

Module 02 shall test roles, permissions, assignments, authorization policies, authorization decisions, deny-by-default behavior, and privilege-escalation controls.

Cross-module integration tests shall verify that Module 02 consumes the Module 01 context through approved contracts and that no direct database coupling exists.

[WEMP-M01-006 Section 9] 9. Cross-Module Outputs

Part 6 produces:

Authentication-to-Authorization Integration Boundary
Authenticated Identity Context Contract
Authentication Handoff Requirements
Audit and Observability Ownership Boundary
Testing Ownership Boundary
Proposed Authentication-to-Authorization Boundary ADR

[WEMP-M01-006 Section 10] 10. Acceptance Criteria

Part 6 is complete for draft review when:

Module ownership is explicit.
The authenticated identity context contains no business authorization data.
Authentication middleware performs no business authorization evaluation.
Direct database coupling is prohibited.
Audit, monitoring, and testing ownership are separated.
Module 02 remains the authoritative owner of authorization.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

[WEMP-M01-006 Section 11] 11. Proposed Architecture Decision Record

ADR: Proposed ADR-M01-004 – Authentication-to-Authorization Boundary

Status: Proposed

Context

Module 01 must provide trustworthy authentication results to Module 02 without owning or duplicating authorization capabilities.

Decision

Module 01 shall own the universal Identity, identity authentication lifecycle, authentication-security classifications and session Authentication Assurance.

Module 03 shall own Seller profiles, seller organizations, seller onboarding and seller business status.

Module 06 shall own Customer profiles and customer business data.

Module 02 shall own roles, permissions, assignments, authorization policies, authorization evaluation and authorization decisions.

JWTs, sessions and the authenticated identity context shall contain authentication information only and shall not contain roles, permissions, profile state, user-type claims or authorization decisions.

Integration shall use approved versioned contracts, stable Identity Identifiers and correlation identifiers. Direct database coupling and cross-module foreign keys are prohibited.

Consequences

Authentication and authorization can evolve independently.
Shared business authorization data is not embedded in Module 01 tokens or persistence.
Module 02 can be separated into an independent service later without redesigning Module 01 domain ownership.
Cross-module contract, audit-correlation, failure, and integration testing requirements become mandatory.

This ADR remains Proposed and does not supersede any approved ADR until formally reviewed and approved.

Part 6 Draft Status

Part 6 remains Draft and is not approved for implementation.

Why this structure?

Part 7 translates everything defined in Parts 1–6 into technical contracts.

It defines:

REST API standards
Domain-Driven Design boundaries
Database entities
DTO architecture
