It does not define business features such as products, orders, or payments. Those modules will implement APIs according to the standards established here.

WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform
Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 7.1 – API Foundation

Document ID: WEMP-M01-007A

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-007A Section 1] 1. Objective
Purpose

Module 01 – Part 7 establishes the enterprise API architecture for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

This part defines how platform services expose functionality through standardized API contracts while maintaining consistency, security, scalability, and backward compatibility.

The API architecture shall provide:

Standardized request processing
Consistent response formats
Versioned endpoints
Secure communication
Centralized validation
Predictable integration
Long-term maintainability

Business logic shall remain independent of transport protocols.

API Objectives

The API platform shall ensure:

Consistency
Simplicity
Security
Stability
Version compatibility
Discoverability
Auditability
Future extensibility
[WEMP-M01-007A Section 2] 2. Scope
Included

Part 7 includes:

API architecture
API design principles
API versioning
Request standards
Response standards
REST contract governance
Future integration boundaries
OpenAPI foundation
API lifecycle governance
Excluded

The following remain outside Part 7:

Authentication logic
Authorization logic
Business workflows
Seller onboarding
Product APIs
Inventory APIs
Order APIs
Payment APIs
Shipping APIs
Notification implementation

These modules shall consume the standards established here.

[WEMP-M01-007A Section 3] 3. API Design Principles

The API platform shall comply with the following principles.

3.1 API-First Design

API contracts shall be designed before implementation begins.

Implementation shall conform to approved contracts.

3.2 Consistency

Equivalent operations shall use consistent:

Resource naming
HTTP methods
Status codes
Request structures
Response structures
Error models
3.3 Request-Oriented Transport and State-Aware Authentication

Each HTTP request shall carry the transport metadata and client-supplied information required by its approved contract.

Request-oriented HTTP transport shall not be interpreted as stateless authentication.

For every protected operation:

JWT cryptographic and claim validation is required.
The authoritative C2 Session state and Session Version shall be validated.
A valid JWT alone shall not establish current authentication.
Redis may supply only the approved non-authoritative Session Validation Projection.
Server-managed Session state intentionally determines authentication acceptance.

Business request semantics shall not depend on undocumented client state.

3.4 Backward Compatibility

Approved APIs shall remain backward compatible throughout supported versions.

Breaking changes require a new API version.

3.5 Secure by Default

All APIs shall:

Require HTTPS
Validate input
Validate authentication where required
Validate the accepted Session class and AAL where required
Pass authenticated identity context to Module 02 where authorization is required
Generate required audit events

Security shall never depend on client behavior.

3.6 Standard Idempotency Contract

Approved retryable mutations shall use the Idempotency-Key request header.

The key shall be scoped to the authenticated Identity or approved anonymous client fingerprint, API operation identifier and API version.

The platform shall persist a protected request fingerprint and the committed result reference.

An identical replay shall return the original committed result and shall not repeat side effects.
A replay with the same key and a different fingerprint shall fail with IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED.
An in-progress replay shall return the deterministic IDEMPOTENCY_IN_PROGRESS response.
Idempotency retention shall be centrally configured; no unapproved fixed duration is established here.

Operations not marked Idempotency Required shall reject or ignore the header according to the endpoint catalogue and shall never infer idempotency silently.

3.7 Standardization

Every API shall follow approved standards for naming, validation, errors, pagination, filtering, sorting and documentation.

3.8 Observability

Every API shall support request tracing, correlation identifiers, structured logging, metrics, monitoring and required audit logging without recording authentication secrets.4. Enterprise API Architecture
Objective

The WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform shall implement a centralized API contract architecture.

The architecture shall separate:

Transport
Validation
Authentication
Authorization
Business Logic
Persistence

Each layer shall remain independently maintainable.

Architecture Decision Reference

ADR: ADR-M01-005

Decision: Enterprise API Contract Architecture

Status: Approved

C2 Revocation and Rotation Clarification

ADR-M01-003 shall define ACTIVE, REVOKED and EXPIRED as the canonical Session states; atomic single-successor Refresh Token rotation; family and Session revocation after confirmed reuse; Session Version increment on security invalidation; idempotent revocation; and rejection of every protected request beginning validation after committed revocation.

[WEMP-M01-007A Section 5] 5. API Versioning Strategy
Objective

API versioning ensures platform evolution without breaking existing client integrations.

Versioning Principles

The platform shall:

Support multiple API versions
Maintain backward compatibility
Clearly identify deprecated versions
Provide migration paths
Preserve client stability
Version Format

Phase 1 shall use URL-based versioning.

Example:

/api/v1/auth/login
/api/v1/auth/register
/api/v1/auth/logout

Future versions:

/api/v2/...
Deprecation Policy

When introducing a new version:

Existing versions shall remain supported during the approved deprecation period.
Clients shall receive advance notice before removal.
Deprecated versions shall continue to receive security fixes where applicable.
Version Governance

Every API version shall define:

Supported endpoints
Breaking changes
Compatibility guarantees
Documentation version
Lifecycle status
Recovery API Contract Requirements

Module 01 shall define versioned contracts for:

Starting a Recovery Request
Submitting Recovery evidence
Requesting human approval where required
Recording an approval decision from a Module 02-authorized approver
Completing the single permitted Recovery operation
Cancelling Recovery
Retrieving only a safe non-enumerating Recovery status

Every mutating Recovery endpoint shall require an idempotency key, correlation identifier, Recovery Request Identifier and expected state or version where applicable.

Approval decisions shall be bound to the Recovery Request, recovered Identity, operation class, approver Identity, decision and expiration. Clients shall never select a weaker policy row.

Recovery APIs shall use enumeration-safe responses and shall never return evidence values, secrets, Module 02 roles or permissions.
[WEMP-M01-007A Section 6] 6. Authoritative REST API Contract Architecture

This section is the authoritative Phase 1 Module 01 REST operation catalogue. An operation not listed here is not approved for implementation.

Endpoint Exposure Classes

PUBLIC_ANONYMOUS	No established Identity required; abuse controls apply
PUBLIC_ENUMERATION_SAFE	Anonymous operation whose external response must conceal Identity, registration, channel or Recovery existence
AUTHENTICATED_IDENTITY	Valid ordinary C2 Session required
AAL2_REQUIRED	Valid ordinary Session with current AAL2 required
RECOVERY_SESSION	Valid purpose-bound C3 RECOVERY Session restricted to the bound Recovery Request and operation
MODULE_02_AUTHORIZED	Valid ordinary Session plus a current Module 02 authorization decision through the approved contract
INTERNAL_SERVICE	Authenticated service-to-service contract; not internet-public
BOOTSTRAP_CONTROLLED	Deployment/operations-controlled bootstrap contract; unavailable after approved bootstrap completion
NOT_EXPOSED	Capability has no REST operation

Common Headers

X-Correlation-ID	Optional client correlation identifier; server generates one when absent
Idempotency-Key	Required only where the catalogue states Required
If-Match	Required where the catalogue states Version; contains the quoted current resource version/ETag
Authorization	Bearer Access Token for protected Web and Flutter API requests
X-CSRF-Token	Required for Web requests that rely on the Refresh Token cookie
Content-Type	application/json for request bodies
Accept	application/json

Common Contract Rules

All paths are prefixed by /api/v1.
All protected operations apply C2 JWT, Session-state and Session-Version validation.
RECOVERY_SESSION operations additionally validate Recovery Request, permitted operation, expiry, policy version and expected state/version.
MODULE_02_AUTHORIZED operations obtain a current authorization decision and never read Module 02 storage directly.
No endpoint returns roles, permissions, profile state, password hashes, token digests, OTP values, MFA secrets or internal database fields.
Every mutating operation is non-cacheable.

NOT_EXPOSED Capabilities

Direct role management, permission management and authorization-policy management are not Module 01 REST operations.
Raw Credential, token, MFA-secret, Recovery-evidence, audit-payload and database-record retrieval are not exposed.
Customer and Seller profile management are not Module 01 REST operations.
Trusted Device rename is deferred because no approved business requirement exists.

[WEMP-M01-007A Section 7] 7. Token Transport Model

Web

Access Token: returned in the successful authentication/refresh response body, held in application memory only and sent in the Authorization Bearer header.
Refresh Token: delivered only in the Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict cookie named __Secure-walrus_rt with Path=/api/v1/auth and no Domain attribute.
CSRF: Web requests that rely on the Refresh Token cookie shall present X-CSRF-Token using the approved server-bound double-submit or equivalent centrally approved mechanism.
Logout: clears the Refresh Token cookie and commits C2 authoritative revocation before success.
Browser localStorage and sessionStorage shall not store Access or Refresh Tokens.

Flutter

Access and Refresh Tokens may be returned in the approved mobile response fields over TLS.
Refresh Token shall be stored only in operating-system-backed secure storage.
Access Token shall be held in memory where practical and sent as Authorization Bearer.
Refresh Token shall be submitted only to M01-AUTH-003 in its approved protected request field.
Logout shall delete local tokens after the authoritative server response; local deletion failure shall not reverse server revocation.

Common Token Rules

Tokens shall never appear in URLs, query strings, logs, traces, metrics, analytics, crash reports or error details.
Refresh Tokens shall not be accepted by ordinary protected endpoints.
CORS shall use an explicit allowlist; wildcard origins are prohibited with credentials.
Authentication and token responses shall use Cache-Control: no-store and Pragma: no-cache.

[WEMP-M01-007A Section 8] 8. Optimistic Concurrency Contract

Mutable resource operations marked If-Match shall require a strong ETag representing the current logical aggregate/state version.
The client sends that value in If-Match.
A missing required precondition returns PRECONDITION_REQUIRED with HTTP 428.
A stale or mismatched version returns RESOURCE_STATE_CONFLICT with HTTP 412.
An idempotent replay of an already committed identical request returns the original committed result before a stale-version error is considered.

REGISTRATION

Thresholds, windows and lockout interaction are centrally configured and remain explicit approved configuration values. Rate-limited responses use HTTP 429, stable RATE_LIMITED code and safe Retry-After metadata where disclosure is acceptable.
[WEMP-M01-007A Section 10] 10. Operation Criticality and Degraded-Mode Catalogue

Criticality Tiers

TIER_A_AUTHENTICATION_CRITICAL	Session validation, login continuation, token refresh and current authorization-context establishment
TIER_B_SECURITY_MUTATION	Revocation, credential/MFA change, Recovery execution, privileged approval and Identity security transitions
TIER_C_LIFECYCLE	Registration, verification initiation, enrollment, device management and deletion requests
TIER_D_ASYNCHRONOUS	Provider delivery, notifications and external event publication

Degraded Modes

AVAILABLE	Normal approved dependencies available
AUTHORITATIVE_FALLBACK	Redis-dependent read may use protected PostgreSQL fallback
QUEUED_SIDE_EFFECT	Authoritative state committed; non-security external delivery is pending through Outbox/reconciliation
TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE	Operation not started or safely rejected due dependency outage
FAIL_CLOSED	Security-sensitive acceptance prohibited because required state cannot be established

Operation Group	Tier	PostgreSQL Failure	Redis Failure	Module 02 Failure	Provider/Outbox Failure
M01-AUTH-001 through M01-AUTH-003	A	FAIL_CLOSED	AUTHORITATIVE_FALLBACK or FAIL_CLOSED	Not Applicable	QUEUED_SIDE_EFFECT only for non-security notifications; signing/key failure is FAIL_CLOSED
M01-AUTH-004, M01-AUTH-005 and M01-SES-003	B	TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE with reconciliation for uncertain commit	AUTHORITATIVE_FALLBACK or FAIL_CLOSED	Not Applicable	QUEUED_SIDE_EFFECT for audit/event publication after atomic Outbox intent
M01-SES-001, M01-SES-002, M01-DEV-001	A/C	FAIL_CLOSED	AUTHORITATIVE_FALLBACK or FAIL_CLOSED	Not Applicable	Not Applicable
M01-CRED-001 through M01-CRED-003	B	FAIL_CLOSED	AUTHORITATIVE_FALLBACK or FAIL_CLOSED	Not Applicable	QUEUED_SIDE_EFFECT only after authoritative commit
M01-MFA-001 through M01-MFA-005	B/C	FAIL_CLOSED	FAIL_CLOSED where challenge/rate state cannot be established; otherwise authoritative fallback	Not Applicable	TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE for required delivery
M01-VER-001, M01-VER-002 and M01-REG-001 through M01-REG-005	C	TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE	FAIL_CLOSED where abuse/challenge state cannot be established; otherwise fallback	Not Applicable	TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE or UNCERTAIN according to provider matrix
M01-REC-001 through M01-REC-004, M01-REC-006 and M01-REC-007	B	FAIL_CLOSED	FAIL_CLOSED where Recovery/rate state cannot be safely established	Not Applicable	QUEUED_SIDE_EFFECT only for notification after mandatory security commit
M01-REC-005	B	FAIL_CLOSED	FAIL_CLOSED	FAIL_CLOSED	QUEUED_SIDE_EFFECT only after approval record commits
M01-ID-001 through M01-ID-003 and M01-ID-005	B/C	FAIL_CLOSED	AUTHORITATIVE_FALLBACK or FAIL_CLOSED	Not Applicable	QUEUED_SIDE_EFFECT after authoritative commit
M01-ID-004	B	FAIL_CLOSED	AUTHORITATIVE_FALLBACK or FAIL_CLOSED	FAIL_CLOSED	QUEUED_SIDE_EFFECT after authoritative commit
M01-ADM-001 and M01-CLS-001	B	FAIL_CLOSED	FAIL_CLOSED	FAIL_CLOSED where authorization is required	QUEUED_SIDE_EFFECT after authoritative commit
M01-ADM-002	B	FAIL_CLOSED	FAIL_CLOSED	FAIL_CLOSED where coordination is required	TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE
M01-KEY-001	A	Not Applicable	Not Applicable	Not Applicable	TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE when the approved public-key set cannot be served safely; no private-key fallback

Every one of the 41 approved operations is covered by the groups above. Degraded behavior shall not change its C5/C7/C10 DTO, exposure, token transport, idempotency or concurrency contract.

Dependency Readiness and Circuit Breakers

Liveness reports process viability only. Readiness denies new traffic when an operation class cannot meet its authoritative dependency contract.
Dependency health shall distinguish healthy, degraded, unavailable and recovering.
Circuit breakers shall use centrally approved thresholds and shall not cache security success.
Circuit opening for PostgreSQL, Module 02, signing/key or security provider dependencies shall produce the catalogued fail-closed/temporary behavior.
Half-open probes shall be bounded and shall not perform unsafe security mutations.
Connection pools, fallback concurrency and retry budgets shall be bounded to prevent retry storms and PostgreSQL overload.
Part 7.1 Acceptance Criteria

Part 7.1 is complete when:

API objectives are approved.
Scope and exclusions are approved.
API design principles are approved.
ADR-M01-005 is recorded.
Enterprise API architecture is approved.
Layer responsibilities are approved.
API versioning strategy is approved.
Deprecation policy is approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

Part 7.2 – Domain Model

Document ID: WEMP-M01-007B

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-007B Section 6] 6. Domain-Driven Design (DDD) Boundaries
Objective

Module 01 forms an Identity & Authentication bounded context responsible for:

Identity Management
Registration
Authentication
Verification
Session Management
Identity Recovery
Authenticated Identity Context

Authorization is a separate bounded context owned by Module 02.

Business capabilities outside this context shall belong to their respective modules.

Boundary Principles

Module 01 shall expose approved APIs and events without sharing database ownership.
The Module 01 domain shall not contain role, permission, assignment, authorization-policy, or authorization-decision models.
Module 02 shall consume the authenticated identity context through approved contracts.

Context Ownership Map

Bounded Context	Owned Concepts
Module 01 – Identity & Authentication	Universal Identity, credentials, verification, MFA, sessions, devices, recovery, authentication-security classification and session assurance
Module 02 – Role & Permission Management	Roles, permissions, assignments, policies and authorization decisions
Module 03 – Seller Management	Seller profile, seller organization, onboarding and seller business status
Module 06 – Customer Management	Customer profile and customer business data

The same Identity Identifier may be referenced by each context without shared table ownership.
[WEMP-M01-007B Section 7] 7. Authoritative Persistence Inventory
Objective

The persistence inventory defines every approved Module 01 logical record category required by the Identity & Authentication bounded context.

Persistence Category Definitions

Aggregate Root	Authoritative consistency boundary that controls state transitions and aggregate-owned records
Aggregate-Owned Entity	Persistent entity whose lifecycle and invariants are controlled by one Aggregate Root
Append-Only Record	Immutable historical or security record that may be inserted and read but never updated or deleted by application workflows
Projection	Rebuildable non-authoritative representation derived from authoritative state
Integration Record	Operational record supporting idempotency, reliable event delivery or external contract processing

Approved Aggregate Roots

Identity
Session
Verification Challenge
Recovery Request

Identity Aggregate-Owned Entities

Identity Identifier
Credential
Credential History Record
Password History Record
Authentication-Security Classification Assignment
MFA Enrollment
MFA Factor

Trusted Device
Identity State Transition

Refresh Token Family
Refresh Token Record

Verification Challenge Aggregate-Owned Entities

Verification Attempt
OTP Evidence Record

Recovery Request Aggregate-Owned Entities

Recovery Evidence Record
Recovery Approval Record
Recovery Attempt
Recovery State Transition
Recovery Notification Record

Append-Only Records

Authentication Audit Record
Recovery Audit Record
Security State Transition Record

Projections

Redis Session Validation Projection

The Redis Session Validation Projection shall remain rebuildable and non-authoritative. PostgreSQL shall remain authoritative for Identity, Credential, Session, Refresh Token, Verification, MFA, Trusted Device, Recovery and audit state.

Integration Records

Transactional Outbox Record
Inbound Idempotency Record where an approved inbound operation requires durable deduplication
API Idempotency Record where an approved mutating endpoint requires durable replay protection
Notification Delivery Record where delivery reconciliation is required

Excluded Persistence

Module 01 shall not persist:

Module 02 roles
Module 02 permissions
Module 02 assignments
Module 02 authorization policies or decisions
Customer profile or customer business fields owned by Module 06
Seller profile, seller organization, onboarding or seller business fields owned by Module 03
Cross-module foreign keys
Raw passwords
Raw Refresh Tokens
Raw OTP values
Raw Recovery codes
Authorization data inside Sessions or JWT persistence

Additional authoritative structures require formal architecture approval.

[WEMP-M01-007B Section 8] 8. Aggregate Boundaries and Ownership
Objective

Aggregate boundaries define which invariants shall commit atomically in PostgreSQL.

Identity Aggregate

The Identity Aggregate Root controls:

Universal Identity lifecycle
Identity Identifiers
Current Credentials
Credential and Password History
Authentication-Security Classification Assignments
MFA Enrollments and Factors
Recovery Code Sets and Records
Trusted Devices
Identity State Transitions

The Identity Aggregate shall not contain Customer, Seller, Admin or Super Admin profiles, roles or permissions.

Session Aggregate

The Session Aggregate Root controls:

Canonical Session state
Session Version
Idle and absolute expiration
Revocation state and reason
Refresh Token Families
Refresh Token Records
Atomic Refresh Token consumption and successor creation
Refresh Token Family revocation

The Session Aggregate shall preserve all approved C2 semantics.

Verification Challenge Aggregate

The Verification Challenge Aggregate Root controls:

Verification purpose and channel
Protected challenge evidence
Expiry
Attempt counters
Single-use consumption
Verification Attempts
OTP Evidence Records

Recovery Request Aggregate

The Recovery Request Aggregate Root controls:

One primary Recovery Operation Class
Canonical C3 Recovery state
Recovery policy version
Recovery Assurance
Evidence Records
Approval Records
Attempts
State Transitions
Permitted operation
Expiry
Idempotent completion outcome

Recovery Approval authorization evidence may reference a stable Module 02 decision contract, but shall not copy roles or permissions and shall not create a cross-module foreign key.

Privacy Request Aggregate

The Privacy Request Aggregate Root controls request type, canonical orchestration state, policy version, optimistic-concurrency version, Module 01 deletion-execution child state where applicable, retention-hold reference, idempotent execution and Module 01 completion evidence. It owns Privacy Execution Records. It shall not own or mutate Module 02, Module 03 or Module 06 data.
Aggregate Principles

Each aggregate shall protect its own invariants.
Cross-aggregate changes shall use approved orchestration and reliable integration.
Domain state and its Transactional Outbox Records shall commit in the same PostgreSQL transaction.
Aggregate versions shall support optimistic concurrency where simultaneous state changes are possible.
No Redis write shall be required to make an authoritative PostgreSQL transaction valid.

Canonical State Ownership

Identity state shall use the approved Part 1 lifecycle.
Session state shall use ACTIVE, REVOKED and EXPIRED exactly as approved by C2.
Refresh Token state shall use ACTIVE, USED, REVOKED and EXPIRED exactly as approved by C2.
Recovery state shall use the canonical C3 state machine without modification.
Verification, MFA-factor and Trusted Device state sets shall be explicitly constrained by the logical schema catalogue before implementation.

[WEMP-M01-007B Section 10] 10. Domain Events, Integration Events and Commands
Objective

Module 01 shall distinguish internal Domain Events, externally published Integration Events and directed cross-module Commands. A Domain Event shall not become externally publishable merely because it exists inside the Module 01 domain.

Message Taxonomy

INTERNAL_DOMAIN_EVENT

Represents a fact used within the Module 01 bounded context. It is not externally published unless a separately approved Integration Event contract maps from it.

INTEGRATION_EVENT

Represents an immutable committed Module 01 fact that approved external consumers may observe. It reports a completed fact and shall not instruct another module to perform an operation.

INTEGRATION_COMMAND

Represents a directed request for one authoritative target module to perform an operation it owns. A command shall identify exactly one authoritative target bounded context. Command acceptance shall not be treated as successful completion.

AUDIT_EVENT

Represents immutable security or compliance evidence. Audit Events remain governed by the C4 append-only audit architecture and shall not be used as integration messages.

Internal Domain Event Catalogue

Presence in this catalogue does not authorize external publication.

Approved Cross-Module Command Catalogue

Contract ID: M01-CMD-001
Contract Name: CustomerProfileAssociationRequested
Producer: Module 01
Authoritative Consumer: Module 06 – Customer Management
Purpose: Request creation or association of the Customer profile owned by Module 06 after the approved registration workflow.
Ownership Rule: Module 01 shall not create, update or infer Customer profile state.

Contract ID: M01-CMD-002
Contract Name: SellerProfileAssociationRequested
Producer: Module 01
Authoritative Consumer: Module 03 – Seller Management
Purpose: Request creation or association of the Seller profile owned by Module 03 after the approved registration workflow.
Ownership Rule: Module 01 shall not create, update or infer Seller profile or onboarding state.

Contract ID: M01-CMD-003
Contract Name: RoleAssignmentRequested
Producer: Module 01
Authoritative Consumer: Module 02 – Role & Permission Management
Purpose: Request a role assignment required by an approved registration or provisioning workflow.
Ownership Rule: Module 01 shall not select an unapproved role, persist the assignment or treat command acceptance as authorization.

Contract ID: M02-CMD-001
Contract Name: AuthenticationSecurityClassificationChangeRequested
Producer: Module 02
Authoritative Consumer: Module 01
Purpose: Request the Module 01 authentication-policy change required by an approved privileged role transition.
Ownership Rule: Module 02 owns role assignment and authorization. Module 01 independently validates and applies only authentication-security-classification and Session-security effects it owns.

Approved Integration Event Catalogue

Contract ID: M01-EVT-001
Contract Name: IdentityAuthenticationStateChanged
Producer: Module 01
Source Aggregate: Identity
Purpose: Report a committed Module 01 Identity authentication-state transition to explicitly approved subscribers.
Payload: universal Identity Identifier, previous and current authentication state, aggregate version, effective timestamp and approved reason code only.

Contract ID: M01-EVT-002
Contract Name: AuthenticationSecurityClassificationChanged
Producer: Module 01
Source Aggregate: Identity
Purpose: Report a committed Module 01 authentication-security-classification transition to explicitly approved subscribers.
Payload: universal Identity Identifier, previous and current classification, aggregate version, effective timestamp and approved source reference only. The classification remains non-authorizing.

No approved external payload may contain roles, permissions, profiles, credentials, tokens, OTPs, MFA secrets or Recovery evidence.
No other Module 01 Domain Event is externally publishable until its Integration Event contract, payload, consumers, security classification and retention requirements are approved.

Proposed/Gated C10 Privacy Contracts

These contracts are part of the authoritative C8 catalogue but are not implementation-approved until ENTERPRISE_PRIVACY_ORCHESTRATOR_TBD and the consumer register are resolved.

Contract ID: M01-CMD-004
Contract Name: Module01PrivacyExecutionOutcomeReported
Message Kind: INTEGRATION_COMMAND
Producer: Module 01
Authoritative Consumer: ENTERPRISE_PRIVACY_ORCHESTRATOR_TBD
Purpose: Report only the committed Module 01 privacy-execution outcome.
Minimum payload: privacy_request_id, Module 01 execution outcome, safe reason code, aggregate version, occurred_at and correlation_id.
Completion semantics: transport acceptance is not enterprise completion; the orchestrator owns enterprise status.
Classification: Confidential privacy-operation metadata; no exported personal data, legal-hold detail or authentication secret.

Contract ID: PRV-CMD-001
Contract Name: Module01PrivacyExecutionRequested
Message Kind: INTEGRATION_COMMAND
Producer: ENTERPRISE_PRIVACY_ORCHESTRATOR_TBD
Authoritative Consumer: Module 01
Purpose: Request one approved Module 01-owned privacy operation.
Minimum payload: privacy_request_id, universal Identity Identifier, approved request type, policy version, requested operation, expiry where approved and correlation_id.
Completion semantics: Module 01 independently validates authentication/privacy policy and reports its own outcome only.
Classification: Confidential privacy-operation metadata; no other-module record or authorization state.

Contract ID: M01-EVT-003
Contract Name: IdentityPrivacyStateChanged
Message Kind: INTEGRATION_EVENT
Producer: Module 01
Source Aggregate: Privacy Request or Identity according to the committed transition
Approved Subscribers: CONTRACT-GOVERNANCE-TBD
Purpose: Report a committed Module 01 privacy-state fact.
Minimum payload: universal Identity Identifier, privacy_request_id where applicable, previous/current safe Module 01 privacy state, aggregate version, occurred_at and correlation_id.
Completion semantics: immutable fact only; it grants no authorization and proves no enterprise-wide completion.
Classification: Confidential minimized privacy-state metadata.

All three contracts use the C8 canonical envelope, at-least-once delivery, stable message_id, Consumer Inbox idempotency, schema governance, quarantine and reconciliation. They remain gated and contain no Credentials, tokens, MFA secrets, Recovery evidence, exported personal data or legal-hold details.
Consumer Subscription Rule

The existence of an Integration Event does not authorize every module to consume it. Each consumer subscription requires an approved purpose, minimum payload, access policy, retention rule and owning-module approval. The authoritative consumer/subscriber register remains CONTRACT-GOVERNANCE-TBD before implementation.

Event and Command Principles

Messages shall be immutable after publication; use stable contract identifiers and explicit schema versions; use the canonical C8 envelope; exclude secrets and unnecessary personal information; preserve bounded-context ownership; use the C4 Transactional Outbox; use at-least-once delivery; never claim end-to-end exactly-once delivery; support deterministic idempotent processing; and remain traceable through correlation and causation identifiers.

Role, permission, assignment, authorization-policy and authorization-decision events remain owned exclusively by Module 02.
Recovery approval authorization remains governed by the approved C3/C5 boundary and shall not be silently replaced by asynchronous messaging.
AuthenticatedIdentityContextEstablished remains internal and shall not be used as a durable authorization grant.
