Part 7.3 – Database Design

Document ID: WEMP-M01-007C

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-007C Section 11] 11. Logical Data Model and Persistence Security Principles
Objective

The Module 01 Logical Data Model is the authoritative persistence specification for Identity & Authentication. It shall make C1 ownership, C2 Session and token security, and C3 Recovery Assurance enforceable without introducing implementation code.

Authoritative Store

PostgreSQL is authoritative for all Module 01 persisted domain, audit and integration state.

Redis is non-authoritative and may contain only approved temporary data and rebuildable projections. Redis loss, staleness or unavailability shall not silently change authoritative PostgreSQL state or grant authentication.

General Integrity Rules

All authoritative records shall use immutable UUID Version 7 identifiers unless an explicitly approved exception exists.
All timestamps shall use UTC with timezone awareness and shall be generated or validated by the backend.
All mutable Aggregate Roots shall contain an aggregate_version used for authoritative concurrency control.
Required, unique, check and foreign-key constraints shall enforce the catalogued invariants.
Application validation shall supplement and shall not replace database race-condition protection.
Cross-module identifiers shall be stored as scalar contract identifiers without cross-module foreign keys.

Record Categories

Mutable Aggregate and Entity records may contain created_at, updated_at and aggregate or row version fields.
Append-Only Records shall contain created_at but shall not contain updated_at, updated_by, deleted_at or deleted_by.
Consumed security evidence shall not be physically deleted merely to simulate single use.
Projections shall be rebuildable from authoritative state.
Integration Records shall use explicit delivery or idempotency lifecycles.

[WEMP-M01-007C Section 12] 12. Complete Logical Schema Catalogue
Objective

The catalogue defines the minimum logical fields and controls. Physical PostgreSQL types and Prisma syntax shall be selected during implementation without changing these contracts.

Identity

Purpose: Universal Module 01 Identity Aggregate Root.
Required logical fields: identity_id, identity_state, verification_state, aggregate_version, created_at, updated_at.
Optional logical fields: locked_until, disabled_at, anonymized_at, deletion_requested_at.
Constraints: identity_state and verification_state use approved state sets; aggregate_version is positive and increments on protected changes.
Indexes: primary identity_id; identity_state for approved operational queries; locked_until for lock-expiry processing.
Lifecycle: mutable until anonymization; never stores profiles, roles or permissions.

Identity Identifier

Purpose: Email or mobile identifier owned by one Identity.
Required logical fields: identifier_id, identity_id, identifier_type, normalized_value, verification_state, is_primary, created_at, updated_at.
Optional logical fields: verified_at, retired_at, anonymized_at.
Constraints: normalized_value uniqueness by identifier_type within the active/non-released uniqueness scope; one primary active identifier per type per Identity; verification timestamps match state.
Protection: normalized contact value is confidential personal data and encrypted where approved while retaining a protected deterministic lookup representation when necessary.
Indexes: unique type plus protected normalized lookup; identity_id plus type; pending-verification lookup.

Credential

Purpose: Current authentication credential owned by Identity.
Required logical fields: credential_id, identity_id, credential_type, credential_version, credential_state, protected_secret, protection_key_version where encryption is used, created_at, updated_at.
Optional logical fields: last_used_at, compromised_at, revoked_at, replaced_at.
Constraints: one active current credential per Identity and credential type unless a future approved method explicitly permits more; state and timestamps are consistent.
Protection: passwords use approved one-way password hashing; reversible storage of passwords is prohibited.
Indexes: identity_id plus credential_type and active state.

Credential History Record

Purpose: Immutable credential replacement and compromise history.
Required logical fields: credential_history_id, identity_id, credential_type, credential_version, protected_historical_value where required, event_type, created_at.
Optional logical fields: source_credential_id, correlation_id.
Constraints: append-only; no update or delete through application workflows.

Password History Record

Purpose: Enforce approved password reuse policy.
Required logical fields: password_history_id, identity_id, password_hash, hash_algorithm_reference, created_at.
Constraints: append-only; raw password prohibited; retention depth follows approved password-history policy.
Indexes: identity_id plus created_at descending.

Authentication-Security Classification Assignment

Purpose: Module 01 authentication-policy classification; non-authorizing.
Required logical fields: classification_assignment_id, identity_id, classification, effective_at, assignment_state, aggregate_version, created_at, updated_at.
Optional logical fields: ended_at, source_contract_reference, reason_code.
Constraints: one effective classification assignment at a time unless an explicitly approved precedence model exists; cannot store Module 02 roles or permissions.
Indexes: identity_id plus effective state; classification plus effective state for security operations.

Session

Purpose: Authoritative C2 authenticated Session Aggregate Root.
Required logical fields: session_id, identity_id, session_class, session_state, session_version, authentication_assurance, authentication_security_classification_reference, authentication_methods, created_at, last_activity_at, idle_expires_at, absolute_expires_at, aggregate_version.
Optional logical fields: revoked_at, revocation_reason, device_session_id, mfa_verified_at, correlation_id.
Constraints: session_state is ACTIVE, REVOKED or EXPIRED; session_version and aggregate_version are positive; idle expiry does not exceed absolute expiry; revoked state requires revoked_at and reason; Session contains no roles, permissions, profiles or authorization decisions.
Indexes: identity_id plus session_state; session_id plus session_version; active expiry cleanup; device_session_id where present.

Refresh Token Family

Purpose: Group rotating Refresh Tokens owned by one Session.
Required logical fields: token_family_id, session_id, family_state, aggregate_version, created_at.
Optional logical fields: revoked_at, revocation_reason, reuse_detected_at.
Constraints: each family belongs to exactly one Session; family revocation is monotonic.
Indexes: session_id; active family state.

Refresh Token Record

Purpose: Protected single-use Refresh Token state.
Required logical fields: refresh_token_id, token_family_id, token_digest, token_state, issued_at, expires_at, created_at.
Optional logical fields: consumed_at, revoked_at, successor_token_id, parent_token_id, reuse_detected_at.
Constraints: token_digest is globally unique; state is ACTIVE, USED, REVOKED or EXPIRED; one record has at most one successor; one successor has at most one parent; USED requires consumed_at; raw token is never stored; concurrent consumption creates exactly one successor.
Indexes: unique token_digest; token_family_id plus state; expires_at cleanup; successor_token_id unique where present.

Verification Challenge

Purpose: Authoritative verification/OTP Aggregate Root.
Required logical fields: challenge_id, identity_id where resolved, purpose, channel_type, protected_destination_reference, challenge_digest, challenge_state, attempt_count, maximum_attempts, expires_at, aggregate_version, created_at, updated_at.
Optional logical fields: consumed_at, cancelled_at, correlation_id.
Constraints: challenge digest is protected; raw OTP is never stored; attempt count is non-negative and bounded; successful consumption is atomic and single-use; expiry is after creation.
Indexes: identity_id plus purpose and state; protected destination lookup where approved; expires_at cleanup.

Verification Attempt

Purpose: Append-only attempt evidence owned by Verification Challenge.
Required logical fields: verification_attempt_id, challenge_id, outcome, attempted_at, created_at.
Optional logical fields: source_ip_reference, device_reference, failure_reason.
Constraints: append-only; no raw OTP or secret evidence.
Indexes: challenge_id plus attempted_at.

OTP Evidence Record

Purpose: Protected OTP consumption evidence where separate evidence retention is approved.
Required logical fields: otp_evidence_id, challenge_id, evidence_digest, evidence_state, expires_at, created_at.
Optional logical fields: consumed_at.
Constraints: raw OTP prohibited; single-use state transition; append-only after consumption.

MFA Enrollment

Purpose: MFA configuration owned by Identity.
Required logical fields: mfa_enrollment_id, identity_id, enrollment_state, created_at, updated_at.
Optional logical fields: activated_at, disabled_at, replacement_required_at.
Constraints: state/timestamps consistent; privileged classification requirements enforced by domain policy.
Indexes: identity_id plus enrollment_state.

MFA Factor

Purpose: One enrolled MFA factor.
Required logical fields: mfa_factor_id, mfa_enrollment_id, factor_type, factor_state, encrypted_secret_or_reference, encryption_key_version, created_at, updated_at.
Optional logical fields: verified_at, last_used_at, revoked_at, replacement_reason.
Constraints: secret never logged; active factor requires verified_at; revoked factor cannot authenticate; factor identifiers are unique within enrollment as approved.
Indexes: mfa_enrollment_id plus factor_state; factor type plus active state for approved operations.

Recovery Code Set

Purpose: Versioned set of emergency Recovery factors owned by Identity.
Required logical fields: recovery_code_set_id, identity_id, set_version, set_state, created_at.
Optional logical fields: invalidated_at, invalidation_reason.
Constraints: at most one active set per Identity; new set activation invalidates every prior unused code.
Indexes: identity_id plus set_state; unique identity_id plus set_version.

Recovery Code Record

Purpose: One protected, single-use Recovery code.
Required logical fields: recovery_code_id, recovery_code_set_id, code_digest, code_state, created_at.
Optional logical fields: consumed_at, invalidated_at.
Constraints: code_digest unique; raw code never stored; state transition is atomic and single-use; consumed or invalidated records are not reactivated.
Indexes: unique code_digest; recovery_code_set_id plus code_state.

Trusted Device

Purpose: Identity-bound device trust record.
Required logical fields: trusted_device_id, identity_id, protected_device_fingerprint, device_state, trust_expires_at, aggregate_version, created_at, updated_at.
Optional logical fields: last_seen_at, revoked_at, revocation_reason.
Constraints: device cannot bypass mandatory MFA; revoked or expired device is never trusted; protected fingerprint uniqueness is scoped to Identity.
Indexes: identity_id plus device_state; protected fingerprint plus identity_id; trust expiry cleanup.

Recovery Request

Purpose: Authoritative C3 Recovery Aggregate Root.
Required logical fields: recovery_request_id, identity_id, operation_class, recovery_state, recovery_assurance, recovery_policy_version, permitted_operation, state_version, expires_at, aggregate_version, created_at, updated_at.
Optional logical fields: approved_at, execution_started_at, completed_at, terminal_reason, idempotency_key, correlation_id.
Constraints: exactly one operation class; C3 canonical states only; state/version optimistic concurrency; idempotency key unique within approved scope; terminal transitions are monotonic.
Indexes: identity_id plus state; state plus expiry; unique idempotency scope; operation class plus state for approved security monitoring.

Recovery Evidence Record

Purpose: Request-bound single-use Recovery evidence.
Required logical fields: recovery_evidence_id, recovery_request_id, evidence_type, protected_evidence_or_reference, evidence_state, evidence_boundary, expires_at, created_at.
Optional logical fields: verified_at, consumed_at, failure_reason.
Constraints: raw secrets prohibited; evidence is bound to one request and operation; single-use; independent evidence boundaries cannot be falsely duplicated.
Indexes: recovery_request_id plus evidence_state; expiry cleanup.

Recovery Approval Record

Purpose: Immutable request-bound privileged Recovery approval evidence.
Required logical fields: recovery_approval_id, recovery_request_id, recovered_identity_id, operation_class, approver_identity_id, authorization_evidence_reference, decision, decided_at, expires_at, created_at.
Constraints: append-only after decision; unique recovery_request_id plus approver_identity_id; approver_identity_id differs from recovered_identity_id; approval is request-, Identity-, operation- and expiry-bound; no Module 02 role or permission copy; two distinct approvers required by C3 policy where applicable.
Indexes: recovery_request_id plus decision; approver_identity_id plus decided_at; expiry.

Recovery Attempt

Purpose: Append-only Recovery evidence or execution attempt.
Required logical fields: recovery_attempt_id, recovery_request_id, attempt_type, outcome, attempted_at, created_at.
Optional logical fields: failure_reason, source_ip_reference, device_reference.
Constraints: append-only; no secret evidence.

Recovery State Transition

Purpose: Append-only canonical Recovery transition history.
Required logical fields: recovery_transition_id, recovery_request_id, from_state, to_state, state_version, transitioned_at, created_at.
Optional logical fields: actor_identity_id, reason_code, correlation_id.
Constraints: append-only; unique recovery_request_id plus state_version; transitions comply with C3 state machine.

Recovery Notification Record

Purpose: Recovery security-notification intent and reconciliation.
Required logical fields: recovery_notification_id, recovery_request_id, notification_type, delivery_state, destination_reference, created_at.
Optional logical fields: delivered_at, failed_at, failure_reason.
Constraints: no secrets; destination is protected; delivery failure does not reverse committed Recovery security effects.

Authentication Audit Record

Classification: Append-Only Security Evidence
Purpose: Authoritative Module 01 evidence for authentication, Identity, Credential, Session, Device, MFA, cryptographic and related security operations.
Required logical fields: authentication_audit_id, audit_stream_id, audit_sequence, event_type, event_category, event_schema_version, occurred_at, recorded_at, source_service, source_environment, actor_type, action, outcome, reason_code, correlation_id, data_classification, previous_record_integrity_digest, record_integrity_digest, integrity_key_id, integrity_algorithm_version, created_at.
Conditionally required fields: identity_id, actor_identity_id, actor_service_id, session_id, causation_id, authentication_assurance, protected_source_ip, protected_user_agent, protected_device_reference, Module 02 decision reference and C8 message_id where relevant.
Constraints: append-only; application audit writer has INSERT-only access; readers cannot mutate; no update/delete fields; audit_stream_id plus audit_sequence unique; authentication_audit_id globally unique; integrity digest binds canonical protected record, predecessor, stream, sequence, schema and environment; missing predecessor fails verification; prohibited secrets/personal data absent.
Indexes: identity_id plus occurred_at; actor_identity_id plus occurred_at; session_id plus occurred_at; event type/category plus occurred_at; correlation_id; audit_stream_id plus audit_sequence; recorded_at; integrity_key_id plus algorithm version.
Recovery Audit Record

Classification: Append-Only High-Assurance Security Evidence
Purpose: Authoritative Module 01 evidence for C3 Recovery policy, evidence, approval receipt, dual-control validation, execution, invalidation and completion.
Required logical fields: recovery_audit_id, audit_stream_id, audit_sequence, recovery_request_id, identity_id, event_type, event_schema_version, occurred_at, recorded_at, actor_type, action, outcome, reason_code, correlation_id, recovery_operation_class, recovery_state, recovery_assurance, previous_record_integrity_digest, record_integrity_digest, integrity_key_id, integrity_algorithm_version and created_at.
Conditionally required fields: causation_id, actor_identity_id, approver_identity_reference, Module 02 authorization-decision reference, approval evidence reference, C8 message_id and protected source/device references.
Constraints: append-only and database-enforced insert-only; no update/delete fields; recovery_request_id plus audit_sequence unique; C3 self/duplicate approval prohibitions preserved; references remain request-, Identity-, operation-, policy- and expiry-bound; no Module 02 authorization copy or Recovery secret; integrity failure blocks certification and raises a security incident.
Indexes: recovery_request_id plus occurred_at; identity_id plus occurred_at; actor_identity_id plus occurred_at; event_type plus occurred_at; audit_stream_id plus audit_sequence; correlation_id; recorded_at.
Transactional Outbox Record

Purpose: Reliable external publication intent for approved C8 Integration Events and Integration Commands.
Required logical fields: message_id, contract_id, contract_name, message_kind, schema_version, producer_context, occurred_at, aggregate_type, aggregate_id, aggregate_version, correlation_id, data_classification, protected_payload_or_reference, publication_state, attempt_count and created_at.
Conditionally required logical fields: target_context for Integration Commands, causation_id, trace_context where approved, next_attempt_at, lease_expires_at, published_at, last_error_reference and quarantine_reference.
Canonical mapping: message_id is the unique publication/retry identity and replaces the legacy outbox_event_id concept; schema_version replaces event_schema_version; contract_name plus message_kind replaces ambiguous event_type usage for external publication. No independent legacy identifier may diverge from message_id.
Constraints: message_id unique; message_kind is INTEGRATION_EVENT or INTEGRATION_COMMAND; the complete C8 envelope or deterministic protected reference is retained; aggregate state and Outbox insertion commit atomically; payload excludes secrets; retries retain message_id; published messages are not recreated under new identifiers; state transitions follow the C8 canonical Outbox lifecycle.
Indexes: publication_state plus next_attempt_at; lease_expires_at; aggregate_id plus aggregate_version; unique message_id; contract_id plus schema_version; quarantine state plus updated_at; occurred_at for reconciliation.
Inbound Idempotency Record

Purpose: Durable deduplication of approved inbound contracts.
Required logical fields: inbound_idempotency_id, source_context, idempotency_key, operation_type, request_fingerprint, processing_state, created_at.
Optional logical fields: completed_at, result_reference, expires_at.
Constraints: unique source_context plus operation_type plus idempotency_key; one committed outcome per key/fingerprint.

API Idempotency Record

Purpose: Durable replay protection for approved mutating APIs.
Required logical fields: api_idempotency_id, identity_or_client_scope, operation_type, idempotency_key, request_fingerprint, processing_state, created_at.
Optional logical fields: response_reference, completed_at, expires_at.
Constraints: unique approved scope plus operation type plus key; mismatched fingerprints are rejected.

[WEMP-M01-007C Section 13] 13. Relationship and Constraint Catalogue
Objective

The following relationships are authoritative.

Identity	Identity Identifier	One-to-Many
Identity	Credential	One-to-Many across credential types and lifecycle; maximum one active current Credential per approved type unless explicitly approved otherwise
Identity	Credential History Record	One-to-Many
Identity	Password History Record	One-to-Many
Identity	Authentication-Security Classification Assignment	One-to-Many history; one effective assignment under the approved precedence rule
Identity	Session	One-to-Many
Identity	MFA Enrollment	One-to-Many history
MFA Enrollment	MFA Factor	One-to-Many
Identity	Recovery Code Set	One-to-Many versions
Recovery Code Set	Recovery Code Record	One-to-Many
Identity	Trusted Device	One-to-Many
Identity	Verification Challenge	One-to-Many
Verification Challenge	Verification Attempt	One-to-Many
Verification Challenge	OTP Evidence Record	Zero-to-Many
Identity	Recovery Request	One-to-Many
Recovery Request	Recovery Evidence Record	One-to-Many
Recovery Request	Recovery Approval Record	Zero-to-Many
Recovery Request	Recovery Attempt	One-to-Many
Recovery Request	Recovery State Transition	One-to-Many
Recovery Request	Recovery Notification Record	Zero-to-Many
Identity	Authentication Audit Record	One-to-Many
Recovery Request	Recovery Audit Record	One-to-Many
Session	Refresh Token Family	One-to-Many over Session lifetime
Refresh Token Family	Refresh Token Record	One-to-Many
Refresh Token Record	Successor Refresh Token Record	Zero-or-One

Foreign-Key Rules

All relationships above remain inside Module 01.
Required owned records use restrictive deletion behavior unless an approved anonymization or retention workflow applies.
Security history, audit and evidence records shall not be cascade-deleted by ordinary Identity deletion.
No foreign key shall reference Module 02, Module 03 or Module 06 storage.

Critical Constraint Catalogue

Canonical email/mobile lookup uniqueness is database-enforced within the approved active/non-released scope.
One primary active identifier per type per Identity is enforced.
One active current Credential per approved credential type is enforced.
One active Recovery Code Set per Identity is enforced.
Refresh Token digest is unique.
Refresh Token successor is unique and zero-or-one.
Recovery Request idempotency scope is unique.
Recovery Approval is unique per request and approver.
Recovered Identity and approver Identity cannot be equal.
Recovery State Transition version is unique per request.
Outbox Event Identifier is unique.
Inbound and API idempotency scopes are unique.
All canonical state values use check constraints or equivalent authoritative database enforcement.
Required timestamp/state combinations use check constraints.

Identity Normalization and Reuse

Email shall be normalized using the approved email normalization policy before protected lookup and uniqueness enforcement.
Mobile shall be normalized to the approved international canonical representation before protected lookup and uniqueness enforcement.
Unicode, whitespace and case behavior shall be deterministic and shared by registration and lookup.
Pending and active registrations shall participate in race-safe identifier reservation.
Application duplicate checks shall not replace unique database enforcement.
Soft deletion shall not release an identifier unless the approved Identifier Reuse Policy explicitly permits release.
Anonymization shall destroy or irreversibly transform direct identifiers while retaining non-identifying security records required by approved retention policy.
Legal/commercial reuse timing remains TBD until approved; implementations shall not invent a duration.

[WEMP-M01-007C Section 14] 14. Sensitive-Data Storage Matrix
Objective

Every sensitive value shall use the following minimum protection.

Data Element	Storage Classification	Required Protection
Password	Never persisted	Accepted only transiently over protected transport
Password Hash	Highly confidential authentication secret	Approved one-way password hashing with algorithm parameters
Refresh Token	Never persisted raw	Store only cryptographic digest
OTP Value	Never persisted raw	Store only protected digest bound to challenge and expiry
Recovery Code	Never persisted raw	Store only cryptographic digest
MFA Shared Secret	Highly confidential reversible secret	Envelope encryption or approved external secret reference with key version
Recovery Evidence Secret	Highly confidential	Hash, encrypt or exclude according to evidence type; never log
Email/Mobile Identifier	Confidential personal data	Protected storage and protected deterministic lookup representation where required
Device Fingerprint	Confidential security data	Protected representation; never treated as sole authentication proof
IP Address/User Agent	Confidential security telemetry	Protected according to privacy policy and excluded where unnecessary
JWT Access Token	Never persisted as general state	Only approved identifiers or security metadata may be recorded
Signing Private Key	Not stored in application database	Approved secrets/key-management service only
Outbox Payload	Confidential integration data	Minimized, secret-free and protected at rest
Audit Integrity Metadata	Security metadata	Immutable and protected from application modification

Encryption and Hashing Governance

Encrypted records shall retain key-version metadata.
Key rotation shall not require weakening or exposing protected values.
Hash comparison shall use approved constant-time behavior where applicable.
Logs, traces, metrics, errors, audit payloads and outbox payloads shall not contain raw authentication secrets.

C7 Cryptographic Metadata and Sensitive-Data Additions

Logical metadata records may include Signing Key Metadata, Cryptographic Policy Version and Re-encryption Progress Record.
Signing Key Metadata contains kid, algorithm, public-key reference, environment/issuer, lifecycle state, activation/retirement/compromise timestamps and audit references only.
Private signing keys, plaintext DEKs and plaintext secrets shall never be stored in PostgreSQL.
Encrypted records shall contain ciphertext, nonce/IV, authentication tag, algorithm version, encrypted DEK/reference, KMS key reference and key version.
Re-encryption progress is idempotent, observable and contains no plaintext.

Cryptographic Asset Protection Matrix

JWT private key	ES256 KMS ECC_NIST_P256	AWS KMS	Non-exportable	Emergency compromise and scheduled rotation
MFA secret	Authenticated envelope encryption	PostgreSQL ciphertext/KMS KEK	Plaintext process-memory only	Rewrap/re-encrypt
Password	Argon2id profile	PostgreSQL hash	Non-reversible	Progressive rehash
Refresh/OTP/Recovery value	Purpose-bound digest	PostgreSQL digest	Raw value never persisted	Invalidate/regenerate
Lookup/CSRF/Audit key	Dedicated purpose key	Secrets Manager or KMS	No cross-purpose export	Independent rotation
Authoritative Privacy Request and Deletion Execution Mapping

Privacy Request State is the enterprise-orchestration-facing lifecycle state owned by the approved privacy orchestrator boundary. Module 01 Deletion Execution State is a DELETION-specific child state owned by Module 01. The Privacy Request record stores privacy_request_state and, only for DELETION, module01_deletion_execution_state as separate fields. Neither field is named generic state.

Privacy Request State	Module 01 Deletion Execution State	Safe DeletionStatus Projection
RECEIVED	REQUESTED	REQUESTED
IDENTITY_VERIFICATION_REQUIRED	VERIFICATION_REQUIRED	VERIFICATION_REQUIRED
VALIDATED	VALIDATED	VALIDATED
SCOPE_RESOLUTION	DEPENDENCY_CHECK_PENDING	CHECKING_DEPENDENCIES
DEPENDENCY_PROCESSING	DEPENDENCY_CHECK_PENDING	CHECKING_DEPENDENCIES
RETENTION_HOLD	RETENTION_HOLD	ON_HOLD
READY_FOR_EXECUTION	SCHEDULED	SCHEDULED
EXECUTING	EXECUTING	EXECUTING
PARTIALLY_COMPLETED	COMPLETED when Module 01 execution completed; otherwise current child state	MODULE01_COMPLETED or current mapped status
COMPLETED	COMPLETED	COMPLETED
CANCELLED	CANCELLED	CANCELLED
REJECTED	REJECTED	REJECTED
FAILED_SECURELY	Last committed safe child state plus failure evidence	FAILED_SECURELY

The mapping does not allow state skipping. PARTIALLY_COMPLETED never means enterprise completion. FAILED_SECURELY does not overwrite the last committed child state.

DeletionStatus DTO Schema

Schema name: Module01DeletionStatus
Allowed values: REQUESTED, VERIFICATION_REQUIRED, VALIDATED, CHECKING_DEPENDENCIES, ON_HOLD, SCHEDULED, EXECUTING, MODULE01_COMPLETED, COMPLETED, CANCELLED, REJECTED and FAILED_SECURELY.

M01-ID-002 creates a DELETION Privacy Request and returns privacyRequestId, Module01DeletionStatus, nextAction and version.
M01-ID-003 cancels the Privacy Request only when both orchestration and child states permit cancellation, and returns no body.
M01-ID-005 reads the Privacy Request and DELETION child state and returns privacyRequestId, deletionStatus using Module01DeletionStatus, nextAction and version. It never exposes raw internal states, hold details, other-module outcomes or case details.
C10 Privacy Logical Records

Terms Acceptance Receipt: append-only fields terms_acceptance_id, Identity/registration reference, document ID/version, accepted_at, locale, channel, evidence schema, correlation and created_at. It does not imply consent.

Privacy Notice Receipt: append-only fields receipt ID, Identity/registration reference, notice ID/version, presented_at, conditional acknowledged_at, locale, channel, purpose references, schema, correlation and created_at. It does not imply consent.

Consent Record: aggregate-owned fields consent_id, identity_id, purpose_id, notice ID/version, state, channel, schema, version, created_at and conditional grant/withdraw/supersede/evidence/correlation fields. One current state per Identity/purpose; withdrawal is monotonic for a version; history is not overwritten; consent grants no authorization.

Privacy Request: concurrency-protected fields request_id, identity_id, type, canonical state, policy version, requested_at, version, correlation, created_at and conditional verification/hold/schedule/execution/completion/cancellation/rejection/reason/orchestrator/idempotency fields. Terminal transitions are monotonic; legal-hold details are not public.

Identifier Tombstone: fields tombstone_id, type, protected collision reference, original Identity reference, state, created_at and conditional reuse/reassignment/policy/hold references. It cannot authenticate; history never transfers.

Privacy Execution Record: append-only fields execution_id, request_id, owner module, operation, state, started_at, correlation, created_at and conditional completion/outcome/failure/message/retry fields. No secret or exported personal payload.
C10 Authoritative Relationship and Constraint Catalogue

Terms Acceptance Receipt is Identity-owned append-only evidence and references one Identity or pending registration; it has no consent semantics.
Privacy Notice Receipt is Identity-owned append-only evidence and references one Identity or pending registration; acknowledgement has no consent semantics.
Consent Record belongs to the Identity Aggregate; identity_id plus purpose_id plus current-version policy permits one current state while history remains immutable.
Privacy Request is an Aggregate Root referencing one universal Identity; it uses no cross-module foreign key and stores only protected orchestrator/hold references.
Identifier Tombstone belongs to the Identity Aggregate; protected collision reference plus identifier type is unique; it cannot authenticate and cannot transfer history.
Privacy Execution Record belongs to one Privacy Request and is append-only per execution attempt; owning_module is a stable context identifier, not a foreign key.

C10 Authoritative Retention and Deletion Catalogue

Terms and Notice Receipts: retain under the approved contractual/privacy evidence class; append-only; exact duration Legal/Privacy-TBD.
Consent Records: retain grant, withdrawal, supersession and invalidation history under approved purpose/evidence retention; do not overwrite history.
Privacy Requests and Execution Records: retain protected lifecycle/outcome evidence under approved privacy/security retention; legal hold overrides ordinary processing.
Identifier Tombstones: retain while collision, anti-impersonation, security, legal or cross-module policy requires; reuse remains prohibited until approved eligibility.
Identity deletion shall not cascade-delete these records where approved evidence/hold requirements apply. C9 integrity-preserving transformation governs protected audit content.

C10 Authoritative Transaction Boundaries

Registration Evidence: Identity/pending registration plus required Terms Receipt, Notice Receipt and genuinely required Consent Record commit atomically before registration success.
Consent Transition: validate current version/purpose, append evidence and change current Consent state atomically; withdrawal never deletes unrelated state implicitly.
Privacy Request Transition: lock or compare Privacy Request version, validate canonical orchestration/deletion mapping, append Privacy Execution/Audit/Outbox evidence and commit atomically.
Module 01 Deletion Execution: transition to EXECUTING; revoke/invalidate Module 01 authentication state; apply approved secret destruction/tombstoning; preserve C9 evidence; create C8 intent; record child execution outcome and orchestration projection atomically where they share the boundary.
Identifier Reuse: lock protected tombstone/identifier scope, validate every approved eligibility condition and create the new association without transferring history; fail atomically on conflict.

C10 Approved Access Patterns and Indexes

Resolve Terms/Notice evidence: identity or registration reference plus document/notice ID and version.
Resolve current Consent: unique identity_id plus purpose_id plus current-state/version selector; history by identity_id plus purpose_id plus created_at.
Load Privacy Request: unique privacy_request_id; list by identity_id plus request_state plus requested_at; processing queues by state plus scheduled/updated time; idempotency scope unique.
Resolve Identifier Tombstone: unique identifier_type plus protected_collision_reference; reuse queue by tombstone_state plus reuse_eligible_at.
List Privacy Execution attempts: privacy_request_id plus started_at; reconcile by execution_state plus updated/started time; C8 message_id where present.
C9 Security Audit Evidence, Integrity and Retention Architecture

Canonical Audit Event Envelope

Required fields: audit_event_id, audit_stream_id, audit_sequence, event_type, event_category, event_schema_version, occurred_at, recorded_at, source_service, source_environment, actor_type, actor_reference where known, subject_type/reference where applicable, action, outcome, reason_code, authentication_assurance/session reference where relevant, correlation_id, causation_id, approved trace/source context, data_classification, previous_record_integrity_digest, record_integrity_digest, integrity_key_id and integrity_algorithm_version. This is internal evidence, not a C8 Integration Event or Command.

Canonical Actor Types

ANONYMOUS, IDENTITY, RECOVERY_APPROVER, SERVICE, SYSTEM, SECURITY_OPERATOR, DEPLOYMENT_OPERATOR and DATABASE_MAINTENANCE_PROCESS. Actor type does not grant authorization; Module 02 roles/permissions are not copied; an unknown actor is not invented.

Canonical Audit Categories

IDENTITY_LIFECYCLE, REGISTRATION, VERIFICATION, AUTHENTICATION, CREDENTIAL, MFA, SESSION, REFRESH_TOKEN, TRUSTED_DEVICE, RECOVERY, PRIVILEGED_PROVISIONING, CRYPTOGRAPHIC_OPERATION, API_SECURITY, ASYNCHRONOUS_SECURITY, AUDIT_ADMINISTRATION, AUDIT_INTEGRITY, DATA_RETENTION and LEGAL_HOLD.

Authoritative Audit Event Registry

Every type defines stable name, category, schema, trigger, actor/subject requirements, mandatory/prohibited fields, outcomes, reason codes, severity, retention class, legal-hold eligibility, stream, monitoring and owner. Unregistered events cannot be emitted. C1-C8 event names shall be mapped before implementation.

Audit Persistence Access Model

AUDIT_WRITER inserts only; AUDIT_READER reads minimized authorized fields only; AUDIT_INTEGRITY_VERIFIER verifies without mutation; AUDIT_ARCHIVER reads finalized records and writes immutable archive objects; AUDIT_RETENTION_EXECUTOR performs only policy-bound retention/legal-hold procedures; DATABASE_MIGRATION_ROLE performs approved forward-only structure changes without ordinary audit-data mutation. BREAK_GLASS_DATABASE_ADMINISTRATION requires emergency approval, separation of duties, external immutable evidence and review. Privileged database activity is independently logged and checked against checkpoints/archive.

Cryptographic Integrity

Approved Phase 1 mechanism is KMS-backed HMAC-SHA-256 using the dedicated non-exportable C7 audit-integrity key, separated from JWT, encryption, lookup and CSRF keys. Integrity binds environment, stream, sequence, event ID, schema, canonical protected content and predecessor digest. Deterministic integrity streams avoid a global bottleneck; partitioning remains Security/Architecture-TBD. Missing, duplicated, modified or reordered records are detectable. Rotation links old/new key versions through a sealed checkpoint; compromise triggers incident response and affected-range verification.

Sealed Checkpoints and Immutable Archive

Each stream periodically seals checkpoint_id, stream, first/last sequence, last digest, count, time, integrity key/tag, archive reference and verification state. Interval/max exposure remain Security/Operations-TBD. Approved Phase 1 archive is Amazon S3 with encryption, versioning and S3 Object Lock. Mode, retention, legal-hold ownership, account, region and replication remain Compliance/Security/Operations-TBD. Archive writers cannot overwrite, shorten retention or remove holds. PostgreSQL remains operationally authoritative; Redis is non-authoritative.

Mandatory Transaction and Failure Behavior

Security mutations validate, prepare state, create Audit Record and required archive Outbox intent, then commit atomically. Mandatory Audit insertion failure fails closed. Rejected authentication attempts use a bounded authoritative audit transaction. Archive failure after commit remains pending and reconciled without reversing state. Audit-integrity/KMS failure follows an approved per-category fail-closed or pending-finalization policy matrix, which is Security-TBD before implementation.

Audit Access and Export

No public Audit API is created. Interactive access requires current Session, AAL2, current Module 02 authorization, approved purpose/case reference, field minimization, time-bound access and audit-of-audit. Bulk export additionally requires separation of duties, dual approval, encrypted/watermarked time-bound output, recipient validation and immutable export evidence. Authentication-security classification is not authorization.

Retention, Legal Hold and Privacy

Classes: SECURITY_STANDARD, SECURITY_PRIVILEGED, RECOVERY_HIGH_ASSURANCE, CRYPTOGRAPHIC_SECURITY, AUDIT_ADMINISTRATION and LEGAL_HOLD. Durations remain Compliance/Legal/Security-TBD. Legal hold overrides ordinary retention; placement/change/release is authorized and audited; applications cannot release holds directly. Identity deletion preserves required evidence. Approved anonymization creates immutable transformation evidence binding prior/new digests, policy, authority and executor so integrity remains verifiable.

Time Integrity

occurred_at and recorded_at are distinct UTC values. Approved time synchronization and drift monitoring are mandatory. Late events retain both timestamps. Client time never controls integrity sequence. Drift thresholds remain Operations/Security-TBD.
[WEMP-M01-007C Section 15] 15. Retention, Deletion and Anonymization Matrix
Objective

Logical lifecycle behavior is mandatory even where exact legal duration remains TBD.

Record Category	Lifecycle Behavior
Active Identity and current authentication state	Retain while Identity is active; anonymize or delete only through approved Identity lifecycle
Credential and MFA secrets	Destroy or revoke when replaced, disabled or anonymized; retain only protected history required by approved security policy
Password History	Retain only approved history depth/window; exact value follows approved policy
Sessions and Refresh Tokens	Retain active state until expiry/revocation; purge or archive after approved security-retention period
Verification Challenges and OTP Evidence	Expire deterministically; purge protected evidence after approved short security-retention period
Recovery Codes	Retain protected state until consumed, invalidated or set retirement; never restore consumed codes
Trusted Devices	Revoke on approved security events; purge after approved retention period
Recovery Requests, evidence and approvals	Retain protected security history; destroy secret evidence when no longer required; preserve non-secret audit evidence
Authentication and Recovery Audit Records	Append-only; non-deletable by application workflows; archive under approved security/compliance retention
Outbox Records	Retain until published and reconciled; archive/purge only under approved operational retention
Idempotency Records	Retain for the approved replay window; exact duration remains configurable and explicitly approved

Deletion Rules

Identity deletion shall not cascade-delete immutable audit or legally/security-required evidence.
Anonymization shall preserve referential integrity using non-reusable opaque identifiers.
Secret destruction shall be distinguishable from record deletion.
Exact legal, tax, regulatory and commercial retention durations remain explicit TBD decisions and shall not be invented during implementation.

[WEMP-M01-007C Section 16] 16. Transaction-Boundary Catalogue
Objective

Security invariants shall commit atomically where they share one PostgreSQL consistency boundary.

Registration and Identifier Reservation

Create or resolve Identity, reserve normalized identifier, create pending verification state and create required outbox event in one transaction or fail without duplicate Identity creation.

Credential Replacement

Validate current state, create protected replacement, retire prior current credential, add Credential/Password History, increment relevant versions, create audit and outbox records in one transaction.

Refresh Token Rotation

Lock or concurrency-protect the presented token/family, verify ACTIVE state, mark token USED, create exactly one successor, update family/session state as required and create audit/outbox records in one transaction.

Refresh Token Reuse Response

Record confirmed reuse, revoke family and Session, increment Session Version and create audit/outbox records atomically.

Logout

Revoke the target Session, increment Session Version, revoke applicable Refresh Token Families and create audit/outbox records atomically before success.

Logout All Devices

Authoritatively revoke every applicable Session and family through a retry-safe orchestration. Each committed unit is idempotent; completion is reported only after required authoritative changes are durably committed.

MFA Reset

Revoke affected factors, invalidate Recovery Code Sets and Trusted Devices where required, revoke Sessions/families, increment Session Version and create audit/outbox records within approved consistency boundaries.

Recovery Completion

Transition the Recovery Request through concurrency-protected EXECUTING, apply the one permitted operation, commit mandatory C3 invalidation effects, record immutable audit and outbox records, and transition to COMPLETED idempotently. Cross-boundary delivery uses Transactional Outbox, idempotent consumers and reconciliation.

Audit and Outbox

Every security-relevant authoritative state change shall create its required append-only audit and outbox records in the same transaction as the state change when they share the same PostgreSQL boundary.

[WEMP-M01-007C Section 17] 17. Reliable Asynchronous Message Delivery Architecture
Objective

The reliable-delivery architecture shall preserve committed Module 01 state, publication intent, consumer idempotency, ordering evidence and recoverability without claiming distributed exactly-once execution.

Canonical Message Envelope

Every external Integration Event and Integration Command shall contain:

message_id
contract_id
contract_name
message_kind
schema_version
producer_context
target_context for commands
occurred_at
aggregate_type
aggregate_id
aggregate_version
correlation_id
causation_id
trace_context where approved
data_classification
payload

message_id shall be globally unique. contract_id and schema_version shall identify an immutable approved contract. message_kind shall be INTEGRATION_EVENT or INTEGRATION_COMMAND. Roles, permissions, authorization decisions, credentials, tokens, OTP values, MFA secrets, Recovery evidence and raw personal identifiers are prohibited unless the authoritative owner explicitly approves a minimum required field through contract governance.

Transactional Outbox

Authoritative aggregate state and its Outbox Record shall commit in the same PostgreSQL transaction. Redis shall not be an authoritative Outbox. An Outbox Record shall preserve the canonical message envelope or a protected reference sufficient to reproduce it deterministically. Publication shall be at least once. A publisher retry shall retain the original message_id and shall not create a logically new message. Publication acknowledgment proves transport acceptance only; it does not prove consumer completion.

Canonical Outbox States

PENDING
IN_FLIGHT
PUBLISHED
RETRYABLE_FAILURE
QUARANTINED

State transitions shall be monotonic and auditable. Publisher leasing shall prevent uncontrolled concurrent publication while permitting safe recovery after lease expiry. Lease duration, retry intervals, backoff, maximum attempts and quarantine thresholds remain Operations-TBD and shall not be invented during implementation.

Consumer Inbox and Idempotency

Every authoritative consumer shall maintain a durable Inbox or equivalent deduplication record keyed by consumer identity plus message_id. The consumer's domain effect and Inbox completion record shall commit atomically where they share one transactional boundary. A duplicate completed message shall return the previously committed outcome or no-op safely. A duplicate in-progress message shall not execute the domain effect concurrently. A reused message_id with different contract, version or payload fingerprint shall be rejected and treated as a security/integrity incident. Consumer idempotency shall not depend exclusively on Redis.

Ordering

No global ordering guarantee is claimed. Messages requiring aggregate ordering shall use aggregate_id and aggregate_version. Consumers shall detect duplicate, stale and future/gapped aggregate versions. A stale version shall not reverse a newer committed state. A version gap shall pause the affected aggregate stream, trigger bounded reconciliation and prevent unsafe inference. Different aggregates may be processed concurrently.

Retry, Quarantine and Redrive

Retryable delivery failures shall retain the original message_id and increment observable attempt metadata. Non-retryable contract, schema, security or payload failures shall be quarantined. Quarantine shall preserve protected evidence and shall not silently delete the message. Redrive requires approved operational authorization, an audit record and the original message_id. A corrected business message requires a new message_id, causation reference and approved corrective contract; it shall not mutate an already published message.

Schema Evolution

Contract schemas are immutable after publication. Additive backward-compatible changes require a new schema version under the same contract only when existing consumers remain valid. Breaking changes require a new major contract identifier or explicitly approved major version. Producers shall support the approved compatibility window. Consumers shall reject unsupported versions safely and quarantine rather than guess. Compatibility-window duration and schema-registry/tooling selection remain Architecture/Operations-TBD.

Transport Boundary

The Phase 1 asynchronous transport technology is ASYNC_TRANSPORT_TBD. Selection of Kafka, Amazon EventBridge, SNS/SQS or another technology requires a separate approved ADR or an update to the C8 ADR. No implementation may infer transport-specific ordering, acknowledgment, retention or replay guarantees before that decision.

Security and Privacy

Transport encryption and encryption at rest are mandatory. Producer and consumer identities shall use least-privilege publish/subscribe policies. Payloads shall be minimized and classified. Secrets and authentication evidence are prohibited. Sensitive fields shall use approved protected references or C7 encryption where a formally approved contract requires them. Logs, traces, metrics, quarantine records and operational tooling shall redact protected payload fields.

Consumer Inbox Record

Classification: Integration Record
Required logical fields: consumer_inbox_id, consumer_context, message_id, contract_id, schema_version, payload_fingerprint, processing_state, first_received_at, last_attempt_at, completed_at, committed_outcome_reference, attempt_count and correlation_id.
Canonical states: RECEIVED, PROCESSING, COMPLETED, RETRYABLE_FAILURE and QUARANTINED.
Constraints: consumer_context plus message_id is unique; a completed record is immutable except for approved retention processing; the same message_id with a different fingerprint is rejected; Inbox completion and the consumer-owned domain effect commit atomically where they share one database boundary; Redis is not authoritative.

Relationship and Access-Pattern Additions

Outbox to source aggregate is a logical reference by aggregate_type, aggregate_id and aggregate_version. No cross-module foreign key is permitted.
Required Outbox indexes: publication_state plus next_attempt_at; lease_expires_at; aggregate_id plus aggregate_version; unique message_id; contract_id plus schema_version; quarantine state plus updated_at.
Required Inbox indexes: unique consumer_context plus message_id; processing_state plus last_attempt_at; contract_id plus schema_version; aggregate_id plus aggregate_version where ordering is required.

Transaction Boundaries

External production commits authoritative Module 01 state and the corresponding Outbox Record atomically, never publishes before commit and never reports cross-module completion merely because Outbox intent committed.
Inbound consumption validates envelope, source, contract, schema, classification and fingerprint; reserves or loads the Inbox; applies the consumer-owned effect; and commits the effect and Inbox COMPLETED atomically where possible. Duplicate completion performs no second effect. Version gaps and unsupported contracts fail safely into reconciliation or quarantine.

Reconciliation

Module 01 shall reconcile committed aggregate state without required Outbox intent; unpublished or repeatedly failing Outbox Records; published messages lacking an expected outcome where an outcome contract exists; Inbox records left in progress; aggregate-version gaps; quarantined messages; and cross-module commands without terminal outcomes. Reconciliation shall be idempotent, observable, resumable and unable to grant authentication or authorization through inference.
[WEMP-M01-007C Section 18] 18. Index and Approved Access-Pattern Catalogue
Objective

Indexes shall map to approved access patterns and shall not be added speculatively.

Access Pattern	Required Index
Resolve Identity by canonical email/mobile	Unique protected identifier type plus normalized lookup
List active identifiers for Identity	identity_id plus identifier_type/state
Load current Credential	identity_id plus credential_type/active state
Check Password History	identity_id plus created_at descending
Validate Session by identifier/version	session_id plus session_version/state
List active Sessions for Identity	identity_id plus session_state
Expire Sessions	idle_expires_at and absolute_expires_at for active state
Resolve Refresh Token	unique token_digest
Load/revoke token family	token_family_id plus token_state; session_id on family
Load active verification challenge	identity_id/destination plus purpose/state
Expire challenges	expires_at plus active state
Load active MFA factors	identity_id/enrollment plus factor state
Resolve Recovery code	unique code_digest
Load Trusted Device	identity_id plus protected fingerprint/state
Load active Recovery Requests	identity_id plus recovery_state
Expire Recovery Requests	recovery_state plus expires_at
Validate distinct Recovery approvals	recovery_request_id plus decision and unique approver
Read audit timeline	identity_id/recovery_request_id plus occurred_at
Trace correlation	correlation_id
Publish Outbox backlog	publication_state plus next_attempt_at
Reconcile aggregate events	aggregate_id plus aggregate_version
Deduplicate inbound/API operation	unique approved idempotency scope and key

Index Governance

Index names shall follow approved naming standards.
Index changes require query evidence and write-cost review.
Secret plaintext shall never be indexed.
Unused and redundant indexes shall be reviewed using production evidence before removal.
Partitioning may be introduced for audit/outbox scale only through approved migration and retention design.

[WEMP-M01-007C Section 19] 19. Migration and Schema Evolution Governance
Objective

Schema evolution shall preserve security invariants and supported application versions.

Approved Rules

Prisma remains the approved migration framework.
Migrations are forward-only.
Rollback uses corrective roll-forward migrations unless an explicitly approved recovery procedure applies.
Expand-and-contract shall be used for incompatible structural changes.
New constraints on existing data require verified backfill and precondition checks.
Backfills shall be idempotent, observable and resumable where material.
Migration locks, statement timeouts and deployment ordering shall be defined before execution.
Destructive or data-transforming migrations require security, privacy, backup and restoration review.
Application and schema compatibility shall be preserved throughout the approved deployment window.
Live AWS or production migrations remain outside this documentation correction.

[WEMP-M01-007C Section 20] 20. Logical Data Model Acceptance Criteria

Part 7.3 is complete when:

The persistence inventory is complete.
Aggregate ownership is explicit.
The logical schema catalogue is approved.
Relationship and constraint catalogues are approved.
Sensitive-data protection is approved.
Retention/deletion behavior is approved, with unapproved durations explicitly TBD.
Transaction boundaries are approved.
Transactional Outbox behavior is approved.
Indexes map to approved access patterns.
Migration governance matches Module 00.
PostgreSQL remains authoritative.
Redis remains non-authoritative.
C1, C2 and C3 remain unchanged.
No Prisma schema, migration, SQL or application code has been introduced.
Part 7.3 Acceptance Criteria

Part 7.3 is complete only when every criterion in §20 is satisfied and the proposed persistence ADR remains Proposed pending formal approval.

Module 01 – Identity & Authentication
Part 7.4 – DTO Standards & Validation

Document ID: WEMP-M01-007D

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-007D Section 16] 16. Operation-Specific DTO Catalogue

DTOs are immutable transport contracts and remain independent of C4 persistence records.

Common Request Metadata

Headers are defined by Part 7.1. Request bodies reject unknown fields for security-sensitive operations. Secret fields are write-only and never echoed.

Registration DTOs

M01-REG-001 Request: entryWorkflow, email or mobile according to workflow, password, termsAcceptanceReference, privacyAcceptanceReference, locale where supplied.
M01-REG-001 Response: registrationId, state, verificationRequired, correlationId.
M01-REG-002 Request: channelType; Response: accepted, challengePurpose, correlationId. Destination existence/details remain concealed.
M01-REG-003 Request: challengeId, verificationEvidence; Response: registrationId, state, nextAction, version.
M01-REG-004 Request: no secret fields beyond approved activation evidence; Response: identityId, authenticationState, version.
M01-REG-005 Response: safeState, nextAction, expiresAt where safe, correlationId.

Authentication and Credential DTOs

M01-AUTH-001 Request: normalized identifier, password, clientType, deviceSessionId where approved; Response: authenticationOutcome, accessToken for completed authentication, expiresIn, sessionId, sessionVersion, aal, mfaChallenge when required. Web Refresh Token is cookie-only; Flutter Refresh Token uses the approved mobile-only field.
M01-AUTH-002 Request: verificationEvidence; Response: accessToken, expiresIn, sessionId, sessionVersion, aal, and client-specific Refresh Token delivery.
M01-AUTH-003 Request: Flutter refreshToken only; Web body contains no Refresh Token because the cookie is used. Response: accessToken, expiresIn, sessionId, sessionVersion, and rotated client-specific Refresh Token delivery.
M01-AUTH-004 Request: current session version precondition only; Response: empty.
M01-AUTH-005 Request: current session version precondition; Response: operationId, accepted.
M01-CRED-001 Request: currentPassword where policy requires, newPassword; Response: empty.
M01-CRED-002 Request: normalized identifier and channel preference where approved; Response: accepted and correlationId only.
M01-CRED-003 Request: newPassword; Response: empty.

Verification and MFA DTOs

M01-VER-001 Request: purpose, channelType, destination only where the approved workflow permits a new destination; Response: challengeId, state, expiresAt, version.
M01-VER-002 Request: verificationEvidence; Response: challengeId, verificationState, verifiedAt, version.
M01-MFA-001 Request: factorType; Response: enrollmentId, enrollmentState, protectedSetupMaterial only once where required, expiresAt, version.
M01-MFA-002 Request: verificationEvidence; Response: enrollmentId, enrollmentState, recoveryCodes only at the approved one-time issuance point, version.
M01-MFA-003 Response: enrollmentState, activeFactorTypes, replacementRequired, recoveryCodeCount, version. No MFA secrets.
M01-MFA-004 Request: replacementFactorType; Response: requestId, state, nextAction, version. Recovery-based replacement is executed only by M01-REC-006 when bound to MFA_FACTOR_REPLACEMENT.
M01-MFA-005 Request: AAL2 confirmation metadata where required; Response: recoveryCodes exactly once, setVersion. Recovery-based regeneration is executed only by M01-REC-006 when bound to RECOVERY_CODE_REGENERATION. Raw codes are never returned again.

Session and Device DTOs

M01-SES-001 Response items: sessionId, sessionClass, createdAt, lastActivityAt, idleExpiresAt, absoluteExpiresAt, aal, deviceSummary, currentSession, state, version.
M01-SES-002 Response: the same safe Session fields for one owned Session.
M01-SES-003 Request: no body; Response: empty.
M01-DEV-001 Response items: trustedDeviceId, safeDeviceSummary, state, createdAt, lastSeenAt, trustExpiresAt, currentDevice, version.
M01-DEV-002 Request: no body; Response: empty.

Recovery DTOs

M01-REC-001 Request: operationClass, normalized recovery locator and safe client context; Response: accepted, recoveryRequestLocator, nextAction and correlationId without confirming Identity existence.
M01-REC-002 Request: evidenceType, evidenceValue or protected evidence reference as approved, recoveryPolicyVersion; Response: recoveryRequestId, safeState, recoveryAssurance, nextAction, version.
M01-REC-003 Response: safeState, nextAction, expiresAt where safe, version; never approval internals or evidence details.
M01-REC-004 Request: recoveryPolicyVersion; Response: safeState, approvalRequired, version.
M01-REC-005 Request: decision, recoveryOperationClass, approvalReasonCode, approvalExpiresAt; Response: recoveryRequestId, recordedDecision, version. Module 02 roles/permissions are absent.
M01-REC-006 Request: permittedOperation confirmation and recoveryPolicyVersion; Response: safe completion result or empty according to operation, reauthenticationRequired=true.
M01-REC-007 Request: no body; Response: empty.

Identity and Internal DTOs

M01-ID-001 Response: identityId, identityState, verificationState, authenticationSecurityClassification, mfaState, deletionState, version. No profile, role or permission fields.
M01-ID-002 Request: approved reason code and confirmation; Response: requestId, state, effectivePolicyReference, version.
M01-ID-003 Request: no body; Response: empty.
M01-ID-004 Request: targetIdentityState, reasonCode, sourceContractReference; Response: identityId, identityState, version.
M01-ID-005 Response: privacyRequestId, deletionStatus using Module01DeletionStatus, nextAction and version. Legal-hold, cross-module case and internal policy details are prohibited.
M01-CLS-001 Request: targetAuthenticationSecurityClassification, reasonCode, sourceContractReference; Response: identityId, authenticationSecurityClassification, version.
M01-ADM-001 Request: provisioningReference, identity identifiers and required authentication-security classification only; Response: operationId, state.
M01-ADM-002 Request: controlled bootstrap evidence and universal Identity identifiers; Response: identityId, bootstrapState. No Module 02 role is returned.

Cryptographic Public-Key DTOs

M01-KEY-001 Request: no body. Response: keys array containing only approved public JWK members required for ES256 verification, including kty, crv, use, alg, kid, x and y. Private key parameters, secret material and storage-specific metadata are prohibited.

Field Rules

Required fields are those listed before an explicit conditional phrase. Fields described as where approved, where supplied, client-specific or conditional are optional only under that stated condition.
Identifiers use approved UUID/public identifier formats.
Timestamps use UTC ISO 8601.
Versions are positive opaque client values represented by ETag where mutation concurrency applies.
Unknown fields in authentication, token, MFA and Recovery mutations are rejected.

[WEMP-M01-007D Section 17] 17. Ordered Request Validation

[WEMP-M01-007D Section 1] 1. Enforce TLS, route, method, content type and request-size limits.
[WEMP-M01-007D Section 2] 2. Establish correlation metadata and validate Idempotency-Key/If-Match requirements.
[WEMP-M01-007D Section 3] 3. Parse safely and enforce structural schema and unknown-field policy.
[WEMP-M01-007D Section 4] 4. Apply anonymous abuse controls or authenticate JWT and authoritative C2 Session as applicable.
[WEMP-M01-007D Section 5] 5. Validate ordinary Session class/AAL or purpose-bound C3 Recovery Session.
[WEMP-M01-007D Section 6] 6. Obtain Module 02 authorization decision where required.
[WEMP-M01-007D Section 7] 7. Normalize approved fields and perform semantic/cross-field validation.
[WEMP-M01-007D Section 8] 8. Execute the application use case and C4 transaction/idempotency boundary.
[WEMP-M01-007D Section 9] 9. Commit required audit/outbox records and map the approved response.

Stages that do not apply to a public operation are explicitly skipped. Expensive credential, OTP or Recovery processing shall not occur before applicable transport and abuse controls.

[WEMP-M01-007D Section 18] 18. Response Contract

JSON success responses use: data, meta and correlationId.
meta contains only approved pagination, version or operation metadata.
204 responses contain no body.
Collection pagination uses items, pageSize, nextCursor and hasMore. Total count is returned only when the endpoint explicitly supports an accurate, safe count.
ETag carries the current version for versioned resources.
Every authentication, credential, Session, MFA and Recovery mutation response uses Cache-Control: no-store.

[WEMP-M01-007D Section 19] 19. Stable Module 01 Error Catalogue

Code	HTTP	Meaning
VALIDATION_FAILED	400 or 422	Malformed or semantically invalid request without sensitive disclosure
PRECONDITION_REQUIRED	428	Required If-Match absent
AUTHENTICATION_FAILED	401	Authentication failed using enumeration-safe wording
MFA_REQUIRED	202	Additional MFA challenge required; used only by login continuation contract
AUTHENTICATION_ASSURANCE_INSUFFICIENT	401	Current authentication event does not meet required AAL
SESSION_INVALID	401	Session missing, revoked, expired or version-mismatched
REFRESH_TOKEN_INVALID	401	Refresh credential invalid or expired
TOKEN_REUSE_DETECTED	401	Reuse response committed; no token details disclosed
AUTHORIZATION_DENIED	403	Module 02 denied the operation
RESOURCE_NOT_AVAILABLE	404	Safe absent/inaccessible resource response
RESOURCE_STATE_CONFLICT	412	If-Match/ETag state conflict
CONFLICT	409	Non-version resource conflict
IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED	409	Same key used with different request fingerprint
IDEMPOTENCY_IN_PROGRESS	409	Matching operation is still processing
RATE_LIMITED	429	Approved rate-limit class exceeded
CHALLENGE_INVALID_OR_EXPIRED	400	Challenge invalid, consumed or expired without distinguishing condition
REGISTRATION_NOT_READY	409	Registration cannot activate in current safe state
CURRENT_CREDENTIAL_INVALID	401	Current credential validation failed
PASSWORD_POLICY_FAILED	422	New password violates approved policy
VERIFICATION_NOT_PERMITTED	403	Verification purpose/channel not permitted
MFA_ENROLLMENT_NOT_PERMITTED	409	MFA enrollment unavailable in current state
RECOVERY_EVIDENCE_REJECTED	400	Evidence failed without sensitive detail
RECOVERY_APPROVAL_NOT_REQUIRED	409	Policy does not permit approval request
RECOVERY_APPROVAL_REQUIRED	409	Required approvals incomplete
RECOVERY_APPROVAL_INVALID	403	Approval invalid, expired, duplicate or unauthorized
RECOVERY_OPERATION_NOT_PERMITTED	403	Recovery Session not bound to requested operation
RECOVERY_STATE_CONFLICT	412	Recovery expected state/version mismatch
IDENTITY_OPERATION_NOT_PERMITTED	409	Identity lifecycle operation unavailable
BOOTSTRAP_UNAVAILABLE	404	Bootstrap route unavailable
CONTRACT_INVALID	400	Internal versioned contract invalid
INTERNAL_SECURE_FAILURE	500	Secure internal failure; no implementation details
SERVICE_TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE	503	Required dependency unavailable; operation fails safely

Errors use: error.code, safe localized message key/message, correlationId, timestamp and approved field violations. Stack traces, existence signals and secrets are prohibited.

[WEMP-M01-007D Section 20] 20. API Security and Token Handling

HTTPS is mandatory.
CORS uses an explicit centrally managed allowlist.
Cookie-authenticated Web operations enforce CSRF protection.
Security headers use the Module 00 OWASP-aligned baseline.
JSON request-size limits are centrally configured by rate/exposure class.
Token and authentication responses are non-cacheable.
Recovery Sessions are rejected from ordinary authenticated routes.
Access Tokens, Refresh Tokens, passwords, OTPs, MFA secrets, Recovery codes and evidence are redacted from logs, traces, metrics and error reporting.
Internal and bootstrap routes are not exposed by the public gateway.
C6 Resilience Error Additions

AUTHORITATIVE_STATE_UNAVAILABLE	503	Current PostgreSQL-backed security state cannot be established
AUTHORIZATION_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE	503	Required current Module 02 decision unavailable
VERIFICATION_DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE	503	Verification delivery provider unavailable; existence remains concealed
SECURITY_OPERATION_PENDING_RECONCILIATION	202 or 503 per endpoint	Commit outcome requires idempotent reconciliation
DEPENDENCY_TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE	503	Required non-authoritative dependency unavailable and no safe fallback exists

OpenAPI shall document whether an operation was not started, authoritatively committed with a queued side effect, pending reconciliation or failed closed. Retry-After is returned only when safe and operationally meaningful.
C7 Stable Cryptographic Errors

TOKEN_ALGORITHM_REJECTED	401 externally / security detail internally	Unexpected or prohibited token algorithm
TOKEN_KEY_UNKNOWN	401	No trusted verification key for kid after bounded refresh
TOKEN_KEY_COMPROMISED	401	Token signed by compromised key
SIGNING_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE	503	Required signing/KMS operation unavailable
DECRYPTION_KEY_UNAVAILABLE	503	Required decrypt key unavailable; operation fails closed
CRYPTOGRAPHIC_POLICY_MISMATCH	500 or safe 503	Stored/current policy mismatch requiring secure handling

External errors shall not disclose private key identifiers, KMS resource names, ciphertext or compromise details beyond what Security approves.

Request validation architecture is approved.
Response standards are approved.
Error response model is approved.
API security standards are approved.
Sensitive data protection requirements are approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

Part 7.5 – API Standards & Integration

Document ID: WEMP-M01-007E

Version: 1.0

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-007E Section 21] 21. Authoritative OpenAPI Governance

The reviewed and versioned Module 01 OpenAPI document is the authoritative REST transport contract.

Implementation handlers, validation and generated clients shall conform to it. Framework decorators or implementation annotations shall not silently redefine the approved contract.

Every operation shall document its contract identifier, unique operationId, path, method, exposure/security scheme, headers, parameters, exact request/response schemas, status codes, stable errors, idempotency, concurrency, rate class, deprecation and examples containing no real secrets or personal data.

CI shall fail for:

Invalid OpenAPI syntax
Duplicate operationId
Undocumented implemented route
Documented route without implementation when release-required
Breaking change without approved new version
Schema or security-scheme drift
Generated-client compilation failure
Missing required error/status documentation

OpenAPI artifacts are documentation contracts only during C5 correction; no implementation OpenAPI file is generated here.

[WEMP-M01-007E Section 22] 22. API Naming Conventions

Resources use lowercase plural nouns and stable URL-safe naming.

Approved Module 01 examples:

/api/v1/sessions
/api/v1/verification-challenges
/api/v1/recovery-requests
/api/v1/trusted-devices

/api/v1/roles and /api/v1/permissions are not Module 01 routes and belong exclusively to Module 02 contracts.

GET retrieves, POST creates or invokes a non-idempotent domain command protected by the approved idempotency contract, PUT replaces, PATCH partially updates only where explicitly catalogued, and DELETE performs the catalogued removal/revocation semantics.

[WEMP-M01-007E Section 23] 23. API Deprecation Strategy

Active, Deprecated, Sunset Announced and Retired are the canonical lifecycle states.

Every deprecation defines date, planned retirement, replacement, migration guidance, affected clients and security-fix policy. Exact support duration remains an approved governance value and is not invented here.

[WEMP-M01-007E Section 24] 24. Cross-Module API Contracts

Module 02 is Role & Permission Management.

Module 01 sends only the approved Authenticated Identity Context to Module 02 and consumes a current authorization decision through a versioned contract.
For privileged Recovery, Module 01 consumes approver-eligibility/authorization evidence without copying roles or permissions.
Authentication-security-classification coordination uses stable Identity identifiers and versioned idempotent contracts.
No cross-module database access or foreign key is permitted.

Modules 03 and 06 may initiate or consume Identity contracts but their Seller and Customer profile fields shall not enter Module 01 DTOs.

[WEMP-M01-007E Section 25] 25. Event and Integration Boundary

REST is the Phase 1 synchronous transport. C4 Transactional Outbox records remain authoritative event-delivery intent for approved asynchronous contracts. This C5 correction does not alter event delivery semantics or add a new transport.
C7 OpenAPI JWKS and Security-Scheme Governance

OpenAPI shall document M01-KEY-001 GET /api/v1/.well-known/jwks.json, operationId, public response schema, cache headers and safe failure behavior.
Bearer security schemes shall require ES256 tokens from the exact environment issuer/audience and shall reference the approved JWKS trust contract.
No OpenAPI example may contain a real token, private key, encrypted secret or production identifier.

Part 7.5 is complete when:

OpenAPI standards are approved.

API deprecation strategy is approved.
Cross-module contracts are approved.
Event and integration boundaries are approved.
Documentation governance is approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

ADR-M01-005 – Enterprise API Contract Architecture

Future support for GraphQL, gRPC, Webhooks, Event Streaming, and Service Mesh integration may be added without changing the approved REST API foundation.

Proposed ADR-M01-004	Authentication-to-Authorization Boundary	Proposed
ADR-M01-005	Enterprise API Contract Architecture	Approved

Future ADRs shall remain backward compatible unless formally superseded by a newer approved ADR.

Part 8 Final Approval
Why this structure?

After defining the complete architecture in Parts 1–7, the next responsibility is to define how that architecture will be verified before production.

Part 8 establishes:

Enterprise testing strategy
Quality assurance standards
Security validation
Performance validation
Operational readiness
Release readiness
Testing governance

It ensures that every future implementation follows consistent testing and quality practices before deployment.

This Part intentionally does NOT include
Business feature implementation
API implementation
Database implementation
UI implementation
Infrastructure deployment
Production configuration

Those remain implementation activities and are outside the scope of this specification.

Enterprise Quality Objectives

Part 8 shall ensure:

Functional correctness
Security validation
Performance verification
Reliability validation
Operational readiness
Repeatable automated testing
High deployment confidence

Testing shall validate the approved architecture without modifying it.

[WEMP-M01-008A Section 1] 1. Objective
Purpose

Module 01 – Part 8 establishes the enterprise testing and quality assurance architecture for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

This part defines how the approved architecture shall be verified before production deployment.

Testing shall validate that every implementation conforms to the approved architectural decisions established in Parts 1–7.

Testing shall ensure:

Functional correctness
Security compliance
Performance validation
Reliability
Operational readiness
Auditability
Release confidence

Testing shall verify architecture without modifying it.

Testing Objectives

The testing platform shall ensure:

Repeatable testing
Automated validation
Early defect detection
Enterprise-quality releases
Consistent verification
Risk reduction
Deployment confidence
[WEMP-M01-008A Section 2] 2. Scope
Included

Module 01 – Part 8 includes:

Enterprise testing architecture
Testing principles
Testing strategy
Test environment strategy
Functional testing standards
Security testing standards
Performance testing standards
Operational readiness validation
Testing governance
Excluded

The following remain outside Part 8:

Business feature implementation
UI implementation
Infrastructure deployment
CI/CD implementation
Production monitoring configuration
Infrastructure provisioning

These belong to implementation and deployment phases.

[WEMP-M01-008A Section 3] 3. Testing Principles

The enterprise testing platform shall comply with the following principles.

3.1 Test Early

Testing shall begin during implementation and continue throughout the software lifecycle.

Testing shall not be deferred until development is complete.

3.2 Automation First

Automated testing shall be preferred wherever practical.

Manual testing shall focus on exploratory, usability, and exceptional scenarios.

3.3 Repeatability

Equivalent test executions shall consistently produce equivalent results under identical conditions.

Tests shall remain deterministic.

3.4 Independent Verification

Testing shall verify system behavior independently of implementation details.

Tests shall validate externally observable outcomes.

3.5 Shift-Left Quality

Quality assurance activities shall occur as early as possible within the development lifecycle.

Architecture, APIs, validation, and security shall be verified before production deployment.

3.6 Risk-Based Testing

Testing effort shall prioritize:

Authentication
Authorization
Identity security
Financial workflows
Administrative operations
Critical business functionality

Lower-risk functionality may receive proportionally reduced testing.

3.7 Traceability

Every requirement shall be traceable to one or more approved test cases.

Every critical defect shall be traceable to the affected requirement.

3.8 Continuous Quality

Testing shall support continuous validation throughout development.

Quality shall remain measurable throughout the software lifecycle.

[WEMP-M01-008A Section 4] 4. Enterprise Testing Strategy
Objective

The WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform shall implement a layered enterprise testing strategy.

Architecture Decision Reference

ADR: ADR-M01-006

Decision: Enterprise Testing Architecture

Status: Approved

Enterprise Test Pyramid
                 End-to-End Tests
              -----------------------
                 API Contract Tests
          -------------------------------
             Integration Tests
     ---------------------------------------
                Unit Tests

Additional validation layers include:

Security Testing
Performance Testing
Operational Readiness Testing

These operate across every level of the testing pyramid.

Testing Layers

The platform shall support:

Unit Testing
Integration Testing
API Contract Testing
End-to-End Testing
Security Testing
Performance Testing
Operational Readiness Testing

Each layer validates different aspects of system quality.

Testing Responsibilities

Testing shall validate:

Functional correctness
Security
Data integrity
Business workflows
Performance
Reliability
Compliance
[WEMP-M01-008A Section 5] 5. Test Environment Strategy
Objective

Testing environments shall provide consistent and isolated execution environments for all testing activities.

Environment Types

The platform shall support:

Environment	Purpose
Local Development	Developer testing
Continuous Integration	Automated validation
Integration	Service integration testing
Staging	Production-like validation
Production	Live platform

Each environment shall have a clearly defined purpose.

Environment Principles

Test environments shall:

Be isolated
Be reproducible
Be version controlled
Support automated deployment
Support automated testing
Minimize configuration drift

Production data shall not be used unless explicitly approved and appropriately protected.

Environment Consistency

Testing environments should closely resemble production where practical.

Configuration differences shall be documented and controlled.

[WEMP-M01-008B Section 6] 6. Unit Testing
Objective

Each unit shall be tested independently of external systems, databases, APIs, networks, and infrastructure.
