Execute independently
Produce deterministic results
Complete quickly
Cover normal and exceptional scenarios
Validate business logic
Support automated execution

Module 01 authentication-to-authorization boundary rules; Module 02 owns authorization rules

Independent
Repeatable
Readable
Maintainable
Version controlled

[WEMP-M01-008B Section 9] 9. Authentication Testing
Objective

Authentication Testing validates the complete identity verification process.

Authentication Coverage

Testing shall verify:

Registration
Login
Logout
Password validation

Email verification
Mobile verification
OTP validation
JWT issuance
Refresh token rotation
Session creation
Session expiration

Invalid credentials
Expired credentials
Revoked credentials
Replay attempts
Brute-force protection
Enumeration resistance
Session hijacking protection
C2 JWT and Session Validation Cases

Authentication testing shall include:

Valid JWT with ACTIVE Session
Valid JWT with missing Session
Valid JWT with REVOKED Session
Valid JWT with EXPIRED Session
Valid JWT with stale sessionVersion
Invalid JWT signature with ACTIVE Session
Logout invalidating the next protected request
Logout All Devices invalidating every Session
Selected-Session revocation
Atomic Refresh Token rotation
Two simultaneous Refresh Token requests
Reuse of a consumed Refresh Token
Refresh Token Family revocation after reuse
Idempotent repeated Session revocation
Redis Session Validation Projection miss
Stale Session Validation Projection rejection
PostgreSQL authoritative state overriding cached state
Committed-revocation and in-flight-request boundary
Authentication Quality

[WEMP-M01-008B Section 10] 10. Authentication-to-Authorization Boundary Testing
Objective

Module 01 boundary testing verifies that authenticated identity context is produced securely and handed to Module 02 through approved contracts.

Module 01 Test Ownership

Module 01 testing shall validate:

Authentication enforcement
Authenticated identity context fields and integrity
Exclusion of business roles, business permissions, and authorization decisions from Module 01 tokens and context
Secure handoff to Module 02
Correlation identifier propagation
Failure before handoff when authentication is invalid
Absence of direct database coupling

Module 02 Test Ownership

Module 02 shall own testing for roles, permissions, assignments, resource policies, authorization evaluation, authorization decisions, deny-by-default behavior, temporary permissions, delegated administration, and privilege-escalation controls.

Cross-Module Integration Testing

Cross-module tests shall verify the approved contract between Module 01 and Module 02. These tests shall not transfer authorization-testing ownership to Module 01.
C5 REST Contract Verification

Automated tests shall cover every catalogued operation, exact request/response schema, status and stable error; reject undocumented routes; detect OpenAPI drift and breaking changes; compile generated Web and Flutter clients; verify bearer/cookie transport, cookie flags, CSRF, CORS and no-store behavior; enforce C2 Session validation; enforce C3 Recovery route isolation and dual-control authorization; verify idempotency replay/mismatch/in-progress behavior; verify ETag/If-Match conflicts; test every rate class; verify enumeration resistance and secret redaction; and confirm Module 02, Module 03 and Module 06 ownership boundaries.

Authentication-to-authorization boundary testing architecture is defined for draft review; Module 02 authorization testing remains outside Module 01.

[WEMP-M01-008C Section 11] 11. Verification & OTP Testing
Objective

Verification & OTP Testing validates the integrity, reliability, and security of all identity verification mechanisms.

Testing shall ensure that verification workflows function correctly under normal, exceptional, and malicious conditions.

Verification Test Coverage

Testing shall validate:

Email verification
SMS verification
OTP generation
OTP delivery
OTP validation
OTP expiration
OTP retry limits
OTP resend workflow
Verification completion
Verification failure handling
OTP Security Validation

Testing shall verify:

Single-use OTPs
Expired OTP rejection
Invalid OTP rejection
Replay attack prevention
Brute-force protection
Rate limiting
Enumeration resistance

OTP values shall never appear in logs or error responses.

Verification Quality

Verification shall be tested using both successful and unsuccessful scenarios.

Every verification workflow shall produce deterministic results.

[WEMP-M01-008C Section 12] 12. Session Testing
Objective

Session Testing validates the complete lifecycle of authenticated sessions.

Session Test Coverage

Testing shall validate:

Session creation
Session validation
Session expiration
Session renewal
Refresh-token rotation
Logout
Logout All Devices
Session revocation
Concurrent sessions
Device association
Session Security

Testing shall verify:

Session hijacking protection
Refresh-token replay detection
Invalid session rejection
Expired session rejection
Session timeout behavior
Cross-device isolation
C2 Session Security Validation

Session testing shall verify that:

ACTIVE is the only Session state that permits authentication;
REVOKED and EXPIRED Sessions cannot return to ACTIVE;
Idle is activity metadata rather than a Session state;
Refresh is an event rather than a Session state;
Session Version mismatch rejects the request;
Logout does not wait for Access Token expiration;
Logout All Devices revokes the initiating Session; and
Concurrent Refresh Token use cannot create two valid successors.
Session Quality

Session behavior shall remain consistent across supported clients and environments.

[WEMP-M01-008C Section 13] 13. MFA Testing
Objective

MFA enrollment

Recovery codes
Trusted devices
MFA enforcement
Administrative MFA requirements
MFA Security

Testing shall verify:

Invalid challenge rejection
Expired challenge rejection
Replay prevention
Brute-force protection
Recovery workflow security
MFA Quality

All privileged authentication workflows shall be tested with MFA enabled and disabled where policy permits.

[WEMP-M01-008C Section 14] 14. Security Testing
Objective

Security Testing validates the platform's resistance to unauthorized access, misuse, and common attack vectors.

Security Test Categories

Testing shall include:

Authentication security

API security
Input validation
Session security
Token security
Privilege escalation
Sensitive data exposure
Configuration security
Audit integrity
Vulnerability Testing

Injection attacks
Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)
Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)
Broken Authentication
Broken Access Control
Security Misconfiguration
Sensitive Data Exposure
Rate-limit bypass
Replay attacks

Testing shall align with the current OWASP recommendations.

Security Principles

Security testing shall:

Follow repeatable procedures
Generate audit records where appropriate
Avoid exposing confidential information
Support continuous improvement
[WEMP-M01-008C Section 15] 15. Performance Testing
Objective

Performance Testing validates that the Identity & Authentication platform meets defined performance objectives under expected operating conditions.

Performance Test Coverage

Testing shall evaluate:

Authentication throughput
Authentication-to-authorization handoff throughput; Module 02 owns authorization-evaluation throughput
API response time
Database performance
Session performance
OTP processing
MFA processing
Concurrent user handling
Resource utilization
Performance Metrics

Performance testing shall measure:

Response latency
Throughput
Error rate
CPU utilization
Memory utilization
Database query performance
Cache effectiveness

Performance targets shall be defined separately within operational requirements.

Performance Principles

Performance testing shall:

Use representative workloads
Produce repeatable results
Identify bottlenecks
Validate scalability
Preserve functional correctness

Performance optimization shall never compromise security or data integrity.

C3 Recovery Assurance Testing

Automated tests shall prove:

Every operation selects the deterministic policy row
A replaced or compromised channel cannot solely authorize itself
Recovery Sessions cannot call ordinary authenticated APIs
Recovery Sessions never become ordinary authenticated Sessions automatically
Recovery evidence does not establish AAL2
Fresh qualifying MFA authentication is required for AAL2
Privileged Recovery enforces separation of duties
One approver cannot satisfy dual control
The requester cannot self-approve
Expired, reused or mismatched approvals are rejected
Module 02 approval cannot execute Recovery or create an Authenticated Identity Context
Concurrent completion executes at most once
Partial failure fails securely
Required Sessions and Refresh Token Families are revoked
Session Version is incremented where required
Trusted Devices and Recovery codes are invalidated where required
Outstanding challenges and the Recovery Session expire at completion
Recovery remains fail-closed during PostgreSQL, Redis, event-delivery and notification failures
C7 Cryptographic Verification

Tests shall cover alg=none; unexpected algorithm; missing, unknown, duplicate and compromised kid; wrong issuer, audience and token type; corrupted signature; normal rotation publication/activation/verify-only overlap/retirement; stale JWKS and bounded refresh coalescing; retired/destroyed/compromised key rejection; KMS denial, outage and throttling; signing readiness; envelope encryption/decryption; wrong encryption context; altered nonce/ciphertext/tag; rewrap/re-encryption restart and idempotency; cross-environment rejection; secret/log/trace redaction; Argon2 profile parsing, benchmarking and progressive rehash; CSPRNG/digest purpose separation; and disaster restoration without reactivating compromised keys.

Tests requiring Security-TBD numerical parameters remain blocked until approval, while test harness and policy enforcement remain mandatory.

OTP testing architecture is approved.
Session testing architecture is approved.
MFA testing architecture is approved.
Security testing architecture is approved.
Performance testing architecture is approved.

[WEMP-M01-008D Section 17] 17. Stress Testing
Objective

Stress Testing evaluates platform behavior beyond expected operating capacity.

The objective is to verify graceful degradation and controlled recovery.

Stress Test Coverage

Stress testing shall evaluate:

Extreme authentication traffic
OTP request surges
Session creation spikes
Concurrent administrative activity
Database overload
Cache saturation
External dependency degradation
Stress Testing Principles

Stress testing shall verify:

Controlled failure
Graceful degradation
Automatic recovery
Stable data integrity
Preservation of audit records
Failure Behavior

When capacity limits are exceeded:

Requests may be rejected.
Existing data shall remain consistent.
Security controls shall remain active.
Audit logging shall continue where possible.

Unexpected failures shall never compromise identity integrity.

[WEMP-M01-008D Section 18] 18. Failover Testing

Email providers
SMS providers
Network interruptions
Infrastructure restarts
Failover Principles

Failover testing shall verify:

Service continuity
Controlled recovery
Session consistency
Transaction integrity
Data durability
C2 Authentication Safety Rule

Failover testing shall verify that inability to establish current authoritative Session state never grants authentication.
Recovery Validation

Following recovery:

Services shall resume normal operation.
Identity consistency shall be preserved.
Module 01 authentication data and cross-module contract integrity shall remain intact; Module 02 owns authorization-policy recovery validation.
Audit history shall remain available.

Configuration data

Be documented
Be repeatable
Be periodically tested
Preserve security controls
Protect confidential information

[WEMP-M01-008D Section 20] 20. Monitoring Validation

C4 Persistence and Concurrency Testing

Automated database and integration tests shall prove:

Canonical email/mobile uniqueness under concurrent registration
Credential current/history constraints
Password History enforcement support
Verification and OTP atomic single use
MFA secret protection metadata
Recovery Code digest uniqueness and set invalidation
Canonical Session and Refresh Token state constraints
Session Version concurrency
Exactly one Refresh Token successor under concurrent rotation
Token-reuse revocation transaction
Trusted Device Identity binding and revocation
C3 Recovery state/version concurrency
Two distinct Recovery approvers and self-approval rejection
Idempotent Recovery completion
Append-only audit update/delete rejection
Domain state and Outbox insertion atomicity
Outbox retry and consumer deduplication
Foreign-key and deletion behavior
Retention/anonymization behavior without loss of required audit history
Forward-only migration and expand-contract compatibility
Backup restoration of Identity, Credential, Session, token, MFA, Recovery, audit and Outbox integrity
C6 Resilience, Failover and Restoration Testing

Tests shall cover PostgreSQL loss during Session validation; PostgreSQL failover during Refresh Token rotation; uncertain commit during logout and Recovery; read-only/lagging replica rejection; Redis loss with bounded PostgreSQL fallback; fallback overload protection; stale projection rejection; Redis rebuild without revocation resurrection; Module 02 outage; signing-key/KMS and Secrets Manager outage; email/SMS outage, uncertain delivery and duplicate callback; Outbox publisher outage and poison event; notification failure after committed Recovery; readiness/circuit-breaker transitions; retry storms; backup restoration and integrity validation; restoration order; SLI calculation; approved SLO/error-budget reporting; and measured RTO/RPO against approved targets.

Tests requiring numerical targets shall remain blocked until the relevant TBD values are approved; test scenarios and measurement mechanisms are still mandatory.
C10 Age, Guardian, Residency and Recipient Governance

Age thresholds, protected-person categories, guardian verification/authority/consent, age assurance and jurisdiction behavior remain Business/Legal/Privacy-TBD; registration shall invent none. Applicable regimes, evidence, minimization, authority lifecycle, revocation/expiry and approved notices/consent must be approved first.

Before production, authoritative/backup/DR regions, transfer rules, provider/observability/archive locations, subprocessors, support locations and key-location requirements require Legal, Privacy, Security and Operations approval. Technical availability is not approval.
C10 Privacy and Identity Lifecycle Testing

Tests cover Terms/notice/consent separation; approved-purpose consent; grant/withdraw/supersede/expiry; withdrawal without automatic deletion; canonical deletion transitions and rejected skips; concurrency/cancellation; legal hold; Session/token/Credential/MFA/Device/Recovery invalidation; tombstoning and prohibited/approved reuse without history transfer; cross-module partial completion; Module 01 versus enterprise completion; privacy access/export minimization; retention evaluation; C9 integrity-preserving anonymization; C8 idempotency; backup/restore; and C1-C9 regression preservation.
C9 Audit Integrity and Access Testing

Tests shall cover unauthorized update/delete/truncate; database-role separation; stream sequence collision; record mutation/deletion/reordering/missing-record detection; HMAC canonicalization; key rotation/compromise; checkpoint verification; archive immutability/outage/reconciliation; archive-writer retention restrictions; mandatory-audit fail-closed transactions; legal hold placement/release; anonymization transformation integrity; clock drift/late events; audit access/export AAL2, Module 02 authorization, dual approval and audit-of-audit; restore-time certification; and C1-C8 regression preservation.
C8 Asynchronous Contract and Delivery Testing

Tests shall cover Domain Event/Integration Event/Command separation; every approved contract and schema; envelope validation; duplicate publication and consumption; concurrent duplicates; message_id fingerprint mismatch; Outbox rollback; publisher crashes before and after transport acceptance; consumer crashes before and after commit; stale, duplicate, out-of-order and gapped aggregate versions; retry exhaustion; quarantine; authorized and unauthorized redrive; unsupported and breaking schemas; additive compatibility; payload secret/PII leakage; Outbox/Inbox backup and restore; transport-outage reconciliation; and C1-C7 regression preservation.

[WEMP-M01-008E Section 21] 21. Test Data Management
Objective

Be representative of production scenarios

Be version controlled where appropriate

Be securely managed

Synthetic Test Data
Seed Data

Security Test Data

Protect personally identifiable information (PII)
Protect authentication secrets
Protect financial information
Follow approved retention policies

Sensitive production information shall never appear in development environments without approved anonymization.

Execute consistently

[WEMP-M01-008E Section 23] 23. Defect Management
Objective

Defect Management establishes a consistent process for identifying, tracking, prioritizing, resolving, and verifying software defects.

Defect Lifecycle
Reported
    ↓
Triaged
    ↓
Assigned
    ↓
In Progress
    ↓
Resolved
    ↓
Verified
    ↓
Closed

Rejected or duplicate defects shall follow approved project governance procedures.

Defect Classification

Defects shall be categorized by:

Severity

Objective

Unit tests passed
Integration tests passed
API tests passed
Authentication tests passed
Authentication-to-authorization boundary tests passed; required Module 02 authorization test evidence is available
Security tests passed
Performance tests passed
Critical defects resolved
Documentation updated
Database migrations validated
Rollback plan prepared
Release approval obtained
Release Principles

Every release shall:

Be reproducible
Be traceable
Be fully documented
Be reversible where applicable
Be approved through the defined governance process

Release decisions shall be evidence-based.

[WEMP-M01-009A Section 3] 3. Infrastructure Principles

The enterprise infrastructure shall comply with the following principles.

3.1 Infrastructure Independence

Business functionality shall remain independent of infrastructure technology.

Infrastructure may evolve without changing business rules.

3.2 Automation First

Infrastructure provisioning and deployment shall favor automation over manual processes.

Manual operational activities shall be minimized.

3.3 High Availability

Critical authentication services shall support high availability through approved redundancy strategies.

Single points of failure shall be minimized.

3.4 Secure by Default

Infrastructure shall enforce:

Secure communication
Secure configuration
Secure identity
Secure secrets
Secure networking

Security shall be applied by default.

3.5 Immutable Infrastructure

Infrastructure changes should occur through controlled deployment rather than manual modification of running environments.

Configuration drift shall be minimized.

3.6 Scalability

Infrastructure shall support:

Horizontal scaling
Vertical scaling
Stateless application services
Independent service growth

Scalability shall not require architectural redesign.

3.7 Observability

Infrastructure shall support:

Logging
Metrics
Distributed tracing
Health monitoring
Audit visibility

Operational visibility shall exist across every environment.

3.8 Operational Governance

Infrastructure changes shall be:

Approved
Traceable
Auditable
Version controlled
Reproducible

[WEMP-M01-009A Section 5] 5. Environment Strategy
Objective

Platform environments shall provide isolated stages for development, testing, validation, and production operations.

Approved Environments
Environment	Purpose
Development	Local development and feature implementation
Integration	Service integration validation
Quality Assurance	Functional and regression testing
Staging	Production-like validation
Production	Live platform operations

Future environments may be introduced through approved architecture decisions.

Environment Principles

Every environment shall:

Be isolated
Be reproducible
Be secured
Be monitored
Be version aligned
Be independently deployable

Environment-specific configuration shall remain external to application code.

Promotion Strategy

Software promotion shall follow the approved progression:

Development
      ↓
Integration
      ↓
Quality Assurance
      ↓
Staging
      ↓
Production

Direct deployment to Production shall not bypass approved validation stages.

C6 Phase 1 High-Availability and Dependency Architecture

Phase 1 remains AWS ECS Fargate. Application services shall run without single-instance dependency and shall support multi-task placement across approved failure domains when AWS account, networking and budget values are supplied.

Load-balancer health, readiness, connection draining, graceful startup/shutdown and deployment minimum-healthy behavior shall be centrally configured. Exact timing/count values remain TBD pending operational approval.

PostgreSQL and Redis production services shall use approved managed resilient topologies after the exact AWS services are selected. This document does not deploy resources or invent account/region/network values.

Dependency-health states are HEALTHY, DEGRADED, UNAVAILABLE and RECOVERING. Readiness shall reflect whether the instance can safely serve its assigned operation classes.

[WEMP-M01-009C Section 13] 13. Secrets Management
Objective

Secrets Management protects confidential credentials required by the platform.

Secrets shall never be embedded within application source code.

Protected Secrets

Examples include:

Database credentials
JWT signing keys
Encryption keys
API credentials
Email service credentials
SMS service credentials
OAuth client secrets (Future)
Third-party integration credentials
Secret Management Principles

Secrets shall:

Be encrypted
Be access-controlled
Be rotated periodically
Be centrally managed
Be auditable

Access shall follow the Principle of Least Privilege.

Secret Lifecycle

The lifecycle of a secret shall include:

Creation
    ↓
Secure Storage
    ↓
Controlled Access
    ↓
Rotation
    ↓
Retirement

Retired secrets shall no longer be usable.

C7 Cryptographic Key and Secret Operations

AWS KMS owns non-exportable production JWT signing and envelope-encryption KEKs. AWS Secrets Manager owns provider, database, CSRF and approved HMAC/lookup secrets that are not KMS keys.

IAM shall separate key administrators, key users, deployment roles and security auditors. Application roles receive only required sign/decrypt/secret-read actions and no key-administration permission.

Secret/key lifecycle states, access, rotation, compromise and recovery are auditable through approved AWS and Module 01 telemetry.

Running instances may cache securely obtained secret material only in bounded memory for an approved lifetime. Required secret absence at startup or refresh fails readiness. Secrets are never written to container files, images or logs.

Each environment uses separate keys, aliases, IAM policies, issuers, audiences and credentials. Production material is prohibited in non-production.

Emergency access requires approved break-glass identity, separation of duties, time-bound access, immutable audit and post-use review. Manual extraction of private signing material is prohibited.

AWS account, region, KMS aliases, IAM principals, deletion waiting period, multi-region decision and exact rotation schedules remain Security/Operations-TBD and no resource is created here.
[WEMP-M01-009C Section 14] 14. Container Strategy
Objective

The platform shall support containerized deployment to improve portability, consistency, and operational efficiency.

Containerization shall remain independent of any specific vendor or orchestration platform.

Container Principles

Be immutable
Be versioned
Be reproducible
Be securely built
Support automated deployment
Container Standards

Containers shall:

Package application dependencies
Support health checks
Support graceful startup
Support graceful shutdown
Produce structured logs

Application state shall remain external to containers.

Portability

Container images shall remain portable across approved deployment environments without application modification.

[WEMP-M01-009C Section 15] 15. Database Deployment Strategy
Objective

Database deployment shall preserve data integrity while supporting continuous delivery of approved schema changes.

Database Deployment Principles

Database deployments shall:

Be version controlled
Be repeatable
Be auditable
Preserve referential integrity
Support rollback where feasible
Schema Management

Database schema changes shall:

Be documented
Be reviewed
Be validated before deployment
Be compatible with approved migration procedures

Schema evolution shall preserve supported application versions during approved deployment windows.

Deployment Validation

Every database deployment shall verify:

Schema integrity
Migration success
Constraint validation
Index availability
Data consistency

Deployment shall not proceed if validation fails.

C4 Database Migration, Backup and Operations Requirements

Operations shall monitor PostgreSQL availability, connection saturation, transaction conflicts, lock waits, replication/backup health, Session validation latency, expiry backlog, Outbox backlog and failed publication attempts.

Backup and restoration shall include every authoritative Module 01 table and shall verify Identity, Credential, Session Version, token revocation, MFA, Recovery, audit and Outbox integrity.

Migration execution shall follow forward-only Prisma migrations, expand-and-contract compatibility, verified backfills, bounded locking and corrective roll-forward recovery exactly as defined by Part 7.3 and Module 00.

Redis loss shall trigger projection rebuild or authoritative fallback and shall not restore revoked state or grant authentication.

[WEMP-M01-009D Section 20] 20. Incident Management
Objective

Incident Management establishes a structured process for responding to operational events that impact platform availability, security, or reliability.

    ↓

    ↓
Investigating
    ↓
Mitigated
    ↓
Resolved
    ↓

    ↓

Root Cause Analysis
Impact Assessment

Corrective Actions
Preventive Actions
Architecture Improvement Recommendations

Lessons learned shall be incorporated into future operational improvements.

C3 Recovery Monitoring and Incident Response

Monitoring shall distinguish standard, privileged and Super Admin Recovery activity.

Immediate alerts shall be generated for:

Privileged or Super Admin Recovery initiation
Repeated evidence failures
Self-approval attempts
Duplicate-approver attempts
Expired or replayed approval evidence
Concurrent completion attempts
Recovery invalidation or outbox-delivery failure
Recovery completion with notification-delivery failure
Anomalous Recovery volume or source patterns

Incident procedures shall define ownership, escalation, reconciliation and evidence preservation. Module 01 owns Recovery telemetry; Module 02 owns authorization telemetry for approver eligibility decisions.
C5 API Operations and Contract Observability

The public gateway shall expose only catalogued public operations. Internal and bootstrap operations use separately controlled routes.

Metrics shall use stable operationId and include request volume, latency, status/error code, rate-limit result, idempotency conflict, concurrency conflict, authentication/Recovery failure class, API version and deprecated-operation usage without high-cardinality secrets or identifiers.

Logs and traces shall redact all credentials and tokens. Alerts shall cover abnormal login, token refresh, OTP, Recovery, privileged approval, rate-limit, contract-drift and undocumented-route behavior.
C6 SLI, SLO and Error-Budget Governance

Required SLIs

Availability by stable C5 operationId and criticality tier
Latency by operationId/tier
Session-validation latency and rejection reason
PostgreSQL transaction latency, availability, pool saturation, lock waits and failover
Redis projection hit, miss, fallback, stale rejection and rebuild status
Module 02 decision latency/availability
Provider request, acceptance, delivery, uncertainty and failure
Outbox oldest-unpublished age, backlog, attempts and poison events
Recovery execution latency and secure failure
Readiness, circuit-breaker and degraded-mode state

SLO Register

Availability minimum	99.9%	Approved existing minimum
Measurement window	TBD	Business/Operations approval required
Tier-specific latency objectives	TBD	Product/Operations approval required
Maintenance exclusions	TBD	Business/Operations approval required
Error-budget policy	TBD	Business/Product/Operations approval required
Error-budget owner	TBD	Business/Product/Operations approval required
Burn-rate alert windows/thresholds	TBD	Operations approval required
Reporting cadence	TBD	Operations approval required

No unapproved numeric value shall be inferred. Production approval remains blocked while mandatory values are TBD.

Telemetry shall avoid credentials, tokens, OTPs, MFA secrets, Recovery evidence and uncontrolled high-cardinality identifiers.
C10 Privacy Monitoring and Restoration

Monitor requests by type/state, verification waits, retention holds, cross-module waits, partial completion, failed/repeated execution, invalid cancellation, consent-withdrawal failure, identifier-reuse/tombstone collision, retention evaluation, legal-hold access/change, export access and orchestrator backlog. Thresholds/response obligations remain Legal/Privacy/Operations-TBD. Restore Terms/Notice/Consent history, requests/versions, tombstones, execution evidence, holds, C9 integrity and C8 coordination before privacy readiness.
C9 Audit Integrity Monitoring and Restoration

Monitor insertion failure, sequence gaps/collisions, integrity verification failure, checkpoint age/failure, archive backlog/failure, unauthorized mutation attempts, key denial/rotation/compromise, legal-hold changes, bulk access/export and clock drift. Thresholds remain Security/Compliance/Operations-TBD. Restoration validates PostgreSQL records, C7 integrity keys, stream chains, sealed checkpoints, immutable archive comparison, legal holds and pending archive intent before security readiness is restored. Suspected tampering fails audit certification and triggers incident response.
C8 Asynchronous Delivery Monitoring and Restoration

Monitoring shall cover oldest unpublished Outbox age; backlog by contract/state; publication attempts; publisher lease expiry; duplicate-delivery rate; Consumer Inbox backlog; Inbox records stuck in PROCESSING; aggregate-version gaps; unsupported schemas; quarantined messages; redrive attempts/outcomes; Commands without terminal outcomes; and reconciliation backlog/completion. Thresholds remain Operations-TBD.

Restoration order is PostgreSQL authoritative state; C7 keys/secrets; Outbox and Inbox integrity; approved transport connectivity; consumer compatibility; controlled publication restart; gap detection/reconciliation; quarantine review; and normal readiness. Publication shall not resume before contract compatibility and protected delivery state are verified.
C7 Cryptographic Monitoring and Alerts

Monitor and alert on signing failure; KMS deny/throttle/unavailability; unknown/compromised/retired kid use; algorithm rejection; JWKS refresh/cache failure; key approaching approved retirement; unexpected signing-key state; decrypt/tag/context failure; secret-access anomaly; cross-environment token attempt; re-encryption backlog/failure; Argon2 outdated-profile backlog; and emergency rotation activity.

Metrics, logs, traces, alerts and incident payloads shall contain only safe key identifiers/policy versions and never key material, ciphertext plaintext, tokens or authentication secrets.

Logging architecture is approved.
Monitoring architecture is approved.

Alerting strategy is approved.
Incident management architecture is approved.
Observability principles are approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

[WEMP-M01-009E Section 21] 21. Disaster Recovery and Recovery Objectives
Objective

Disaster Recovery restores Module 01 while preserving C1–C5 security, data integrity and auditability.

Recovery Objective Matrix

Data/Capability	RTO	RPO	Owner	Recovery Requirement
Identity and Identifiers	TBD	TBD	Business, Security and Operations approval required	Restore and validate uniqueness/anonymization integrity
Credentials and Password History	TBD	TBD	Security and Operations approval required	Restore protected hashes/history and key references
Sessions and Refresh Token state	TBD	TBD	Security and Operations approval required	Preserve revocation and monotonic Session Version/token state
MFA factors and Recovery codes	TBD	TBD	Security and Operations approval required	Restore encrypted/digest state without reactivating revoked factors
Recovery Requests and approvals	TBD	TBD	Security, Business and Operations approval required	Preserve C3 state/version, evidence and dual control
Authentication and Recovery audit	TBD	TBD	Security, Compliance and Operations approval required	Restore append-only integrity
Transactional Outbox	TBD	TBD	Engineering and Operations approval required	Restore backlog and reconcile without duplicate effect
Configuration and secrets	TBD	TBD	Security and Operations approval required	Restore approved versions and key access
Redis projections	TBD	Rebuildable; authoritative RPO not applicable	Engineering and Operations approval required	Rebuild only from validated PostgreSQL
OpenAPI and deployment configuration	TBD	TBD	Engineering and Operations approval required	Restore approved versioned artifacts

No numerical RTO/RPO is approved by this correction. Production approval is blocked until every mandatory TBD receives explicit owner approval.

Backup and Restore Requirements

Backups shall cover every C4 authoritative record, configuration reference and required audit/outbox state.
Backup encryption, integrity, access control and restoration testing are mandatory.
Restore shall validate Identity/Credential, Session/revocation, MFA/Recovery, audit and Outbox invariants.
An Outbox backlog shall be reconciled idempotently before normal external delivery is declared healthy.
Redis shall be rebuilt after authoritative PostgreSQL validation and shall not restore stale projection snapshots as trusted state.

Restoration Order

[WEMP-M01-009E Section 1] 1. Networking, configuration, Secrets Manager and signing-key/KMS access.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 2] 2. PostgreSQL authoritative data and database integrity.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 3] 3. Identity, Credential, MFA and Recovery integrity validation.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 4] 4. Session Version, revocation and Refresh Token integrity validation.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 5] 5. Module 01 authentication services in restricted readiness.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 6] 6. Module 02 integration validation.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 7] 7. Redis projection rebuild from PostgreSQL.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 8] 8. Transactional Outbox publication reconciliation.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 9] 9. Email, SMS and notification delivery.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 10] 10. Lower-priority reporting and analytics integrations.

Authentication shall not reopen until mandatory authoritative and security-integrity checks pass.

C7 Cryptographic Restoration Sequence

[WEMP-M01-009E Section 1.2] 1. Restore IAM, KMS control-plane access and approved Security administration.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 2.2] 2. Restore and validate non-secret key metadata and compromise status.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 3.2] 3. Validate ACTIVE_SIGNING and VERIFY_ONLY state without reactivating RETIRED, DESTROYED or COMPROMISED keys.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 4.2] 4. Validate envelope-encryption decrypt capability and encryption context.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 5.2] 5. Restore Secrets Manager credentials under environment isolation.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 6.2] 6. Validate PostgreSQL encrypted-record metadata and representative controlled decryption.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 7.2] 7. Publish and validate environment JWKS.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 8.2] 8. Enable token issuance in restricted readiness.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 9.2] 9. Validate MFA and Recovery cryptographic operations.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 10.2] 10. Resume normal readiness after Security approval.

Compromised keys shall never return to signing or verification after backup restoration.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 22] 22. Business Continuity and Degraded Operation
Objective

Business Continuity applies the C6 criticality tiers and degraded-mode catalogue during disruption.

Tier A authentication-critical capabilities receive first restoration priority, followed by Tier B security mutations, Tier C lifecycle operations and Tier D asynchronous effects.

Security-sensitive operations fail closed when authoritative state or mandatory authorization cannot be established. Non-security external side effects may queue only after authoritative state and Outbox intent commit.

Continuity plans shall define service ownership, escalation, stakeholder communication, approved manual procedures, dependency contacts, reconciliation and criteria for leaving degraded mode.

Manual database mutation, authorization bypass, stale-session acceptance and untracked emergency credential changes are prohibited.

Normal operation resumes only after dependency health, data integrity, projection rebuild, Outbox reconciliation and security monitoring are validated.
[WEMP-M01-009E Section 23] 23. Operational Security
Objective

Operational Security establishes enterprise standards for protecting production environments and operational activities.

Operational Security Principles

Operational operations shall enforce:

Least Privilege
Separation of Duties
Secure Administration
Change Control
Auditability
Continuous Monitoring
Administrative Access

Administrative access shall:

Require approved authentication
Require authorization
Require MFA where applicable
Be logged
Be periodically reviewed

Unauthorized administrative access shall be prevented.

Operational Change Management

Operational changes shall:

Be approved
Be documented
Be tested where applicable
Be traceable
Support rollback procedures

Emergency changes shall follow approved emergency governance procedures.

[WEMP-M01-010A Section 3] 3. Implementation Principles

The engineering organization shall comply with the following implementation principles.

3.1 Architecture First

Approved architecture shall govern implementation.

Developers shall not introduce architectural changes during implementation without formal approval.

3.2 Secure by Default

Every implementation shall preserve:

Authentication security
Authentication-to-authorization boundary security; Module 02 owns authorization security testing
Data protection
Auditability
Privacy
Operational security

Security shall never become optional.

3.3 Maintainability

Software shall remain:

Readable
Modular
Testable
Documented
Extensible

Maintainability shall be considered throughout implementation.

3.4 Consistency

Equivalent business behavior shall be implemented consistently across all modules.

Approved enterprise standards shall be followed without exception.

3.5 Traceability

Every implemented capability shall be traceable to:

Approved architecture
Approved requirements
Approved APIs
Approved test cases

Traceability shall be preserved throughout the software lifecycle.

3.6 Simplicity

Implementation shall favor clear and understandable solutions over unnecessary complexity.

Complexity shall require documented justification.

3.7 Reusability

Reusable platform capabilities shall be shared rather than duplicated.

Duplicate implementations of approved platform services shall be avoided.

3.8 Continuous Improvement

Engineering practices shall support continuous improvement through:

Code reviews
Retrospectives
Metrics
Lessons learned
Architecture reviews

Improvements shall remain compatible with approved architectural decisions.

[WEMP-M01-010C Section 15] 15. Refactoring Policy
Objective

Refactoring improves internal software quality without changing externally observable business behavior.

Refactoring Principles

Refactoring shall:

Preserve functionality
Preserve public contracts
Improve maintainability
Improve readability
Reduce complexity
Maintain test coverage
Refactoring Triggers

Refactoring may occur when:

Complexity increases
Duplication appears
Performance improvements are required
Architecture evolves
Security improvements are needed
Maintainability decreases
Governance

Refactoring activities shall:

Follow approved change management
Preserve architecture compliance
Be validated through automated testing
Be documented where architecture is affected

Large-scale refactoring shall require architectural review before implementation.

Part 10.3 is complete when:

Code review process is approved.
Static analysis standards are approved.
Security review process is approved.
Technical debt management is approved.
Refactoring policy is approved.
Engineering governance principles are approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

[WEMP-M01-010E Section 25] 25. Module Maintenance Policy
Objective

The Module Maintenance Policy governs the ongoing evolution of Module 01 after implementation.

Maintenance Principles

Module maintenance shall:

Preserve architectural integrity
Maintain security
Support backward compatibility
Follow change governance
Preserve documentation quality
Maintenance Activities

Maintenance may include:

Security improvements
Performance optimization
Documentation updates
Dependency upgrades
Operational improvements
Bug fixes
Approved architectural enhancements
Governance

Every maintenance activity shall:

Be documented
Be reviewed
Be tested
Be approved
Be traceable

Maintenance shall not invalidate previously approved architectural decisions without a formal ADR.

C3 Recovery Implementation Gate

Identity Recovery, MFA reset, privileged-account Recovery and Super Admin emergency Recovery shall not enter implementation until the deterministic policy matrix, canonical state machine, API contracts, persistence model, dual-control integration, invalidation behavior, monitoring and test contracts are approved.

Implementation shall preserve Module 01 Recovery execution ownership, Module 02 approver-authorization ownership, C1 boundaries and C2 Session and token invalidation semantics.
C4 Persistence Implementation Gate

Module 01 persistence implementation shall not begin until the authoritative persistence inventory, aggregate boundaries, logical schema, relationships, constraints, sensitive-data matrix, retention behavior, transaction boundaries, Outbox, index catalogue, migration governance and database tests are formally approved.

Implementation shall not invent fields, constraints, retention durations or cross-module relationships. It shall preserve C1 ownership, C2 authentication semantics and C3 Recovery Assurance.
C5 API Implementation Gate

No Module 01 controller, transport DTO, Web/Flutter generated client or OpenAPI implementation artifact shall be implemented until the endpoint catalogue, exposure classes, DTOs, token transport, errors, idempotency, concurrency, rate classes and authoritative OpenAPI governance are approved.

Implementation shall preserve C1 ownership, C2 authentication, C3 Recovery and C4 persistence decisions.
C6 Operational Resilience Production Gate

Production approval is blocked until service tiers, SLI formulas, the 99.9% measurement contract, required latency targets, maintenance treatment, error-budget owner/policy, dependency matrix, degraded-mode catalogue, every mandatory RTO/RPO, backup/restore evidence, failover tests, runbooks, circuit breakers, readiness and alerts are approved.

Engineering documentation and test-mechanism preparation may continue without inventing TBD values. No infrastructure resource is created by this correction.
C7 Cryptographic Readiness Gate

JWT issuance, MFA-secret persistence and production authentication shall not begin until ES256/KMS design, JWKS, issuer/audience values, Access Token lifetime/clock skew/overlap, rotation, compromise response, envelope encryption, environment isolation, Argon2id numerical profile, digest/entropy profiles, key retention/deletion, monitoring and tests are approved.

Documentation and test-harness preparation may continue. No live key, infrastructure, schema, migration or application code is created by C7.
C10 Privacy and Identity Lifecycle Implementation Gate

No production privacy, consent, deletion, anonymization, identifier-reuse, export, age/guardian or cross-module privacy implementation begins until applicable regimes, orchestrator, purposes/legal bases, Terms/notices, consent purposes, request eligibility, waiting/cancellation periods, retention/hold ownership, reuse policy, age/guardian applicability, residency/transfers/subprocessors, C8 contracts, APIs/DTOs and C10 tests are approved. No policy/value is invented. C1-C9 remain unchanged.
C9 Security Audit Implementation Gate

Audit persistence, integrity finalization, archive/export, retention or legal-hold implementation shall not begin until the event registry, database roles, integrity-stream partitioning, canonicalization, KMS key policy, checkpoint interval, S3 Object Lock mode/retention/location, legal-hold ownership, retention durations, KMS failure matrix, access/export policies, clock-drift threshold and C9 tests are approved. No value shall be invented. C1-C8 controls remain unchanged.
C8 Asynchronous Integration Implementation Gate

No external Module 01 Integration Event, Integration Command, Outbox publisher, consumer handler, transport binding or schema artifact shall be implemented until the authoritative message catalogue, producers, consumers, payloads, schema versions, subscription register, transport, acknowledgment/retention/retry/replay behavior, Outbox/Inbox persistence, quarantine/redrive/reconciliation procedures, compatibility window, security/privacy review and complete C8 test suite are approved.

ASYNC_TRANSPORT_TBD, the consumer/subscriber register, compatibility-window values and numerical retry/lease/quarantine settings are unresolved approval gates. No implementation shall invent them. C1-C7 controls shall not be weakened to satisfy this gate.
Part 10.5 Acceptance Criteria

Part 10.5 is complete when:

Module Acceptance Checklist is approved.
Implementation Approval process is approved.
Architecture Certification process is approved.
Future Module Guidance is approved.
Module Maintenance Policy is approved.
Module governance principles are approved.
No implementation-specific source code has been introduced.

Part 10.6 – Final Governance & Module 01 Certification

Document ID: WEMP-M01-010F

Version: 1.12

Status: Draft

Classification: Confidential – Internal Use Only

[WEMP-M01-010F Section 26] 26. Cross-Module Outputs
Objective

Module 01 establishes the enterprise Identity & Authentication foundation for the WALRUS Enterprise Marketplace Platform.

Produced Platform Capabilities

Module 01 produces:

Identity Services
Identity Management
Registration
Identity Lifecycle
Identity Recovery
Deterministic Recovery Policy Evaluation
Purpose-Bound Recovery Sessions
Privileged Recovery Dual-Control Workflow
Authentication Services
Password Authentication
JWT Authentication
Refresh Token Management
Session Management
Device Management
Verification Services
Email Verification
Mobile Verification
OTP Management
MFA Services
Authenticated Identity Context
Authentication-to-Authorization Integration Contract
Authentication Audit Logging and Telemetry
Approved Module 01 API, DTO, validation, database, security, testing, infrastructure, engineering, and operational standards

Module 02 separately owns roles, permissions, assignments, authorization policies, authorization evaluation, authorization decisions, and related audit and telemetry capabilities.
Module 01 owns one universal Identity, authentication-security classifications and session Authentication Assurance.

Module 03 owns Seller profiles, seller onboarding and seller business status.

Module 06 owns Customer profiles and customer business data.

Module 01 JWTs and sessions shall contain authentication information only and shall not contain business roles, permissions, profile state or user-type claims.

C8 Asynchronous Contract Outputs

Module 01 additionally produces the approved M01-CMD and M01-EVT contracts through the C4 Transactional Outbox. Only catalogued contracts are externally publishable. Consumer subscriptions, outcome contracts and the Phase 1 transport remain governed by the C8 approval gates. These outputs do not transfer Module 02, Module 03 or Module 06 ownership.
Consumed By

Module 01 outputs shall be consumed through approved contracts by Module 02 and all future WALRUS platform modules that require identity or authentication capabilities.

No downstream module shall independently implement Module 01 identity, authentication, verification, MFA, session, device, or recovery functionality. This restriction does not transfer Module 02 authorization ownership to Module 01.

[WEMP-M01-010F Section 27] 27. Module Acceptance Criteria

Module 01 shall be considered complete only when all identity and authentication architecture, API, domain, database, testing, infrastructure, engineering, operational, ADR, documentation, and cross-module boundary requirements have been reviewed and approved by the designated authorities.

Module 02 authorization architecture and implementation approval are separate and are not acceptance criteria owned by Module 01.

Implementation shall not begin until the applicable Module 01 acceptance criteria have been satisfied and formal approval has been recorded.

[WEMP-M01-010F Section 28] 28. Final Architecture Review
Module Summary

Module 01 defines the enterprise Identity & Authentication architecture and the contract by which its authenticated identity context is handed to Module 02.

Module 01 covers identity, registration, authentication, verification, MFA, sessions, devices, recovery, authentication APIs, domain and database standards, testing, infrastructure, DevOps, engineering governance, and operational governance.

Module 01 does not own the Module 02 authorization domain.

The document remains a Draft. The architectural quality attributes stated elsewhere remain subject to formal review and approval.

[WEMP-M01-010F Section 29] 29. Architecture Decision References

The former Enterprise Authorization Architecture ADR is removed from Module 01 ownership and preserved as Module 02 source material. Module 01 retains its other existing Module 01 ADR references.

Proposed ADR-M01-004 – Authentication-to-Authorization Boundary – Proposed

The proposed boundary ADR requires formal review and approval before it becomes authoritative.

ADR-M01-001, ADR-M01-002 and ADR-M01-003 include the C1 ownership and assurance clarifications recorded in this Draft.

ADR-M01-001 and ADR-M01-003 also include the C2 JWT, Session validation, Refresh Token rotation and revocation clarifications recorded in this Draft.

Proposed ADR-M01-009 – Identity and Privileged-Account Recovery Assurance Architecture – Proposed

The proposed ADR shall record the deterministic Recovery policy matrix, canonical state machine, Recovery Session boundary, Recovery Assurance model, privileged dual-control model, channel independence, post-Recovery invalidation, failure guarantees and audit requirements.

ADR-M01-002 shall receive the C3 Recovery Assurance clarification.
ADR-M01-003 shall receive the C3 Recovery invalidation clarification.
Proposed ADR-M01-004 shall receive the C3 Recovery approval-boundary clarification and shall remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-010 – Identity and Authentication Logical Data Model and Persistence Security Architecture – Proposed

The proposed ADR shall record the authoritative persistence inventory, aggregate ownership, logical schema and constraint catalogues, sensitive-data matrix, append-only audit model, retention categories, transaction boundaries, Transactional Outbox, approved indexes and migration governance.

ADR-M01-001 shall receive the C4 credential, Session Version, Refresh Token digest and PostgreSQL authority clarification.
ADR-M01-002 shall receive the C4 verification, OTP, MFA secret, Recovery Code and Trusted Device persistence clarification.
ADR-M01-003 shall receive the C4 Session aggregate, Refresh Token family, atomic rotation, Redis projection and revocation-transaction clarification.
Proposed ADR-M01-004 shall prohibit cross-module foreign keys and copied authorization data and shall remain Proposed.
ADR-M01-005 shall preserve API-contract independence from internal persistence structures.
Proposed ADR-M01-009 shall receive the C4 Recovery persistence, dual-control constraint, idempotent completion and Outbox clarification and shall remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-011 – Module 01 REST API Contracts, Token Transport and OpenAPI Governance – Proposed

The proposed ADR shall record the endpoint inventory, exposure classes, Web and Flutter token transport, security schemes, DTO catalogues, standard headers, idempotency, optimistic concurrency, stable errors, rate-limit classes, authoritative OpenAPI governance, compatibility and deprecation.

ADR-M01-001 shall receive the C5 token-transport and state-aware authentication clarification.
ADR-M01-002 shall receive the C5 Verification, OTP, MFA and Recovery-code API clarification.
ADR-M01-003 shall receive the C5 refresh, logout, Session and token-clearing API clarification.
Proposed ADR-M01-004 shall receive the C5 authorization and Recovery-approver API-boundary clarification and remain Proposed.
ADR-M01-005 shall receive the complete C5 authoritative contract decision.
Proposed ADR-M01-009 shall receive the C5 purpose-bound Recovery API clarification and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-010 shall remain Proposed and internal persistence fields shall not become DTO fields.
Proposed ADR-M01-012 – Availability, Dependency Failure, Degraded Operation and Disaster Recovery Architecture – Proposed

The proposed ADR shall record service tiers, SLI/SLO definitions, 99.9% measurement governance, error-budget approval, dependency failure matrix, degraded-mode catalogue, PostgreSQL/Redis/provider/key-management/Module 02 behavior, readiness and circuit breakers, Outbox recovery, RTO/RPO matrix, restoration order and production gates.

ADR-M01-001 shall receive C6 authoritative-state and signing/key failure behavior.
ADR-M01-002 shall receive C6 verification-provider uncertainty and callback behavior.
ADR-M01-003 shall receive C6 PostgreSQL/Redis failover, revocation uncertainty and projection rebuild behavior.
Proposed ADR-M01-004 shall receive C6 Module 02 unavailability behavior and remain Proposed.
ADR-M01-005 and Proposed ADR-M01-011 shall receive C6 degraded API outcomes and retry documentation; ADR-M01-011 remains Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-009 shall receive C6 Recovery fail-closed behavior and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-010 shall receive C6 restore, Outbox reconciliation and Redis rebuild behavior and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-013 – Cryptographic Key, JWT Signing, Encryption and Secret Lifecycle Architecture – Proposed

The proposed ADR shall record ES256 with AWS KMS ECC_NIST_P256, mandatory kid and exact issuer/audience policy, canonical signing-key lifecycle, JWKS, normal rotation, emergency compromise, envelope encryption, key-use separation, environment isolation, Argon2id parameter governance, monitoring and restoration.

ADR-M01-001 shall receive the C7 signing, kid, issuer/audience, JWKS, rotation and compromise decision.
ADR-M01-002 shall receive C7 OTP/Recovery digest and MFA envelope-encryption behavior.
ADR-M01-003 shall receive C7 key-status validation in addition to C2 Session validation.
Proposed ADR-M01-004 shall receive the public-verification/private-key boundary and remain Proposed.
ADR-M01-005 and Proposed ADR-M01-011 shall receive the JWKS/OpenAPI/error contract; ADR-M01-011 remains Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-009 shall receive Recovery evidence cryptographic protection and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-010 shall receive cryptographic metadata/key-reference rules and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-012 shall receive KMS/JWKS failure, readiness and restoration behavior and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-014 – Event, Command and Reliable Asynchronous Delivery Architecture – Proposed

The proposed ADR shall record the message taxonomy, approved contract catalogue, canonical envelope, producer/consumer ownership, at-least-once delivery, prohibition of end-to-end exactly-once claims, Transactional Outbox, Consumer Inbox, idempotency, aggregate ordering, retry/quarantine/redrive, schema compatibility, privacy/security, reconciliation, transport boundary and implementation gates.

Proposed ADR-M01-004 shall receive the C8 non-transfer-of-ownership and Module 02 boundary clarification and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-009 shall receive the C8 rule that asynchronous messages cannot bypass C3 Recovery authorization or dual control and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-010 shall receive the C8 Consumer Inbox and expanded Outbox logical-record clarification and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-011 shall receive the C8 separation between synchronous REST and asynchronous contracts and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-012 shall receive C8 transport failure, backlog, quarantine, reconciliation and restoration behavior and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-013 shall receive C8 transport/payload protection and C7 key-separation requirements and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-015 – Security Audit Evidence, Integrity, Retention and Access Architecture – Proposed

The proposed ADR shall record ownership, canonical envelope, actors/categories/registry, insert-only roles, KMS-backed HMAC-SHA-256 integrity streams, checkpoints, S3 Object Lock archive, failure behavior, access/export, legal hold, privacy transformation, time integrity, monitoring, restoration and gates.

ADR-M01-001, ADR-M01-002 and ADR-M01-003 shall receive their authentication, Verification/MFA and Session/Refresh Token audit requirements. Proposed ADR-M01-004 shall preserve Module 02 authorization-audit ownership and remain Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-009 shall receive high-assurance Recovery integrity and remain Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-010 shall receive Audit Record/access constraints and remain Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-011 shall confirm no public audit API and remain Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-012 shall receive archive failure/restoration readiness and remain Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-013 shall receive the dedicated audit-integrity key and remain Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-014 shall preserve Audit Event separation from Integration Events/Commands and remain Proposed.
Proposed ADR-M01-016 – Privacy, Notice, Consent, Retention and Identity Lifecycle Architecture – Proposed

The proposed ADR shall record the applicable-regime boundary, purpose/legal-basis catalogues, Terms/notice/consent separation, consent lifecycle, Privacy Request taxonomy, deletion state machine, retention/holds, orchestration, tombstones/reuse, anonymization/destruction, age/guardian and residency/transfer gates, monitoring, tests and implementation gate.

ADR-M01-001, ADR-M01-002 and ADR-M01-003 receive deletion authentication, Verification/MFA and Session/token/device invalidation respectively. Proposed ADR-M01-004 preserves Module 02 authorization and remains Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-009 preserves Recovery boundaries and remains Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-010 receives privacy records and remains Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-011 receives M01-ID-005/deletion clarification and remains Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-012 receives orchestrator failure/restoration and remains Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-013 receives cryptographic erasure/protected identifiers and remains Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-014 receives privacy coordination contracts and remains Proposed. Proposed ADR-M01-015 receives legal-hold and integrity-preserving anonymization and remains Proposed.
[WEMP-M01-010F Section 30] 30. Module 01 Draft Status

Module 01 Corrected Draft v1.12 remains Draft.

This consolidated Draft incorporates the approved C1 through C10 architecture corrections and the final consolidation findings M1 through M6 and m1 through m5. Consolidation changes align existing contracts, persistence catalogues, terminology, section identifiers, document control and encoding; they do not introduce new business rules or implementation artifacts.

All implementation-gated TBD decisions and Proposed ADRs remain pending their designated approvals. Formal final document and implementation approval remain pending.
